# Kernel-behavior findings (complementary to KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md)

**This doc is the complement of [`KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md`](KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md)** (the canonical kernel-truth prose — owner-authored, integrated in-repo 2026-07-09 from `a12-kernel-demo`). The layer split stays: KERNEL-SEMANTICS holds the settled canonical behavior; KERNEL-FINDINGS is the **evidence log** behind it — dates, oracles (source-read / probe / differential), source citations, and lock pointers, section-numbered to mirror it — plus the verified facts *outside* the canonical doc's evaluation scope. A new kernel fact lands HERE first (verified + test-locked); folding it into the KERNEL-SEMANTICS prose is a deliberate, separate upstream edit, not part of recording a finding.

Kernel-behavior facts **verified by experiment against kernel `30.8.1`**; we **consult KERNEL-SEMANTICS before each increment** to frame them. Each finding is locked by a test in this repo (named per entry) and carries its **fold status** against the canonical prose (the contract below): a *folded* finding keeps its claim + provenance + locks as the evidence skeleton, with the narrative detail living upstream. rulekit's *treatment* of each fact lives in [`KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md`](./KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md).

> **What lands HERE vs INTERPRETER-FINDINGS (the routing rule + decision test).** This doc holds **kernel truths** — a behavior, capability, error code, limit, or extensibility surface that is true *regardless of our code*. How the **interpreter** reproduces/diverges-from/locks such a truth is an `IF<n>` in [`INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md`](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md) (which cites the kernel fact here); how **rulekit/modelkit** treats it is in [`KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md`](KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md). **Decision test:** *"Would this fact be true, and worth recording, even if our interpreter and dmkit did not exist?"* → **yes ⇒ here.** The full three-home table + the worked mis-file lesson live in the INTERPRETER-FINDINGS header.

> **Fold status (the two-doc contract, 2026-07-11).** Every finding heading below carries a fold-status line as its first body line: `> Fold: folded → §n.` (the finding's canonical claim now lives in that [KERNEL-SEMANTICS](KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md) section — the entry here keeps the claim, the provenance, and the lock pointers; several targets join with ` + `), `> Fold: not-foldable — <reason>.` (nothing to fold — an authoring/structure/serialization/toolchain fact outside KERNEL-SEMANTICS's evaluation scope, or pure provenance), or `> Fold: pending — <reason>.` (genuinely unsettled content). A **folded** finding MAY be compressed to its evidence skeleton — claim + provenance (date, oracle, kernel version) + locks. **Retro-compressing the existing folded entries is DECLINED (2026-07-29), and new entries are authored at skeleton weight instead.** The reason is that these docs are read by grep/sed, never whole: the heading already carries claim + oracle + date and `grep -A1` adds the fold marker, so the only cost compression saves is a full-entry `sed` — which a reader performs precisely when they want the detail compression would delete. What a folded entry uniquely holds is the **worked measurement** (probe inputs, matrices, the readings ruled out); the canonical section holds the claim and the lock test holds the executable assertion, but neither narrates what else was measured. *Re-open trigger:* per-entry `sed` cost becomes a measured bottleneck. A finding is **never deleted or renumbered**: `-GAPS` deletions, code comments, and the SEMANTICS-MAP resolve through these headings, and the kernel-upgrade workflow re-verifies findings on a pin bump. A `folded` marker is a **live claim**: appending a new paragraph to an already-folded finding either folds the new claim upstream in the same commit or flips the marker back to `pending — <what still needs folding>`. The contract — markers, targets, the §-mirror, and the canonical **anchor set** (the 14 section slugs + the field-types table, every intra-doc link resolving) — is enforced by `cli … KernelDocsFoldContractTest` (the `semanticsMapGuardTest` lane).

## The canonical Document JSON and `DocumentPointer` string — A12 already defines both (source-verified 2026-07-28) <a id="kf1"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — serialization/addressing format, outside KERNEL-SEMANTICS's runtime-evaluation scope.

**Document JSON** is a first-class kernel format with a published serializer, `IDocumentV2Serializer.{serializeV2, deserializeV2}` (`DocumentSerializationConfig` / `DocumentDeserializationConfig`). Its shape is a **nested object tree mirroring the model**: a non-repeating group is a nested object, a **repeating group is a JSON array** whose index *is* the repetition, an unfilled row in that array is `{}`, a present-but-empty group is `{}`, an omitted entity is a **missing key**, and a field is its value — **native JSON for number and boolean** (`"Grams": 250`, `"PremiumPartner": true`), a **model-format string** for the date family (`DateType` `"2022-07-05"`, `DateTimeType` `"2012-12-12T12:12:12"`, `TimeType` `"12:12:12"`, `DateFragmentType` `"2000-05"`), and the **S-value** for `EnumerationType`. A root-level `id` is a serializer special case, not a model field.

Two deserialization facts matter for anything that reimplements this. First, the reader **stringifies every JSON value** before parsing — `String strValue = value.isNull() ? null : value.asText();` — and then parses model-driven, because the model is what disambiguates string from date/time. Second, when conversion **fails**, the broken value is **not dropped**: the reader reports a parse notification and stores the raw string verbatim (`DocumentJsonAndXmlDeserializationInfoImpl.handleValueConversionResultFallback`, carrying the kernel-team `[A12K-3638]` comment that keeping it "has some merit … we must deserialize it first so the user can fix it"), and serialization writes strings back untouched. So malformed input **is** representable in the official shape; a separate raw-text transport buys nothing.

**`DocumentPointer`** is the canonical address, with a string form (`DocumentPointerImpl`): parts joined by `/`, each part `([\w_-]+)(\[(\d+)?\])?`. An absent bracket means **repetition 1**, and `toString` **omits `[1]`** — so `Recipe/Ingredients[3]/Name` is canonical and a field part simply carries no bracket. A **leading `/` is accepted and stripped as redundant** (a pointer is always relative to the document or group it is applied to); a **trailing `/` is rejected**. `[0]` is `DocumentMultiPointer.WILDCARD` = all repetitions. *Corrected 2026-07-31 — this sentence used to add "legal **only on the last part**; every earlier part must be `>= 1`", which conflates two different rules and is wrong for the multipointer.* Source (`DocumentMultiPointer` javadoc): the wildcard is legal at **each** path part — the class's own example is `DocumentMultiPointer.of("a[0]/b[2]")`, wildcard on the FIRST part — and it expands to one exact pointer per non-wildcard repetition at every wildcarded level. The last-part-only restriction belongs to the **exact** `DocumentPointer`, where a trailing `0` addresses `RepetitionsV2`. **Omission means repetition 1 on BOTH types**, never "all". *Corrected again 2026-07-31 (review): this sentence went on to call `A12PointerFormat.parse`'s refusal of an earlier-part `[0]` "an over-rejection with no kernel basis". That is wrong, and the basis is in the very class this entry cites — `DocumentPointerImpl`'s constructor throws `IllegalArgumentException("All repetition indexes must be >= 1, the last repetition can also be 0")` for exactly that input. `A12PointerFormat` models the **exact** pointer, so its refusal is kernel-conformant and `A12PointerFormatTest` already locks it; declaring a committed, correct refusal a defect was the mirror of the client-side-refusal rule — a claim of unsoundness needs measuring too.* **What is genuinely wrong is a VOCABULARY conflation:** `A12PointerFormat` calls `[0]` "the wildcard", which is the *multipointer's* meaning, while enforcing the *exact* pointer's last-part-only position rule — two types' rules under one name. Consequence for us: name the type each parser models (and give a multipointer parser, if one is ever needed, the wildcard-at-every-part rule); and `cli … RelevantSelection` maps an omitted index to ALL, which **is** a real behavioural defect — it is the opposite of A12's meaning for that spelling — while inventing `[*]` where A12 writes `[0]`.

**Number I/O is a THREE-part contract, and each part is separately load-bearing** (probe-measured against Jackson 2.18.2 replicating the kernel's exact configuration, 2026-07-28):

| stage | kernel mechanism | consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **parse** | `USE_BIG_DECIMAL_FOR_FLOATS` + `STRIP_TRAILING_BIGDECIMAL_ZEROES=false` | `250.00` is a `DecimalNode`, not a `DoubleNode` |
| **read** | `ValueNode.asText()` | the text handed to the converter is `BigDecimal.toString()` — `250.00` stays, `1e5` becomes `1E+5`. *(Corrected 2026-07-28: this is an intermediate, not what the document stores — the converter turns it into a `BigDecimal` and every later text is re-rendered from that value. See the value-normalization paragraph below.)* |
| **write** | `numberNode(BigDecimal)` + `JsonGenerator.Feature.WRITE_BIGDECIMAL_AS_PLAIN` | `toPlainString()` — **never scientific**, scale preserved: `250.00` → `250.00`, `1e5` → `100000`, `1.2e-3` → `0.0012`, `-0.0` → `0.0` (BigDecimal has no negative zero, and short-circuits zero at a negative scale, so `0e5` → `0`) |

So **A12 does not write numbers as strings** — `250.00` is emitted unquoted and its trailing zeros survive natively; a string is used only for a value the converter could not parse. Any reimplementation must reproduce all three stages: the parse configuration alone is not enough (a default mapper's `asText()` already returns `250.0`), and the write stage normalizes independently of how the value was authored.

**The ingress is VALUE-NORMALIZING for every typed kind, and the runtime's text is a FOURTH rendering — not `asText()` (kernel-probed 2026-07-28).** The document does not keep the authored text at all: `FieldValueConverter` turns the stringified node into `String | Boolean | BigDecimal | Instant | InstantRange` and *every* later text is re-rendered from that stored value. There are consequently two renderings of one stored number, and they differ:

| consumer | mechanism | `1e5` | `250.00` | `0.0000001` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **serializer** (`toJsonFieldNode`) | `numberNode(bd)` + `WRITE_BIGDECIMAL_AS_PLAIN` = `bd.toPlainString()` | `100000` | `250.00` | `0.0000001` |
| **runtime `IData`** (`ValidationData.getValue` → `MetaField.convertFromBasicType` → `FormatDefinitionZahl`) | `bd.setScale(max(bd.stripTrailingZeros().scale(), minFractionalDigits)).toPlainString()` | `100000` | **`250`** | `0.0000001` |

They differ in **both** directions, not one: a fractional trailing zero beyond `minFractionalDigits` is **stripped** (`250.00` → `250`) *and* a value with fewer fractional digits than `minFractionalDigits` is **padded** (`250` → `250.00`). **Corrected 2026-07-29:** this paragraph used to say they "agree except on fractional trailing zeros", which named only the strip half even though the formula it quotes pads — the omission is what left the padding direction undrawn as a consequence. Both halves are now pinned by discriminating cases rather than by reading:

- the **`max(…, minFractionalDigits)` term was NOT discriminated by the matrix as it then stood** (49 rows; independent review, 2026-07-28). The strictly simpler `bd.stripTrailingZeros().toPlainString()` predicts every one of those rows identically, because no field in that model declares `minFractionalDigits`; the "only possible if" wording overstated what the rows could show.
- the review measured the term rather than parking it: on a `minFractionalDigits: 2` field the V1 positive control (stored text `250`) fires `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` while the same value through JSON ingress (stored `BigDecimal` `250`) fires nothing.
- and the **sharper, code-independent discrimination is a LENGTH bound** (2026-07-29): `min/maxLength` count characters of the *rendered* text, so `250` (3) and `250.00` (6) are told apart by a code that has nothing to do with decimal separators. On a `minFrac 2, maxFrac 2, maxLength 5` field a stored `250` fires **`zahlZuLang`** — impossible unless the runtime text is the 6-character `250.00` — while a stored `1` (→ `1.00`, 4) fires nothing. The mirror pins the strip half: on a `maxFrac 2, minLength 4` field a stored `250.00` fires **`zahlZuKurz`**, so the runtime text is the 3-character `250`. *(`NumberRenderChannelKernelTest`.)* **A further incompleteness:** `FormatDefinitionZahl.convertFromBasicType` ends with `resultWithDot.replace('.', decimalSeparator)`, where the separator is a **codegen parameter** (`ICodeGeneratorParameters.getDecimalSeparator()`), not the constant `.` the bare `toPlainString()` rendering implies. Our fixtures all set `"decimalSeparator": "."` and the interpreter models none, so nothing here is wrong today — but a reimplementer on a comma-configured project inherits an obligation this formula does not state: on a `maxFractionalDigits: 0` field, `250.00` draws **no** `zahlHatDezimalTrenner` (so the runtime text has no point) while `0.0000001` **does** (so it is plain, not `1E-7`), and `1e5` draws nothing at all (so it is `100000`, not the `asText()` form `1E+5`). Per kind, measured the same way:

- **NUMBER** — the converter gate is `new BigDecimal(String)`, **broader than JSON's grammar**: `"+5"` → `5`, `"007"` → `7`, `1.5e1` → `15`, `-0.0` → `0.0`. The one exception is `NumberUtil.createBigDecimalIfPossible`'s branch for a **`leadingZerosAllowed`** field, which returns the *original String* — so `"007"` there stays text and is written back quoted.
- **BOOLEAN / CONFIRM** — `"true".equalsIgnoreCase(value)` (and `"false"` for boolean), then `booleanNode`: `"TRUE"`, `"True"`, `"FALSE"` all convert. A **confirm has no false**: `parseConfirm` accepts only `true`, so even a native JSON `false` is a conversion failure stored as the String `"false"` and re-emitted quoted (`feldJaFalsch`).
- **the DATE family** — nothing normalizes, because nothing non-canonical parses: `ValidationDateParser` is non-lenient, so `2024-3-5`, `2024-03-5`, `20240305`, `12024-03-05`, `14:5:0`, `14:05`, `1.1.2024-31.12.2024` all *fail* conversion and are kept verbatim, and a spelling that parses already is the model format. The **one** exception is a **pre-Gregorian** date, where parsing uses `SimpleDateFormat`'s Julian/Gregorian hybrid and `serializeInstant` formats with proleptic-Gregorian `java.time`: `1583-10-15` is rewritten `1583-10-14` (both spellings are `datumFalsch` regardless).
- **STRING / Enumeration** — never normalized; the converter succeeds with the text itself, and a native JSON `5`/`true` simply becomes `"5"`/`"true"`.
- **`""` on any TYPED kind** is `ConversionResult.success(null)` — present with no value, written back as `null`. A STRING keeps its `""`.

**This regime is reached only through the serializer.** A document built the V1 way (`IDocumentFactory.createFieldInstance(path, reps, String)`) holds Strings, and `ValidationData.getValue` returns a String **unchanged** — so no conversion happens and the authored text is what validation sees. Both regimes are real kernel behaviour; a reimplementation must not assume the one its differential happens to exercise. *(Provenance: probe against kernel 30.8.1 through `MDSerializerFactory.createDocumentSerializerV2` + `IDocumentRtService.validateFull`, 2026-07-28, corroborated by a source read of `FieldValueConverter`, `NumberUtil`, `ValidationData`, `MetaField`, and `FormatDefinitionZahl`/`Ja`/`JaNein`. Locked: `adapter.laws.A12JsonIngressKernelTest` — a 55-row matrix of authored value → re-serialized JSON + fired codes; the interpreter's side is `adapter.laws.A12JsonIngressDiffTest`.)*

**The regime, not the channel, picks the rendering — measured channel by channel (2026-07-29).** The two-renderings fact invites the question "which consumer needs which text?", and the answer is that no consumer chooses: `ValidationData.getValue` renders **once** per regime and every evaluation channel reads that one text. The measurements, on a stored decimal whose two renderings disagree:

| channel | which text | how it was measured |
|---|---|---|
| the **formal check** — number format *and* the `min/maxLength` value-string bounds | the regime's | the table above |
| **`FieldValueAsString(numberField)`** — a genuine text read compared to a literal | the regime's, identically | V2: on `maxFrac 2` a stored `250.00` makes `== "250"` fire and `== "250.00"` not; on `minFrac 2` the reverse. V1: the mirror |
| **semantic index `[F For "k"]`** | **insensitive** — both sides are normalized | `IndexFieldCache.normalizeValue` routes a Number index field through the very same `convertFromBasicType`, so `[Load For "250.00"]` matches a stored `250` *and* a stored `250.00`, and on a no-`minFrac` index field both `For "250"` and `For "250.00"` match a stored `250.00` |
| **`uniqueIndex`** (built-in) | **insensitive** — same normalization | rows `{250, 250.00}` on a `minFrac 2` index field collide (both render `250.00`) |
| **`RepetitionNotUnique`** | **insensitive** — numeric | fires in V1 too, where the two stored texts are literally different |
| a **`pattern`** on a NUMBER field | **no such channel** | `NumberType` has no `pattern` property and A12's model reader *rejects* the key outright (round-trip throws, naming it) |
| `$field.value$` interpolation | a **third** rendering, not measured here | it reads `getValueForDisplay` → `ConversionService.convertToUI` → the *presentation*-aware `convertFromBasicType` overload — a different accessor, so it is a separate question, not evidence about the eval text |

Two by-products worth keeping. A **semantic-index literal is format-checked against the index field's own declaration at code generation**: on a `minFractionalDigits: 2` index field `[F For "250"]` is refused with `MVK_INDEX_VALUE_INVALID`, so only `"250.00"` is authorable there. And `FieldValueAsString`'s long-standing description as "the RAW stored value, no re-canonicalization" is **true only of the V1 regime** — corrected in `FieldValueCoercionDiffTest`, whose V1-built documents are what measured it. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.NumberRenderChannelKernelTest` (kernel) + `NumberRenderChannelDiffTest` (ours); the consequence for the interpreter is INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF231.)*

**The reader's `asText()` is only lossless because the document mapper is deliberately configured for it.** `DocumentSerializerImpl` parses through `ObjectMapperProvider.createObjectMapperForDocSerializer()`, which enables `USE_BIG_DECIMAL_FOR_FLOATS` and disables `JsonNodeFeature.STRIP_TRAILING_BIGDECIMAL_ZEROES` under the kernel-team comment *"we want to preserve trailing zeroes and the scale of the value"*. On a **default** Jackson mapper the same `asText()` returns `250.0` for `250.00` and `1.2345678901234568E21` for a decimal beyond double precision (probe-measured, Jackson 2.18.2), so the scale a `NumberType`'s formal check and comparisons depend on is destroyed at parse time — the identical configuration pair §5 already requires for `dm.core.ModelReader`. The interpreter reaches the same result by a different route: kotlinx's `JsonPrimitive.content` retains the literal text verbatim, so **both engines preserve `250.00` and there is no divergence here** — but a reimplementation on Jackson inherits the kernel's obligation to configure the mapper.

**Both A12 mappers also accept JSON comments, and neither accepts a trailing comma — so a commented model or document is a LEGAL A12 artifact** (source-read 2026-07-28). `ObjectMapperProvider` enables `JsonParser.Feature.ALLOW_COMMENTS` in *both* factories: `createObjectMapperForDocSerializer()` (the document serializer's, above) and `createPreconfiguredObjectMapper()` (the model serializer's, which §5's `dm.core.ModelReader` pair also draws on). `ALLOW_TRAILING_COMMA` is enabled in neither. This is a **parse-configuration** fact rather than a shape fact, and it is exactly the kind a reimplementation misses by matching the tree and defaulting the parser: refusing a comment does not produce a different tree, it makes a file the A12 tooling round-trips **unreadable**. Comment tolerance therefore rides the ingress, not the format — a comment is concrete syntax that leaves no trace in the parsed document, the same relationship the condition lexer's `;;` comments have to a parsed condition ("Condition comments" below). *(Ours: one shared `A12_JSON` parse configuration behind every `:interpreter` DM-JSON/Document-JSON parse, and `ALLOW_COMMENTS` on the JVM `core.ModelReader` mapper — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF228; locked `interpreter … A12JsonCommentToleranceTest` (JVM+JS), `cli … DocumentInstanceIngressTest`, `cli … CommentedModelSourceTest`. Note `ModelReader` already carried the number pair and lacked this one: a mapper matched to A12 for one concern is a signal its other concerns were never audited.)*

Two further reader facts, source-verified the same day and material to any reimplementation. First, `repetitionsFromJson` **never consults repeatability**: an `ObjectNode` at a repeating group is one row at repetition 1, and an `ArrayNode` at a *non-repeating* group deserializes as rows 1..n (a later structural check, not the reader, is what rejects the excess) — so the shape can carry an over-repetition inbound even though the writer asserts `size() <= 1` there. Second, a **`null` value keeps the field present with no value**: `value.isNull() ? null : asText()` reaches `parseStringValueCommon(null)`, where every field type maps null (and `""`) to `ConversionResult.success(null)` → `FieldInstanceV2.ofValue(null)`. A null is therefore never an omission — omission is a **missing key**. The writer's key order is **direct fields sorted by name, then direct subgroups sorted by name** (`groupToJson`, `Map.Entry.comparingByKey`), and a null value writes `NullNode`.

*(Provenance: source read of the consult-only `../a12-kernel` checkout, kernel 30.8.1 — `IDocumentV2Serializer`, `DocumentSerializerImpl`, `DocumentV2JsonReader`, `DocumentV2JsonWriter`, `DocumentJsonAndXmlDeserializationInfoImpl`, `DocumentJsonAndXmlSerializationInfoImpl`, `FieldValueConverter`, `RepetitionsV2`, `DocumentPointerImpl`, `PathPartImpl`, `DocumentMultiPointer` — corroborated by kernel-written documents: `a12-kernel-demo` `recipe-built.json` (produced by `serializeV2`), and real documents in the sibling `dataservices` and `a12-workflows` trees. License-safe: mechanism recorded, no expression copied. Locked by `interpreter … A12PointerFormatTest` (the pointer grammar) and `interpreter … DocumentTransportTest` (the document shape, both directions, on the public boundary — IF227); the sibling repositories' kernel-produced documents remain the corroborating oracle rather than a differential.)*

**Why this is recorded so prominently:** we had invented three parallel shapes for it — the interpreter's `DocumentInputV1` placement transport, dmtool's `{"fields":{…}}` wrapper, and the corpus's `placements[]`. See the CLAUDE.md rule "NEVER invent a shape A12 already defines". **All three are gone as of 2026-07-28** (INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF227 / IF230 and the CLI consolidation): one codec, one shape, and the corpus stores it too — so the reader a reimplementation must get right is finally the reader the regression net exercises.

*Corrected 2026-07-31 — this paragraph used to name a fourth item, "a `DocumentPointer` class reusing A12's *name* with a `path` + `coordinates[]` representation", and then said "all three are gone", leaving a live public type listed among the inventions with its status unstated. A review read that as a defect; it is not one, and the type is **deliberately KEPT**.* `interpreter … InterpreterApi.DocumentPointer` is an **in-memory, ABI-locked** handle for model-owned relevance selection (arity- and owner-validated, produced by `ModelMetadata.pointer`), never a wire shape — and its `path` + repetition-coordinate decomposition is **not** an invention: A12's own `DocumentPointer` exposes exactly that pair, `fullName()` + `repetitionIndexes()`, which is how `adapter … RuntimeLaws.fmtPointer` reads a kernel pointer. What the string form must be canonical about is the **serialization** (this section's grammar, `[1]` omitted), not the in-memory decomposition. Two precision notes while correcting: the *exact* pointer carries plain integer coordinates (`GroupPointer`/`FieldPointer`), and the `RelevantCoordinate.All` wildcard belongs to the separate `RelevantSelectionPointer`, not to `DocumentPointer`.

## Terminology — the kernel has NO "cell" noun; a repetition's value is a `FeldWert` / "field value" (source-verified 2026-07-11) <a id="kf2"></a>

> Fold: folded → §3.

"Cell" (with "cell state" and "index column") is a house coinage, not kernel vocabulary: the kernel says *field value* (`FeldWert`) at a *repetition* (`Kontext`/`Kontextnummer`) and has no single noun for the pair, nor for a field's values across the repetitions; the value-state taxonomy maps to the predicates `isWertAngegeben` / not-specified / `nichtPruefungsrelevant`, not a named type. The surviving house terms are declared once in the KERNEL-SEMANTICS §3 house↔kernel vocabulary table; agent-facing surfaces use the kernel wording. *(Provenance: source sweep of the consult-only `../a12-kernel` checkout + the merged BA/dev docs, 2026-07-11, kernel 30.8.1. A vocabulary finding, no behavior; license-safe — no expression copied.)*

## DocumentModel expansion — include mounting, imported type-definitions, the synthetic RootGroup (source-verified 2026-07-11) <a id="kf3"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — model-assembly/structure semantics, outside KERNEL-SEMANTICS's runtime-evaluation scope; the clean-room reimplementation is the decoupling Stage 2 reader.

`IDocumentModelService.expand(model, resolver)` is a **mutating tree walk** that folds includes and resolves type-definitions in place. The mechanism, source-read from the consult-only `../a12-kernel` checkout (kernel 30.8.1; MECHANISM only, no expression copied):

- **Include mounting has NO synthetic alias group.** The mount point is a **pre-existing** group in the base carrying `Group.modelAlias` with empty `elements`; expansion appends the **children of the included model's root group** onto that mount group. The included root group *itself is discarded* — its final `usageType` slot replaces the mount's slot, including absence, while the other root metadata disappears. So an included field lands at `/<mountGroupName>/<childName>`; the included model's own root-group name **never appears** in any path. The provider is fully expanded before that discard: if its sole root group is itself an include mount, the nested provider's children survive directly under the outer mount. `modelAlias` is only a header-lookup key (→ `modelReferences[alias].reference`, the string the resolver actually resolves) + a transitive-dedup key, never a tree node.
- **`includeLevel`**: `SINGLE_RG` (default; the included model must have exactly one non-repeatable root group) mounts that root group's children; `MODEL_ROOT` mounts every top-level group (each kept as a subgroup of the mount, preserving its name). Each mount fully expands its provider before that mount's `excludeRules`/`excludeComputations` flags filter the final expanded tree and the retained content is mounted. Nested mounts therefore carry the effective outer exclusion behavior, except an **`attachment`-usage group shields its subtree**. The shield is decided from the group's final expanded `usageType`, never a transient marker that a nested `SINGLE_RG` include later replaces. For `SINGLE_RG`, the provider root disappears but its final `usageType` slot and shield transfer to the mount: a final attachment preserves the template's four mandated rules under an outer `excludeRules`, while a provider with no `usageType` clears a raw mount marker and supplies no shield. Both ordinary nested mounts and an include at the discarded provider root exhibit this replacement behavior in the real Kernel.
- **Malformed structural input is not uniformly strict.** Expansion rejects a non-object included element; an unknown child or provider-root element type; a known non-Group root element; a Group root without its object body; a numeric, Boolean, object, array, or blank `modelAlias`; an unknown, null, or wrong-typed `includeLevel`; null or non-array `elements`; and a `SINGLE_RG` provider with zero or multiple roots. An otherwise well-formed Field record placed either in an unknown `header` member or in an unknown member of a real Field body is rejected during deserialization with `UnrecognizedPropertyException`; it cannot become model content outside `content.modelRoot.rootGroups` and nested `Group.elements`. For the sole provider root, an absent `repeatability` defaults to one; measured refusals include `2`, `2.1`, both Booleans, an object, an array, and the nonnumeric string `"one"`. Measured acceptances include explicit null, `-1.5`, `-1`, `0`, `0.5`, `1.1`, `1.5`, `1.9`, and the numeric string `"1"`; this is an observation matrix, not a claim about the deserializer's mechanism. An explicit null alias is treated like absence and disables the include. It accepts a non-Boolean `excludeRules` or `excludeComputations` value as false; the corresponding rule or computation survives expansion. A mount group that also carries local `elements` is treated as already expanded: the local elements survive and the referenced include is silently ignored. `includeLevel` on an ordinary group is rejected, while `excludeRules` and `excludeComputations` there are accepted inertly.
- **The source deserializer accepts escaped unpaired UTF-16 surrogates.** An escaped high surrogate in a group name deserializes and expands instead of being rejected. This is a source-admission fact, not a portable value guarantee: the portable serialization contract does not assign that malformed code unit a host-independent prepared representation and therefore refuses it. *(Locked: Kernel acceptance by `WorkspaceSourceUnicodeKernelTest`; portable refusal and sizing backstop by common `WorkspaceSourceAdmissionTest` and `PreparedJsonSizeTest`.)*
- **Element ids are prefixed on include** (`includingGroupId + "_"`, recursively) but **names are not** — this is exactly the F-IDX suffixed-id-vs-name-path split (an included field's kernel id is `/Storefront/Inventory_/Catalog/ListPrice`, its name-path `/Storefront/Inventory/ListPrice`). **Type-definition ids are neither prefixed nor renamed** across models: `TypeDefCache` keys by `FieldTypeDefinition.getId()` and silently unifies two models sharing an id (origin tracked only via `allModelReferencePaths`).
- **Imported type-definitions** (header `modelReferences` with `purpose == "typeDefinitions"`) are collected recursively and merged into `content.typeDefinitions`, but **only if the base model declares no local type-definitions** ("a mixture of definition ways is not allowed"). Direct `include` references become `transitiveInclude` when hoisted into the base header.
- **Effective type**: `IField.getEffectiveType()` returns the resolved `TypeDefType`'s definition's own concrete `fieldType`, and **`Optional.empty()` for a dangling reference** (set to null on purpose — best-effort expansion). Type-defs resolve one level (a def's `fieldType` is concrete).
- **The synthetic RootGroup.** `content.modelRoot` is a fixed internal `Group` named+id'd `"RootGroup"` whose children are the modeler-visible top-level "root groups". The kernel's own `ElementUtils.getPath` **omits this synthetic root**, so a top-level group is `/Name`, not `/RootGroup/Name`. A reimplementation must index one level below the synthetic root (prefix `""` at the RootGroup level).
- **Included rule/computation references are rebound before mounting (source-verified 2026-07-12; completed 2026-07-19).** `DocumentModelExpandService.RuleAndComputationConditionUpdaterVisitor` with `EntityPathsRelativizer` identifies parsed entity-reference spans in the included model before integration and applies position-based updates as their targets move. This covers rule conditions and computation common preconditions, alternative preconditions, and operations; it is not a search for slash-shaped text, so quoted literals/comments remain ordinary text. Absolute/root-turning references that name the disappearing root are rewritten, move-invariant relative-downward references stay unchanged, and a flag-enabled unique cross-group short name is bound in the included model so an including-model collision cannot retarget it. The clean-room `ModelReader` reproduces this behavior with an original reference-aware lexer/span collector and post-exclusion residual lifecycle; `IncludeAssemblyStrategyDiffTest` compares its preparation against kernel expansion through dynamic-Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter.

*(Provenance: source sweep of `DocumentModelExpandService`, `TypeDefCache`, `Field.getEffectiveType`, `EntityReferenceResolverVisitor`, `DocumentModelRoot`, `ElementUtils`, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-11; the reference-update mechanism from `DocumentModelExpandService.RuleAndComputationConditionUpdaterVisitor` + `EntityPathsRelativizer`, 2026-07-12; out-of-tree header/Field-body record probes against kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-26. Clean-room reimplemented kernel-free as `dm.core.ModelReader`; legal structural mounting, including `MODEL_ROOT`, a nested include at the discarded provider root, and `SINGLE_RG` attachment shielding, is locked by the Java-reader/Kernel `WorkspacePreparationParityTest` matrix. `WorkspaceIncludeStructureKernelTest` locks the malformed-include accept/refuse matrix, both malformed-exclusion coercions, the out-of-tree record refusals, and the `SINGLE_RG` root constraints; `WorkspaceSourceUnicodeKernelTest` locks escaped-surrogate acceptance. Reference movement is locked by `IncludeReferenceRemapTest`, `IncludeReferenceBindingTest`, `IncludeResidualRefDiffTest`, and tri-engine `IncludeAssemblyStrategyDiffTest`. The kernel remains the oracle; R3 `checkConsistency` is untouched.)*

## §5 supplement — a NumberType's minValue/maxValue read back at the AUTHORED LITERAL's exact scale (parity-surfaced 2026-07-12) <a id="kf4"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — a read-back representation fact, outside KERNEL-SEMANTICS's evaluation scope.

The kernel's `INumberType.getMinValue()`/`getMaxValue()` return the bound **at the authored literal's exact scale** — the deserialized `BigDecimal` keeps the JSON literal verbatim, NOT the field's `maxFractionalDigits`: `10` reads back `10`, `0.00` reads back `0.00`, `50.00` reads back `50.00` (all on a scale-2 field). Surfaced by the Stage-2 parity net (`adapter.ModelIndexParityTest`) over the workspaces corpus. To match, the kernel-free `dm.core.ModelReader` parses numbers with `USE_BIG_DECIMAL_FOR_FLOATS` **and `STRIP_TRAILING_BIGDECIMAL_ZEROES` disabled** (Jackson 2.15+ strips trailing zeros by default — `50.00` → `5E+1` — which would diverge); `ModelIndex.numberMin/numberMax` then returns the node's `BigDecimal` unrescaled. *(Provenance: differential vs the live kernel over authored own-domain fixtures + the workspaces + `a12-kernel-mm` corpora, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-12. Locked `adapter.ModelIndexParityTest`.)*

## §6 supplement — `DifferenceIn{Hours,Minutes,Seconds}` is instant subtraction; DST is a model-`timeZone` axis, NOT a codegen-target axis (source-verified 2026-07-12, refutes the old §6 note) <a id="kf5"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

The Java runtime service and Java/Groovy codegen reduce the operation to signed epoch-millisecond subtraction followed by integer division truncated toward zero (`(instant₂ − instant₁) / unit`): the runtime subtracts epoch-millisecond instants (`BedingungsOperatorHelper.getDifferenceIn*`), and the generated targets call that same runtime controller instead of inlining target-specific arithmetic. The earlier claim that Java/Groovy results depend on the generated-code target was therefore mechanically wrong (IG62). DST enters through `content.modelConfig.timeZone` (copied internally as `IMetaKeysInternal.MODEL_TIME_ZONE`, default `UTC`) at parse time. The documentation emphasizes `UTC` and `Europe/Berlin`, but code does not whitelist those two: `NoMetaModelChecks.checkTimeZone` accepts Java `TimeZone` ids except unknown ids that collapse to GMT (literal GMT is valid), and runtime parsing passes the accepted id to `TimeZone.getTimeZone`. Plain DATE values are zoned too—a `yyyy-MM-dd` value is midnight in the model zone. Under UTC the wall-clock and instant gaps agree; under Berlin, `2024-03-31T01:30:00` CET → `03:30:00` CEST is one elapsed hour. **Interpreter status:** IF128 modeled UTC/GMT and the modern 1996+ Berlin regime; it did not close the kernel's wider accepted zone-id/full-history domain, now IG83. Locked for the exercised Berlin cases by `adapter.laws.DstTimeZoneDiffTest` + `adapter.laws.WallDayArithmeticDiffTest` and kernel-free JVM+JS `ModelTimeZoneTest`. *(Provenance: source sweep of `BedingungsOperatorHelper`/`RuntimeController`/`ValidationDateParser`/the codegen `DateDiffOperation` templates, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-12; accepted-id re-audit of `NoMetaModelChecks`, 2026-07-19. The canonical correction is in KERNEL-SEMANTICS §6.)*

## §6 supplement — the Berlin DST edge hours, calendar-step day differences, and wall-day addition (kernel-probed 2026-07-18; corrected 2026-07-20) <a id="kf6"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

Probe-verified against the real kernel (`RuntimeLaws` over a Berlin-zoned model, 30.8.1), locked by `adapter.laws.DstTimeZoneDiffTest` + `adapter.laws.WallDayArithmeticDiffTest` (kernel differential) and `interpreter ModelTimeZoneTest` (kernel-free, JVM+JS):

- **The spring-forward gap is a FORMAL error.** A stored wall time that does not exist (`2024-03-31T02:30:00` Berlin) draws `datumFormatFalsch` — `ValidationDateParser`'s `setLenient(false)` round-trip rejects it — and suppresses a referencing rule, exactly like any malformed datetime. It is NOT rolled forward.
- **The fall-back ambiguous hour parses as STANDARD time** (CET): `01:30 → 02:30` on `2024-10-27` is `DifferenceInMinutes = 120`, and two stored `02:30` values are instant-equal.
- **Datetime comparison and `DifferenceIn{Hours,Minutes,Seconds}` are instant-based end-to-end, and a CHAINED `Add{Hours,…}` result keeps its exact instant** (`VkDate` identity): `AddHours(01:30 CEST, 1)` lands on the DAYLIGHT side of the ambiguous hour, so it is NOT equal to the equal-looking stored `"02:30:00"` (which re-parses standard) — the pair is 60 real minutes apart.
- **`DifferenceInDays` counts signed model-zone legacy-calendar day steps, not `Δ wall-seconds / 86 400`.** The earlier operand advances by `Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH` until the next stateful landing would pass the later instant; `23:00 → 01:00` next day is **0**, while Berlin `2024-03-30T02:30 → 2024-03-31T01:45` is **1** because the gap step lands at `01:30`. That changed clock persists: the same start → `2024-04-01T02:00` is **2**, not the result of one direct two-day landing. DATE_TIME and mixed `(datetime, date)` operands are legal; a DATE reads as model-zone midnight. Locked by `CalendarStepDifferenceInDaysDiffTest`.
- **`AddDays` accepts a DATE_TIME operand and is TIME-PRESERVING** (`15T22:00 + 1 = 16T22:00`). The landing rule is **source-instant-based and direction-INDEPENDENT** *(corrected 2026-07-29, from a12-kernel-lean SPEC-2026-07-29-01; the earlier account below resolved a nominal wall LABEL by direction of travel)*: `GregorianCalendar.add(DAY_OF_MONTH, n)` adds the raw elapsed span to the SOURCE instant (so the source's offset and its sub-second remainder ride along), **re-fits** by the offset delta when the landing's offset differs — preserving the wall clock — and keeps that re-fit **only when it preserves the landing's civil date**. Two adjacent additions can therefore select different sides of one overlap: a step reaching 1916-10-01 from a CEST September source lands on the daylight side while a 214-day add from a CET March source lands on the fresh CET side — same civil label, one hour apart.
  - **The measured separator** is the composite `DifferenceInDays(2023-04-01T02:30, 2024-04-05T02:30)` = **369** (both kernel strategies). The whole-year seed lands its candidate at 2024-03-31T02:30, which is in the spring GAP; carrying the CEST source offset and re-fitting puts the cursor at wall `03:30`, and every subsequent day step carries that changed clock — so the count stops one short of the naive 370. Under the old direction-based rule the cursor landed at `01:30` and the interpreter returned 370. Locked by `CalendarStepDifferenceInDaysDiffTest` (its candidate set now brackets 368/369/370 so the value is discriminated, not merely matched).
- **`AddMonths`/`AddYears` land by SIGN-INDEPENDENT compute-time resolution, not by direction — a different mechanism from the day arm** *(added 2026-07-29, a12-kernel-lean SPEC-2026-07-29-01)*. `Calendar.MONTH`/`Calendar.YEAR` mutate the civil fields and let `GregorianCalendar` re-resolve them, so no source instant rides along: an **overlap** label takes the **later** instant and a **gap** label normalizes to the **post-gap** label (`02:30` → `03:30`).
  - **The measured separator is the GAP half**, on the maintained `DifferenceInDays` surface, where the year helper qualifies the whole-year candidate: `DifferenceInDays(2003-03-31T02:30, 2024-03-31T03:00)` = **7670** on both kernel strategies. The 21-year candidate's landing falls in the 2024 spring gap; the post-gap resolution puts it at `01:30Z`, AFTER the later endpoint (`01:00Z`), so the candidate is rejected. The previous direction-selected forward resolution put it at the pre-gap `00:30Z`, admitted the candidate, and froze the seed one day high → 7671. The day loop only ever ADDS, so an admitted-but-overshooting seed is unrecoverable — which is what makes this case separating where a one-year overlap construction is not.
  - **The OVERLAP half is now measured too, by a different observer** *(2026-07-29)*. A `DifferenceInDays` construction cannot show it — the day loop only ever adds, so a seed difference self-corrects (worked through for a 2023-10-27 → 2024-10-27 ambiguous landing, and confirmed by the loop's behaviour). **Direct Date equality** over the one ambiguous midnight can, because it introduces no second calendar mutation: `AddMonths(Date("1","9","1916"), 1) == Date("1","10","1916")` and the `AddYears` twin are **true**, the `AddDays` shape is **false**, on both kernel strategies. So month/year taking the LATER copy — and the day arm keeping its source season — are observed facts. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ConstructedDateInstantDiffTest`, mutation-checked — disabling day retention makes the kernel and interpreter disagree on the first row — plus JVM/Node `interpreter ConstructedDateInstantTest`.)*
  - **Exact-instant retention is what makes any of it observable.** A constructed Date retains the instant its FRESH parse resolution selected; a Date-valued day shift retains its source-instant landing; a Date-valued month/year shift retains its compute-time field-mutation landing. Without retention the `Val` boundary (`asTemporalVal` → `Val.Txt(text)`) reduces every Date to its civil label, and the three shapes above are indistinguishable. The date comparison therefore resolves instants structurally, exactly as the DATE_TIME branch already did; it is **not** gated on retention, because a field read must derive its instant by the ordinary parse resolution (which is what the kernel's `VkDate` holds for it) — gating on retention would compare a shifted Date against a field by label and diverge. Footprint: wherever midnight is unambiguous the derived instant and the label order agree, so only 1916-10-01 can change.
  - **The millisecond arm: no fix was required, and that is measured.** A DAY shift carries the source instant's sub-second remainder — with the validation clock at `…T12:00:00.999`, a field holding the exact second one day out is BEFORE `AddDays(Now, 1)` and not equal to it, on both kernel strategies and in the interpreter (`NowMillisecondIdentityDiffTest`, which already covered the sub-day residual).
  - **The February-28 clock clearing is source-faithful but UNOBSERVABLE on the maintained surface** *(mutation-measured 2026-07-29)*. The branch clears the time-of-day when a leap-year Feb 28 source lands in a non-leap year, and its only consumer is the whole-year qualification inside `DifferenceInDays` — which the day loop absorbs. Disabling the branch outright leaves **273 date/calendar/Berlin/DST/wall-day differential cases unchanged**. It is retained because it matches the kernel source, not because any observation requires it.
  - Do **not** generalize this to `AddMonths`: the month arm shares the source reading but has no maintained separator yet, so its disposition is still open.
  - The **superseded** reading, kept because its four adjacent ±1-day probes are still green and cannot distinguish the two accounts: "a landing in a special hour resolves so the step never OVERSHOOTS the direction of travel — a forward add takes the DAYLIGHT (earlier) instant, a backward add the STANDARD (later) instant." It coincides with the source-instant rule whenever source and landing share an offset season, which every ±1-day probe did.
- **Operand gates** (`MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`): `DifferenceIn{Hours,Minutes,Seconds}` REJECT a plain DATE operand; `DifferenceIn{Months,Years}`, `AddMonths`, and `AddYears` REJECT a DATE_TIME operand — so those unprobed combinations are unreachable in a valid model.
- **Historical status:** this was the modern-only IF128 state. IF155/IF165 now pin and exhaustively lock the full legacy-`java.util.TimeZone` Berlin history described in the §6 supplement below. The kernel's own JS/TS runtime (luxon) may resolve edge hours differently from its Java strategies; per IF136 the interpreter follows dynamic Groovy and records a static-strategy split rather than adopting it.

## §6 supplement — the Berlin FULL history is a pinned `java.util.TimeZone` step function; the kernel path DROPS the pre-1916 LMT; offsets are {CET, CEST, CEMT} (source + probe 2026-07-20, the IG83 characterization) <a id="kf7"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

The kernel parses every DATE/DATE_TIME with `ValidationDateParser` = `SimpleDateFormat` + `TimeZone.setTimeZone(TimeZone.getTimeZone(id))` — the **legacy `java.util.TimeZone`**, NOT `java.time`. That distinction is material for Europe/Berlin's history, and it was probed exhaustively (JDK 21.0.11, **tzdb 2026a**; `SimpleDateFormat` non-lenient is the exact kernel mechanism):

- **The kernel path DROPS the pre-1916 LMT.** `java.time.ZoneRules` models Berlin before 1893-04-01 as **LMT +00:53:28 (3208 s)**, but `java.util.TimeZone.getOffset` — the kernel's path — returns **flat CET +01:00 (3600 s)** for every instant before the first *DST* transition (1916-04-30). So a legal 1583–1916 Berlin value is CET to the kernel; the interpreter must match `java.util`, not `java.time`. (Of the 63 `java.time` transitions, exactly the 1893 LMT→CET one is not seen by `java.util`; the other 62 agree.)
- **Berlin has THREE historical offsets, not two:** CET +01:00 (3600), CEST +02:00 (7200), and **CEMT +03:00 (10800)** — the 1945 *and* 1947 double-summer windows (e.g. 1945-05-24 → 1945-09-24 the offset is 10800). The pre-2026 interpreter assumed {3600, 7200}; the model must admit 10800.
- **The 1980–1995 DST end is the last Sunday of SEPTEMBER**, not October; only from **1996** does the modern EU rule (last-Sunday-March → last-Sunday-October, 01:00 UTC) hold continuously. So a hand rule is exact only for 1996+; 1916→1995 needs the explicit transition table.
- **Exactly ONE date in Berlin's pinned history has a special MIDNIGHT, and it is 1916-10-01** (enumerated over all 62 transitions in the pinned table, 2026-07-29). Two transitions touch midnight: 1916-04-30 springs forward from wall `23:00` straight to `00:00` on May 1, so the GAP is `[23:00 Apr 30, 00:00 May 1)` — midnight itself exists; and 1916-10-01 falls back from `01:00` to `00:00`, so the wall interval `[00:00, 01:00)` on that date occurs **twice** and `1916-10-01T00:00` is **ambiguous**. Every other transition sits at 01:00/02:00/03:00 local.
- **An admitted component constant's positional DECODING, including its digit repertoire** (measured 2026-07-29, closing a12-kernel-lean SPEC-2026-07-27-11). Day has **no width gate**, so `"0001"` is the 1st; a one-digit month decodes; the four-part form composes **Century × 100 + Short-Year**, and a `"00"` Short-Year stays **present** (a fixed zero, not an omission) so `19`/`00` is 1900. The digit repertoire is **per UTF-16 code unit** — `StringUtils.isNumeric` loops `Character.isDigit(char)` — so a **BMP** non-ASCII digit sequence is admitted and decodes (Arabic-Indic `"١٠"` is the 10th), while a **supplementary-plane** digit is refused (`MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`) because its surrogate halves are not digits. A code-POINT digit predicate would wrongly admit the supplementary form — that is the whole content of the boundary.
  - **Each position's range, pinned on both sides** so the bound is located rather than merely exceeded: Day `[1,32)` (31 ✓, 32 ✗, 0 ✗), Month `[1,13)` (12 ✓, 13 ✗, 0 ✗), complete Year `[1800,2200)` (1800 ✓, 2199 ✓, 2200 ✗, 1799 ✗), Century `[18,22)` (18 ✓, 21 ✓, 22 ✗, 17 ✗), Short-Year `[0,100)`. The complete Year is **width-gated at exactly four** code units and Century at exactly two — unlike Day and Month, which have no width gate at all — so `"196"`, `"19634"` and a one-digit century are refused. An **empty** component is refused (the digit sequence must be nonempty). Every refusal is the single code `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`. Constants **mix freely with field components**, since each position is checked independently.
  - **The Time LITERAL gate is a different rule**, exact ASCII `HH:mm:ss`, so the same BMP digits are REFUSED there (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`). The two gates are locked as a pair so they cannot be collapsed into one.
  - *(Locked: `rulekit.validate.laws.DateComponentConstantLawsTest` for admission and both Unicode arms; `adapter.laws.DateComponentConstantDiffTest` for the positional runtime values, tri-checked with a negative control per row; `interpreter DateComponentConstantTest` for JVM+Node — the BMP row is host-sensitive, since it depends on the platform's decimal decoding accepting a non-ASCII digit, and it holds on both.)*
- **A `Date(...)`/`Time(...)` component constant must be QUOTED — `datumParam` has no bare-number alternative** (measured 2026-07-29). The grammar is `datumParam: entitySpec | datumExtractOp | StringLiteral`, so `Date("30", "9", "1916")` is admitted while `Date(30, 9, 1916)` draws **`MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`** — the kernel reads the digits as an entity name, not a number. **This was a live defect in our own authoring path:** `DslRenderer` rendered a `NumberLiteral` component bare, so `Dsl.date(number(30), number(9), number(1916))` produced a condition the kernel refuses; `Dsl.time` was never exposed because its factory takes Strings. Nothing exercised the shape end-to-end, which is why it survived. *(Fixed by a `renderDateParam` position renderer; locked by `rulekit.dsl.RenderConditionTest.rendersConstantDateAndTimeComponentsQuoted` and `rulekit.validate.laws.DateComponentConstantLawsTest`, which also measures the combined left-hand-shift vs constructed-Date comparison a12-kernel-lean's SPEC-2026-07-29-01 needs.)*
  - **Why it matters: a plain DATE is model-zone midnight**, so this is the single witness by which a DATE-only operand can reach overlap resolution at all. It is why a12-kernel-lean's SPEC-2026-07-29-01 examples both land on 1916-10-01 — `AddDays(Date(30, 9, 1916), 1)` and `AddDays(Date(1, 3, 1916), 214)` select opposite sides of that one overlap.
  - **Consequence for the month arm** (SPEC-2026-07-29-01, still open): the tempting disposition "a month landing's instant is unreachable because `AddMonths` rejects DATE_TIME (locked in `DateFunctionsOverDateTimeDiffTest`) and a DATE's midnight is never special" is **false** — that second link breaks here. `AddMonths(Date(1, 9, 1916), 1)` lands on 1916-10-01, whose midnight is ambiguous, so the month arm does need a resolution rule and a separator is constructible. Observing it needs the landing instant to survive into a consumer, which is the same open `TemporalValue` exact-instant retention item.
- **Wall→instant resolution generalizes the IF128 rule uniformly across all offsets** (probed, `SimpleDateFormat` non-lenient): a **gap** wall time (spring-forward, `oAfter>oBefore`) is REJECTED (`datumFormatFalsch`) in every era (1945-05-24T02:30 and 2024-03-31T02:30 both throw `ParseException`); an **overlap** wall time (fall-back, `oAfter<oBefore`) resolves to the **smaller (after) offset** — 2024-10-27T02:30 → CET(3600), 1945-09-24T02:30 → CEST(7200, not the 10800 before it), 1945-11-18T02:30 → CET(3600). So "try the candidate offsets ascending, take the first instant whose true offset matches" reproduces the kernel for {3600, 7200, 10800} and subsumes the old two-offset code.
- **The accepted-zone-id contract** (`NoMetaModelChecks.checkTimeZone`, restated + probe-confirmed): a model `timeZone` is kernel-VALID iff it is the literal `"GMT"` OR `TimeZone.getTimeZone(id).getID() != "GMT"` — i.e. any known IANA id (`Europe/Berlin`, `America/New_York`, `Asia/Tokyo`, …), a `GMT±HH:MM` offset form, `UTC`, `Zulu`, `Etc/UTC`; REJECTED (`MVK_INVALID_TIME_ZONE`) only when the id silently collapses to GMT (a typo like `Europe/Berln`, or `""`). The kernel's own `IMetaKeysInternal` docs nevertheless narrow *supported* to "only `UTC` and `Europe/Berlin`", and every model in our corpora uses `Europe/Berlin` (or the UTC default) — no exotic zone appears.

**Interpreter status:** these facts shipped as **IF155**, review-completed by **IF165/IF166** — a Berlin transition table pinned to the JDK 21.0.11 / tzdb 2026a observation profile (the 62 `java.util` transitions above, offsets {3600, 7200, 10800}, pre-1916 flat CET, the recurring EU rule for 1998+), replacing the 1996+-only hand rule. Exact `{UTC, GMT, Europe/Berlin}` is the enforced supported set; explicit invalid ids are configuration errors, while known kernel-legal outside-set ids receive `RK_INTERPRETER_TIMEZONE_UNSUPPORTED`. The wider zone-id domain remains IG83 Route A, owner-deferred. *(Provenance: `NoMetaModelChecks.checkTimeZone` source, kernel 30.8.1; `java.util.TimeZone`/`SimpleDateFormat`/`java.time.ZoneRules` probes on JDK 21.0.11 tzdb 2026a, 2026-07-20. `BerlinHistoricalTzTest` locks the independent pinned vector and mutation classes; `BerlinTransitionOracleDiffTest` compares before/at/after every transition against both executing kernel strategies and reports the active profile.)*

## §7 supplement — `Length` (and string min/maxLength) count UTF-16 code units (source + lock, 2026-07-12, graduates IG63) <a id="kf8"></a>

> Fold: folded → §7.

The kernel measures string length in **UTF-16 code units** in every length-bearing path, with no code-point folding or grapheme clustering: `Length(field)` returns the value's UTF-16-unit count (runtime `BigDecimalUtils.laenge` → the platform string length — Java `String.length()` / TS `string.length`; the Groovy/Java/JS codegen reuse the same runtime helper, so no strategy diverges), and the `minLength`/`maxLength` formal checks (`stringZuKurz`/`stringZuLang`) compare that same count. So a **decomposed combining sequence** `e` + `U+0301` counts as **2** (the sense in which §7 says a combining sequence is "several characters"), not 1 grapheme. Grapheme clustering appears only in the *legal-charset / DIN-91379* acceptance check (IF43), never in the count. **Interpreter parity:** the interpreter counts with Kotlin `String.length` = UTF-16 units on both JVM + JS, matching the kernel; the combining case is differential-locked (`adapter.laws.AbsLengthDiffTest` — Length == 2, ≠ 1) + multiplatform (`interpreter … LengthUnicodeCountTest`). **Residual (recorded, unreachable in practice):** a supplementary-plane / astral character (`U+1F600`, a surrogate pair) is `String.length` 2 and the kernel would count 2 under a permissive charset, but the interpreter currently reads a supplementary-plane string as a non-plain operand (its `Length` is *unknown*, so a `Length == 2` rule does not fire) — a divergence that is unreachable under any realistic charset (the default is BMP, and DIN 91379 excludes astral chars, so such a value is itself an illegal-character formal error). Not locked; characterized here. *(Provenance: source read of the kernel `Length`/`FormatDefinitionString` paths + codegen templates, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-12; interpreter divergence probe-confirmed the same day.)*

## §1 supplement — Boolean/Confirm comparability codes <a id="kf9"></a>

> Fold: folded → §1.

The Boolean≠Confirm split is enforced at parse time with distinct codes: a Boolean compares only to `True`/`False` or another Boolean — anything else (e.g. a string) is **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_YESNO`**; a Confirm compares only to `True` or another Confirm — `[Confirm] == False` and Boolean↔Confirm cross-comparisons are **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_YES`**. "Not confirmed" is written `[Confirm] != True` (an unset confirm counts as not confirmed). *(Locked: `validate.laws.BooleanConfirmLawsTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — Boolean three-state matrix (runtime, 2026-06-06) <a id="kf10"></a>

> Fold: folded → §1.

Runtime-verified in a filled row: an unset Boolean satisfies neither `== True` nor `== False`; `FieldFilled` holds for both values — so `[Flag] == True` ≠ `FieldFilled(Flag)` and `[Flag] == False` ≠ "unchecked". Contrast a Confirm, whose unset value *is* `False` in comparisons. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.BooleanThreeStateLawsTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — the "not …" field-list quantifiers are not complements (runtime, 2026-06-06) <a id="kf11"></a>

> Fold: folded → §1 + §12.

The three negative fill-quantifiers disagree at the edges; the trap is the all-empty case, which `NotAllFieldsFilled` and `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` include but `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` excludes (needs ≥ 1 filled) — canonical §1. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FieldListQuantifierLawsTest`; interpreter parity `adapter.laws.FieldListQuantifierDiffTest`.)*

**The message TYPE of a fired quantifier is data-dependent, not per-operator (runtime, 2026-06-23; unavailable boundary completed 2026-07-20):** `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` types OMISSION at zero known-filled with no unavailable cell and VALUE at two or more known-filled cells, so a static per-operator polarity is wrong for it. *(Locked: `FieldListQuantifierDiffTest.messageTypeMatchesKernel` + `ValidationFillQuantifierProjectionDiffTest`.)*

**"Kontext" = Group — a legacy German kernel term (owner-confirmed, 2026-06-26).** In older kernel/DSL vocabulary a repeatable **group** is a *Kontext* ("context"); the German operator `KontextAngegeben(G)` is today's **`GroupFilled(G)`**, and the structural over-repetition error `zuGrosseKontextnummer` (below) is literally "context number too large" = a field instance beyond the group's declared repeatability. So a real-world rule guarded by `KontextAngegeben(<group>)` transfers to `GroupFilled(<group>)` (rulekit: `m.group(path).filled()`). It is group presence, not a field predicate; for a non-repeatable group the content must have passed formal admission, as refined in the §9 group-presence finding below. *(Locked by `adapter.laws.GroupFilledDiffTest` + `adapter.laws.GroupPresenceFormalInvalidDiffTest`.)*

**Group-list quantifier asterisk bar (source + probe, 2026-06-17).** The *group*-list quantifiers `NotAllGroupsFilled`/`GroupsNotCollectivelyFilled`/`AllGroupsFilled` reject an **asterisk** argument — `NotAllGroupsFilled(Items*, ShippingAddress)` draws **`MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED`** (source `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkNoWildCardsInGroup`, fired on `ref.hasWildCardsInGroup()`). **Group-list quantifier rootGroup bar (source + accept/reject lock, 2026-07-12; graduates IG65).** The **same three** also **bar any rootGroup argument**: `CheckKontextListenBedingungImpl` (A12 path) routes them through `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkNotRootGroupReferenced`, firing **`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED`** ("no Vordruck/root group may be referenced") when any arg is a top-level group — ahead of the arity/asterisk checks. The **other two** (`NoGroupFilled`/`AtLeastOneGroupFilled`) instead route through `checkNoRootGroupWhenMoreThanOneParameter`: a **sole** rootGroup is **accepted**; a rootGroup **with a second parameter** draws **`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_WITH_OTHER_PARAMETERS`** ("a root group may only be the sole parameter"). Confirmed on a **two-non-repeatable-root** fixture (`/Policy` + `/Claim`) — the single-root order fixture couldn't reach it (a rootGroup + sibling trips `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` first), and a *repeatable* root can't be referenced bare (it demands an asterisk, itself barred). *(Locked: `validate.laws.GroupQuantifierLawsTest` — the asterisk bar, the three→`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED`, the two accept a sole root + reject root+other→`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_WITH_OTHER_PARAMETERS`.)*

## §1 supplement — the FIXED group-list quantifiers consume one common field-or-group entity list (source + tri-lock, 2026-07-23; SPEC-2026-07-23-09) <a id="kf12"></a>

> Fold: folded → §1.

Despite their group-list names, the five fixed forms (`AllGroupsFilled`, `NoGroupFilled`, `AtLeastOneGroupFilled`, `NotAllGroupsFilled`, `GroupsNotCollectivelyFilled`) admit **BOTH field and group operands** — `AllGroupsFilled(PolicyNumber, Vehicle)` and even field-only `AllGroupsFilled(PolicyNumber, IncidentNote)` are kernel-legal. The mechanism, from source:

- **Structural gate** — `CheckKontextListenBedingungImpl` routes all five through `CheckEntityListenUtils` over ONE entity list; only the *scalar* `GroupFilled`/`GroupNotFilled` pair carries `checkOnlyGroups` (`MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`), so the list forms admit fields. The shared gates apply to fields too: a direct duplicate draws `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1`, a field (or group) nested under a listed group `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2`, the ≥2-arity family fires `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALID2` even on a singleton *field* (`checkIsMoreThanOneGroupOrField` — the kernel's own name says "group or field"), and a starred field operand (`Damages*/Description`) draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED` there. `NoGroupFilled`/`AtLeastOneGroupFilled` accept a singleton field.
- **Runtime** — `RuntimeController.alleKontexteAngegeben`/`keinKontextAngegeben`/`mindestensEinKontextAngegeben`/`nichtAlleOderKeinKontextAngegeben` (+ the collectively composite) delegate to the SAME `…EntityAngegeben` helpers the field-list quantifiers use: each plain operand contributes its three-way `isWertAngegeben` state — for a **field**: filled / empty / unavailable (formally invalid **or, in partial validation, not check-relevant — in BOTH directions**: an empty non-relevant field is no empty witness either); for a **group**: the §9 product state (admitted content × error × relevance). The five consume the partial tally `(pₜ, pₑ, pᵤ)` per the §1 fixed-entity-list table in [KERNEL-SEMANTICS](KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md); unavailable is never empty, and a decisive witness fires beside an unavailable sibling (`AtLeastOneGroupFilled(malformed, filledGroup)` fires; `NotAllGroupsFilled(malformed, emptyGroup)` fires; `NoGroupFilled(malformed, empty)` does NOT).
- **Lowering preserves both kinds** — `KontextListenBedingungCreator`/`ExpandService` expand a group operand to its descendant fields while a field operand passes through verbatim, so both kernel strategies (dynamic-Groovy md-runtime and static-Java codegen) evaluate the same list.
- **A fixed singleton has the scalar presence semantics** — `NoGroupFilled(F)` ≡ `FieldNotFilled(F)` and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(F)` ≡ `FieldFilled(F)` across filled/empty/malformed (the kernel itself lowers `KEIN_KONTEXT`/`MINDESTENS_EIN_KONTEXT` to the field forms); the read converter canonicalizes the `NoGroupFilled` singleton to the presence node exactly like its single-group sibling.
- **The direct-duplicate bar does NOT reach a WILDCARDED operand, so the same starred group may repeat** *(added 2026-07-29, reconciling lean SPEC-2026-07-23-09's follow-up)* — `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkDuplicatesInDirectRefs` continues past `ref.hasWildCards()` here exactly as it does for the aggregate entity list (KF below, SPEC-2026-07-22-07), so `NoGroupFilled(Damages*, Damages*)` and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(Damages*, Damages*)` are **accepted** — beside a plain operand too. The exemption is wildcard-only: the same shape without a star is still `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1`, and the indirect group/descendant overlap is still `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2`. The other three operators cannot show it at all — the wildcard bar refuses them first with a different code (`MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED`), which is what keeps the two gates distinguishable. **Repeating the witness is truth-idempotent but NOT polarity-idempotent in a naive implementation:** the interpreter typed `NoGroupFilled(G*, G*)` at zero rows VALUE where the kernel says OMISSION, because its add-a-row fillability move required the starred operand to be the *sole* one — with two operands the fixed-arity move ran over an empty `1..f` range. The two moves are independent and a list offers both. *(Locked: `rulekit.validate.laws.GroupListEntityOperandLawsTest` (admission + the retained direct/indirect bars), `adapter.GroupListEntityOperandReadTest` (both occurrences survive render + read-back), `adapter.laws.StarredGroupQuantifierDiffTest` (dual-engine truth AND polarity for the repeated shape), `interpreter StarredGroupQuantifierTest` (JVM+Node).)*

The typed surface carries this as the `GroupListOperand` sum (`FieldRef` | `GroupListRef`) on `GroupListPredicate` — the starred single-group scan (IF68) stays a distinct route. *(Locked: `validate.laws.GroupListEntityOperandLawsTest` (admission + duplicate/overlap/arity/wildcard), tri-engine `adapter.laws.GroupListEntityOperandDiffTest` (mixed/field-only fill states, formal-invalid + partial-relevance field operands, decisive witnesses, singleton equivalence — dynamic-Groovy ∧ static-Java ∧ interpreter), JVM+JS `GroupListFieldOperandTest`, round-trip `adapter.GroupListEntityOperandReadTest`.)*

**In a COMPUTATION precondition the read is ORDER-observable and a starred group scope may be MIXED into the list (dual-kernel-route + JVM/Node, SPEC-2026-07-23-09 compute supplement, 2026-07-24).** The compute-phase `…EntityAngegeben` read is the same order-driven scan as the field-fill quantifiers (§8/IF85), not a full tally: the operands are read in list order and the scan STOPS at its deciding operand (`AtLeastOne` at the first filled, `No` at the first filled, `All`/`NotAll` at the first empty), so a formally-invalid FIELD operand AFTER the deciding one is never read and cannot poison — `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(filledField, malformedField)` and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(filledGroup, malformedField)` both COMPUTE (a value), never clear. A FIELD operand read poisons on a formally-invalid value (`getWertXML` → `CalculationFieldException`, "fehlerhaft berechnet" — the reading computation clears and its dependents' reads poison transitively); an **absent** field is a plain clear (the precondition is FALSE → empty-as-0 downstream, IF77). A GROUP operand is **structural presence, never poison** — a malformed-ONLY group is FILLED (so `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(malformedGroup)` computes and `NoGroupFilled(malformedGroup)` is a plain clear), because a group's presence read does not read a cell value. A STARRED group scope (`NoGroupFilled(G*)`/`AtLeastOneGroupFilled(G*)`, the only forms the kernel lets carry a `*`) may be MIXED with plain operands (`AtLeastOneGroupFilled(field, Coverages*)`), contributing one FILLED per instantiated row; an un-instantiated star contributes nothing. This is [IF219](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md) on the interpreter side. *(Locked: tri-engine `adapter.laws.GroupListEntityOperandComputeDiffTest` (field/group clean·invalid·absent, read-order stop, mixed starred scope — dynamic-Groovy ∧ static-Java ∧ interpreter, discriminated by a dependent `Audit = [Payout]`), mixed-star validation in `adapter.laws.StarredGroupQuantifierDiffTest`, JVM+Node `interpreter.GroupListFieldOperandComputeTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — a STARRED single-group quantifier counts the group's INSTANTIATED repetitions (probe-verified, both strategies, 2026-07-07) <a id="kf13"></a>

> Fold: folded → §1.

All four claims below are canonical in §1 "How the fill quantifiers count"; the evidence skeleton:

`NoGroupFilled(G*)` / `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(G*)` (the kernel's selection idiom) tally the group's **instantiated** repetitions — a created-but-empty row is content — so they fire iff **zero** / **≥ 1** rows; polarity OMISSION / VALUE. Only these two admit the starred single-group form. *(IF68; locked `adapter.laws.StarredGroupQuantifierDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.StarredGroupQuantifierTest`.)*

**A field-fill quantifier over a GROUP SCOPE = "the fields inside the group", recursively — and the runtime uses TWO iteration ranges (source `RuntimeController` + `ExpandService`, probe-confirmed 2026-07-06).** `ExpandService` rewrites a group operand to a field-list quantifier over `getAllContainedFields(group)` (recursive; a repeatable group carries its `*` onto each descendant). `AllFieldsFilled`/`NotAllFieldsFilled` iterate the **full declared range** (`EntityIterator` over `1..repeatability`, no `setzeEndIndicesAufMaxGesetzt` clamp — an un-instantiated rep reads empty); `AtLeastOneFieldFilled`/`NoFieldFilled`/`MoreThanOneFieldFilled`/`NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` clamp to the **instantiated** rows; `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` mixes the two. *(IF49; locked `adapter.laws.GroupScopedFieldFillDiffTest` — bare/starred × both ranges × firing + polarity.)*

**…and a VALUE AGGREGATE accepts a GROUP operand the same way (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** `Sum`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue`/`NumberOfDifferentValues` over a starred group feed the combiner every contained field's cells, recursively; a malformed contained cell poisons like a field-star cell; an empty group is the empty-selection 0 (`NumberOfDifferentValues`'s static scale is 0 — a non-scale-0 target draws `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`). A `Having` on a group star is codegen-rejected (`MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`), so only the unfiltered expansion is reachable. *(IF99; locked `adapter.laws.GroupStarValueAggregateDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … GroupStarValueAggregateTest`.)*

**In unfiltered VALIDATION, a formally unavailable star cell remains in the range but enters neither fill bucket (source + differential 2026-07-08/20).** Malformed and declared-constraint-invalid cells behave identically: they contribute to `n` but to neither known-filled `t` nor known-empty `f`. The seven formulas can still fire from a sufficient known witness—for example `MoreThanOneFieldFilled` and the VALUE arm of `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` hold at `t=2,u=1`, while `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` holds OMISSION at `t=1,e=1,u=1`. Without such a witness the leaf exposes only the collapsed `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`; an invalid-only range makes both `AtLeastOneFieldFilled` and `NoFieldFilled` silent. Computation is different: a reached invalid cell poisons per the §11 finding below, by its own mechanism. A value aggregate is non-evaluable when its scan reaches a non-relevant cell, wherever in the selection that cell sits (measured on the verdict; the ordering, its pre-loop survey, and their limits are the numeric-combiner finding in §3 below). *(IF84/IF188; locked `ValidationFillQuantifierProjectionDiffTest`, `StarQuantifierInvalidCellDiffTest`, and `AggMalformedDiffTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — `MaxValue`/`MinValue` over an EMPTY operand set = 0, not undefined (runtime, probe-confirmed 2026-07-06) <a id="kf14"></a>

> Fold: folded → §2.

One empty operand is dropped from the fold (`MaxValue(-5, empty) == -5` — no `0` folded in); an **entirely empty** selection folds to the fillable `0`, the same empty identity as `Sum` — distinct from a malformed operand, which suppresses. With no present value, that identity is fillable in **both directions regardless of field signedness or aggregate operator**; `Sum`/`MaxValue`/`MinValue` apply their ordinary directions only after a present value enters the fold. Canonical §2 "aggregate identities" carries both, including why the identity stayed latent under most thresholds (`0` and "suppressed" coincide until a shape like `MinValue(...) < 10` separates them). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.MultiFieldAggregateDiffTest` + `NumericAggregatePolarityDiffTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — the COUNT aggregates are monotonic non-decreasing → grow-only polarity (runtime, probe-confirmed 2026-07-06) <a id="kf15"></a>

> Fold: folded → §12.

Filling can only raise a count, so a fired `count >= n` / `> n` is VALUE while `< n` / `== n` stays OMISSION — a count types like an unsigned `Sum` (canGrow while a fillable operand remains, never canShrink); a static per-operator polarity is wrong. Canonical §12 "the counts can only grow". Latent until the `>=` direction was pinned. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.MultiFieldAggregateDiffTest.firedPolarityMatchesKernel` + `NumberOfFilledDiffTest`.)*

## Stored conditions are usually canonical English; configured German storage is also kernel-legal (corrected 2026-07-26) <a id="kf16"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — a serialization-tier fact (the stored form of conditions), outside the canonical doc's evaluation scope.

**Corrected 2026-07-26:** the earlier conclusion confused one serializer/workspace convention with the legal DM-JSON input domain. The real German A12 Tools workspace (2025.06-ext5) does store English keyword text—every observed `errorCondition` reads `GroupFilled(...)`/`FieldNotFilled(...)`/`And`/`NotExactlyOneFieldFilled(...)`/`MinValue(...)`, never `Und`/`FeldAngegeben`—but that corpus observation does not make German stored syntax illegal.

A DM-JSON model whose `content.modelConfig.conditionLanguage.code` is `de_DE` and whose rule/computation fragments contain German catalog keywords passes the real Kernel consistency gate and executes through both dynamic Groovy and generated Java. The discriminating own-domain include fixture covers the German `Fuer` and `Mit` reference separators, localized predicates/functions, and the accepted `Und`/`und`/`UND` plus `Oder`/`oder`/`ODER` families; its mounted rules and computation agree across both Kernel strategies and the interpreter. The broader constants, tolerance/pattern, and `@Von` language profile is a portable parser/remapper lock, not part of that real-Kernel fixture. Therefore the configured condition language is part of stored executable syntax, while display locales remain independent. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.IncludeAssemblyStrategyDiffTest.germanSingleRootMountAgreesAcrossBothKernelStrategies`, `interpreter.ConditionLanguageParseTest`, and the German catalog population in `adapter.laws.ParserCorpusSweepTest`; interpreter treatment: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF223, graduated from IG28.)*

**Include boundary:** expansion does not create a per-rule language slot; the final model is checked under its entry model's one `conditionLanguage`. A German-localized provider mounted through English intermediate and entry models is consequently rejected with `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`; the fixture establishes model-level rejection, not a notification for every retained executable. Configuration inequality alone is not illegal: the matched control uses a `de_DE` provider with language-neutral `[Note] == "x"` syntax under an `en_US` entry and passes consistency plus Java-reader parity. *(Locked as a measured refusal/control pair in `adapter.WorkspacePreparationFixtureGuardTest`; portable treatment in INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF223.)*

## §2 supplement — empty-as-0 is a *filled-row* behavior (runtime, 2026-06-06) <a id="kf17"></a>

> Fold: folded → §2.

The §2 substitutions apply inside a content-bearing row; an entirely empty instance is evaluated only when the condition **can fire empty** — a structural property (`canConditionFireEmpty`, runtime + source-audited 2026-06-23): the negative presence family fires on a blank instance, comparisons and the positive presence checks do not, `And` fires empty iff both branches do, `Or` iff either. All canonical in §2 "the row gate". *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EmptyRowGateDiffTest`; the original filled-row probe rode `[Quantity] < 100` via `IDocumentRtService.validateFull`.)*

Refinements, each canonical in §2 (or where noted):

- **An INSTANTIATED repeatable-group row is itself CONTENT** — per-row empty-as-0 fires on every created-but-blank repeat row, and on a non-repeatable ancestor whose only content is such rows; a non-repeatable group's bare structural presence is not content (runtime-probed 2026-07-06). *(IF53; locked `adapter.laws.RepeatableRowContentDiffTest`.)*
- **PARTIAL validation overrides the content gate** — a relevant instance is always evaluated, empty or phantom; the IF42 relevance gate still bounds a `*`-aggregate under a concrete relevant row (runtime-probed 2026-07-06). *(IF54; locked `RepeatableRowContentDiffTest.partialRelevantEmptyInstanceIsEvaluated`.)*
- **The type-asymmetry of the empty case** — number fires as `0`, confirm as `False`, boolean/string/date not evaluated, a string **in a `+` concatenation** evaluated as `""` — is §2's per-kind table + override table. The concatenation row is the trap an N6 run surfaced (2026-06-08): `[Full] != [First] + " " + [Last]` mis-fires on empty parts unless guarded `AllFieldsFilled(First, Last)`.
- **A bare STRING COPY computation (`X = [S]`) over an EMPTY operand CLEARS** — it does not store the concatenation's `""`; the empty-as-`""` rule is the `+`-position's only (tri-check-verified 2026-07-10, the IG56 Set-S probe; canonical §11). *(Locked `adapter.laws.StringCopyComputeDiffTest`.)*
- **Value-token representation** — a boolean stores lowercase `true`/`false` (any other token is `feldJaNeinFalsch`); a confirm accepts only `true` (`false` → `feldJaFalsch`) — canonical §1. **The token set is literally `true`/`false` for every A12 DocumentModel, including one that DECLARES its own tokens** (measured 2026-07-29 — see the correction below). The comparison is case-SENSITIVE (probe-measured 2026-07-28): `TRUE`, `FALSE`, and `True` are each `feldJaNeinFalsch` on a boolean, and a confirm's `TRUE` is `feldJaFalsch` — so "stores lowercase" is the literal contract, not a normalization the kernel performs on a stored token. Case-insensitivity lives in two *other* layers and must not be read back into this one: the JSON **ingress** converter normalizes `"TRUE"` → `true` before storage, and **evaluating** an already-stored value compares case-insensitively (`TRUE_STRING.equalsIgnoreCase`). A stored token reaches the formal check unnormalized only on the internal path, where a caller fills a document directly. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EmptyValueLawsTest` (the empty-value halves) + `adapter.laws.BooleanTokenFormalDiffTest` (the 15-row token matrix and the declared-token matrix, interpreter vs kernel — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF229); the runtime oracle is `adapter.laws.RuntimeLaws` — KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES "Runtime behavioral-law oracle".)*
