  **Corrected 2026-07-29: the tokens are NOT configurable on the A12 path — this bullet previously said they were.** Reading `FormatDefinitionJaNein.validiereFormat` (it `.equals()`-compares against `jaWert`/`neinWert`, each overridable by `DarstellungsInformationIntern.getWahrWert()`/`getFalschWert()`) supports "configurable" *locally*, and on that basis the bullet asserted lowercase was merely a "default profile" and our hard-coded check a refusal broader than its basis (INTERPRETER-GAPS IG88, withdrawn). **The discriminating case refutes it**: on a model whose `BooleanType` declares `notInDCustomTrueValue: "Y"` / `notInDCustomFalseValue: "N"` (and whose `ConfirmType` declares `"Y"`), the kernel's verdicts are byte-identical to a model declaring nothing — `true`/`false` clean, `Y`/`N` → `feldJaNeinFalsch`, confirm `Y` → `feldJaFalsch`. The keys are not ignored by the *reader* (A12's own model serializer round-trips them), so the model genuinely declares them; validation simply does not consult them. Three source links, each now confirmed by that measurement: (1) `IBooleanType.getNotInDCustomTrueValue()` is `@NotInD`, whose javadoc says the property "will be ignored while generating the validation code and therefore is not relevant for the validation"; (2) `TypeConverter.convertBoolean`/`convertConfirm` gate the declared tokens behind `considerNotInD`, emitting the literals `"true"`/`"false"` when it is false — and **every** kernel construction is the no-arg `new ExternalDocumentModelService()`, which passes `false` (the `boolean` ctor exists for an out-of-tree ExtMP caller; enumerated by grep over `../a12-kernel` `*.java`/`*.groovy` excluding `/build/` and delombok, so an external consumer could still pass `true`); (3) the runtime override lane needs a `PresentationInformation`, which `DocumentProcessingConfig` has no channel for and which the md-runtime service explicitly nulls — `LowKernelApiUtils.configureValidationMode`: *"never pass diverging number and date format here because the document values always use the canonical format"*. The model-level fallback (`metaData.getDefaultPresentationInformation()`) cannot carry the keys either: it is built from `IProjectLanguageSettings`, which has no true/false member. **The lesson is the oracle-by-claim-kind rule:** a locally correct source read of one class ("this comparison is configurable") was promoted to a reachability claim ("so a model can configure it") without attacking the links between them — and the links are where it fails. The `ExtMP` lane (`considerNotInD = true`) is the grain of truth and is outside the A12 DM-JSON contract the interpreter implements.

## §2 supplement — empty operand in a *computation* operation ⇒ no value (stale cleared) (runtime, 2026-06-08) <a id="kf18"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

A DATE-family operation over an empty operand is not evaluated: the computed field gets no value and a stale stored value is CLEARED (probed via `DateRange(OrderDate, DeliveryDate)` on `IDocumentRtService.compute`). Consequence: an `AllFieldsFilled` guard over DATE operands is **redundant** (a false precondition clears exactly as the unevaluated operation does — this settled the 2026-06-08 T13 judge split), but the redundancy is per-type — it does NOT hold for strings-in-concatenation (empty concats as `""`) or numbers (empty-as-0); canonical §11 "one operation, three outcomes" carries the full per-kind table and the guard consequence. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateRangeComputationLawsTest`, via the `RuntimeLaws.compute` oracle.)*

## §11 supplement — compute(): an out-of-range value is ERRORED; a computation reading an errored dependency is SKIPPED (runtime, 2026-06-26) <a id="kf19"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

Two compute() facts, both probe-confirmed via `IDocumentRtService.compute`:

- **An out-of-range computed value is ERRORED, value returned.** When the computed value is not valid for the target field (e.g. a number above its `maxValue`), the kernel returns the value but flags the instance in **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors()`** rather than `…WithChanges()`. So `T (maxValue 999) := [A]` with `A = 5000` yields `T = 5000`, errored. *(Interpreter: `ComputeOutcome.ERRORED`; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeErrorBucketDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF34.)*
- **A dependent of an errored computation gets NO outcome — absent when its input cell was empty, CLEARED when it was filled** (refined 2026-07-08 by the poison finding below). In the cascade, an errored dependency's value is unusable: a dependent's READ of it throws (the poison mechanism), so the dependent computes nothing. Probed: with `T` errored (above), `U := [T] + 1` and `V := [U] + 1` are **absent** from the compute result when unseeded (only the errored `T` is reported) — and a **seeded** dependent is reported **CLEARED** (input-filled, not computed). The poison is READ-driven, not a static dependency skip: a dependent whose evaluation never actually reads the errored target (a short-circuit decided first) computes normally. *(Interpreter: read-driven poisoning, reported CLEARED under its report-all contract; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeCascadeErroredDiffTest` + `ComputePoisonReadDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF40/IF85.)*

The **number** branch is confirmed for computation too (2026-06-09): a numeric copy `EffectiveFee = [BaseFee]` with **`BaseFee` empty** computes **`0`** (a value), not nothing — the empty-as-0 substitution applies in a computation operation exactly as in a comparison, so an empty *number* operand is the opposite of an empty *date* operand (which clears). **Consequence (inverts the date redundancy above):** a presence guard over a NUMBER operand is **NOT redundant** — `EffectiveFee = [BaseFee]` guarded by `FieldFilled(BaseFee)` computes **nothing** (cleared) on an empty `BaseFee`, where the unguarded form computes `0`; the two **differ**, so a candidate that adds such a guard to a number computation is **not** equivalent to the unguarded gold. (The N6 computation/modify judges carry this type split so they don't mis-equate the two.) *(Locked: `cli.ComputeDocVerbTest.anEmptyNumberOperandComputesZero` + `aGuardedNumberComputationClearsWhereUnguardedComputesZero`, via the `model compute` verb / `KernelAdapter.compute` seam.)*

A **FILLED but FORMALLY-INVALID operand CLEARS the computation** (source-consistent + differential 2026-07-08). Distinct from *empty* (a number empty-fires as 0, above — and a REQUIRED-empty operand is STILL empty-as-0, NOT cleared: `nichtAngegeben`, its MANDATORY error is a separate check): a **filled** operand whose value is formally invalid — a NUMBER with the wrong fraction-digit count (violating `min/maxFractionalDigits`) or an out-of-**range** value (`min/maxValue`) — is **not check-relevant** (`nichtPruefungsrelevant`), so `compute()` cannot evaluate an operation reading it and the computed field is **CLEARED**. The SAME operand-level "not check-relevant ⇒ unknown" rule the validation side applies (§2/§3), on the compute path. Probed: `Density = RoundDown([Inhabitants] * 1000000 / [Area], 2)` with `Area` declared `minFractionalDigits = 2` → `Area = 257.71` (2 frac) computes; `Area = 710` / `-843.9` (0 / 1 frac, formally invalid) → **CLEARED**. It is the OPERAND's validity, independent of tier selection (the minimal repro is unconditional). The kernel rule is type-agnostic, and the interpreter now mirrors it for the **full formal-error class** (IF78 generalized IF76 past the initial NUMBER value-domain subset — every number code, string length/pattern, enum-domain, custom type, over-limit), using the SAME `computeFormalErrorAt` gate validation uses; a filled-invalid string used as a **concatenation** operand likewise clears (IF81). **Downstream, this clear is a POISON, not an empty read** — the target is marked invalid and dependents' reads throw (the poison finding below, IF85). *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.compute` threads the `invalid` gate `validate` uses over FILLED operands — a formally-invalid operand → `Val.Unknown` → CLEARED; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeInvalidOperandDiffTest` + `ComputeOperandInvalidityTest` + `ConcatInvalidOperandDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF76/IF78/IF81, found via the `ModelShrinker` delta-debug of a real `_CDM` model, IG44.)*

A **precondition-CLEARED computed field propagates to a downstream computation as EMPTY** (differential 2026-07-08; boundary carved by the poison finding below): a computed field its OWN computation CLEARED **silently** (a false precondition / no matching alternative) reads as **EMPTY** (`nichtAngegeben`) in a dependent — so a dependent's `FieldFilled(dep)` is FALSE and a number operand reads empty-as-0. Source mechanism: the calc run **strips every calculated field's input value** from its working data (`CalculationCommand.getDataWithoutEntitiesNotNeededForCalculations` — *"calculations always produce a new value anyways"*), so an un-computed, un-poisoned calculated field reads empty regardless of any seeded value. An **operand-invalidity clear is NOT this case** — it marks the target invalid, and dependents' reads POISON (below). *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.compute` records CLEARED cascade targets and `OverlayContext` reads them EMPTY, not the stale stored seed; locked `adapter.laws.CascadeClearedDependencyDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF77 — boundary IF85.)*

**…and both cascade read-rules reach INSIDE a `Having` filter and a precondition's quantifiers (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** A downstream computation's `Having` filter (`Sum(/G*/share Having [/G/flag] == True)` over a computed `flag`) and a precondition's filtered quantifier read a calculated field exactly like a direct operand read: a silently-CLEARED upstream target reads **EMPTY** (the input-strip above — the filter drops the row no matter what stale value the input document carried), and a POISONED one **poisons the reading computation** (below). Differentially pinned in BOTH directions on one instance — a stale-"true" ended row is dropped AND a stale-"false" active row is kept — plus the poison and precondition-quantifier arms (groovy-dynamic == java-static). *(Interpreter: the cascade dependency order includes a `Having` filter's refs and a precondition's starred refs, and the compute overlay WRAPS the row contexts a filter iterates; locked `adapter.laws.HavingOverComputedDependencyDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … HavingOverComputedDependencyTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF98.)*

**compute POISONS on reading a formally-invalid field — the read aborts the computation instance, order-dependently, and the poison cascades (source + differential, 2026-07-08/20).** During `compute()` the kernel does NOT apply validation's unavailable-neither-bucket tally: `CalculationCache.convertInvalidFieldsForCalculation` converts formally-invalid entities so that **any read throws** (`CalculationFieldException`—a value read via `getWertXML`, a presence check via `isEntityFilled`, a quantifier scan visit, or a `Having` filter read). The generated alternative catches it, marks the instance **"fehlerhaft berechnet"** (silently—distinct from an ERRORED computed value, which also carries a message), **skips the remaining alternatives**, and produces no value—reported **CLEARED iff the input had the cell filled**, absent otherwise. The invalid marking cascades: a dependent that reads the poisoned target is poisoned too.

**The cascade is cause-blind after every computed-target boundary (SPEC-2026-07-26-04, corrected 2026-07-30).** Once a target is invalid, a later computation reading it observes only that target's invalid membership: no attempted payload, exception subtype, originating source path/cause, or producer message crosses the edge, and the generated catch silently marks the reader invalid without emitting a cascade message. An out-of-range ERRORED producer and a producer poisoned by a formally-invalid operand therefore yield the same dependent observation (CLEARED when seeded, absent when unseeded), despite their different own outcomes; a clean precondition/no-selection clear remains distinct and reads EMPTY downstream. The interpreter representation deliberately matches: `poisoned` is only the set of `(target path, repetition indices)`, `OverlayContext` consults that membership, and the computation-boundary catcher discards `PoisonedReadException.path` instead of retaining or reconstructing a cause. *(Both kernel strategies + interpreter locked by `adapter.laws.ComputedDependencyCauseErasureDiffTest`; JVM/JS outcome lock `interpreter … ComputedDependencyCauseErasureTest`.)*

Because poison is read-driven, evaluation order decides. The fill helpers stop at their operator-specific cell: `AtLeastOneFieldFilled` and `NoFieldFilled` at the first filled, `AllFieldsFilled` and `NotAllFieldsFilled` at the first empty over the declared range, and `MoreThanOneFieldFilled` at the second filled. `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` first runs the no-filled scan and, after a filled witness, **restarts from the beginning** for the more-than-one scan. `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` first runs the instantiated at-least-one scan and, after a filled witness, **restarts from the beginning** for the declared-range not-all scan. Each stage stops independently; the composites remain at most two linear passes. A filtered-star candidate that passes `Having` is then a normal cell read and receives the same complete formal classification as an unfiltered candidate. Value aggregates feed all selected rows to their combiner, generated `And` short-circuits a clean FALSE/UNKNOWN left, `Or` short-circuits a clean TRUE left, a poisoned left aborts either, and `Having` keeps only TRUE rows. **Both strategies agree.** *(IF85/IF188; locks: `ComputePoisonReadDiffTest`, `BareGroupComputeExactnessDiffTest`, `ComputationFillQuantifierRestartDiffTest`, `ComputationFillQuantifierScanContractTest`, `FilteredStarComputeFormalInvalidityTest`, and `ComputePoisonTest`.)*

A **computed NUMBER stores PADDED to the target's `minFractionalDigits` — and BOTH compute strategies render identically** (source + probe, 2026-07-08; **locus corrected 2026-07-09**: the store formatter is `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkBigDecimal, id)` — shared runtime code the codegen templates call — NOT `NumberConversion.determineFractionalDigits`, whose 15-digit render cap the compute store path never applies; the two rules agree on every ≤ 15-digit value, which is why the original 13-case probe couldn't tell them apart — the store-path finding below is where they part). The fit-arm rule is `destFractionalDigits = max(stripTrailingZeros().scale(), minFractionalDigits)`: a value `10` into a `minFractionalDigits`-2 field stores **`10.00`**, `5.5` → **`5.50`**, `0` → **`0.00`**; a field with **no** `minFractionalDigits` (0) stores the canonical **`10`**/`5.5`/`0`, and a value with more natural fractional digits than the min keeps them. **Both compute strategies agree** — the legacy V1 `compute(IDocument)` AND the modern V2 `compute(DocumentV2, config)` (the corpus capture/replay path) ride that one controller and render bit-identically (13-case probe: legacy == modern every time). This **disproves the IG53-filed hypothesis** that "the two kernel render paths disagree on trailing zeros." Corollary: a computed whole number into a `minFractionalDigits`≥1 field is a **VALUE** (padded → valid), not ERRORED. *(Interpreter: `toComputed` renders `Val.Num` via the `storedComputedNumber` store rule; locked `adapter.laws.ComputedNumberScaleDiffTest` — a QUAD-check pinning legacy == modern == java-static == interpreter; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF82, refined by IF92.)*

**The computed-NUMBER store path in full — scale-19 pre-round, an UNCAPPED fit render, a length-bounded no-fit arm; the stored form is never re-rounded to fit the 15-digit check** (source + quad-differential, 2026-07-09). `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkBigDecimal, id)` — both strategies — does, in order: **(1)** `setScale(19, HALF_UP)` (`VkBigDecimal.DEFAULT_SCALE` — observable only for a quotient/product whose scale exceeds 19); **(2)** if the value's natural (stripped) scale **fits** the target's `maxFractionalDigits`: render at `max(naturalScale, minFractionalDigits)` with **NO length cap**, run the formal check on that string (`checkField`), and store it — so a computed `RoundAccounting(product-of-many, 2)` worth `354027231847478.01` (17 digits) stores in FULL, trips `stelligkeitZuLang` (>15 digits, sign excluded), and the cell is **ERRORED with the full value** (`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors` carries it); **(3)** if the natural scale does **not fit** (`minScale > maxNachkommaMeta`): render length-bounded to **16 significant digits** (`BigDecimalUtils.toStringWertLaengenBeschraenkt(v19, 16)` — ≤ 16 significant digits → canonical; longer → `setScale(max(0, 16 − integerDigits), HALF_UP)`), store it, and mark **"fehlerhaft berechnet" unconditionally** (ERRORED even when the bounded string is formally valid — e.g. a scale-4 product `2.4723` into a maxFrac-2 target). The no-fit arm is reachable on a legal model **only** through the computation's **`errorCodesToSuppress: ["MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES"]`** (`IComputation.getErrorCodesToSuppress` — the kernel's own model-level escape hatch for the static assignment-scale gate); without it the codegen REJECTS a scale-mismatched assignment — the gate is an **ERROR** for computations, riding the implicit `[target] != <op>` comparison check (differentially pinned: `[A]*[B]` of scale-2 operands has static scale **4**, a bare division static scale **"?"**, both rejected into a scale-2 target). *(Interpreter: `storedComputedNumber` (commonMain) reproduces all three steps and `toComputed` reports a no-fit cell ERRORED into the IF85 poison set; locked `adapter.laws.ComputedNumberDigitOverflowDiffTest` — a QUAD-check incl. the suppressed no-fit arm and the rejected-without-suppression control — and multiplatform `interpreter … ComputedNumberDigitOverflowTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF92.)*

## §11 supplement — TIERED computations: alternatives are selected in order by precondition; `commonPrecondition` gates the whole (source + probe-verified 2026-06-25) <a id="kf20"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

A computation is a **list of `computationAlternatives`**, each an optional `precondition` + an `operation` (the value expression), optionally under a whole-computation `commonPrecondition`. The kernel codegen (`CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st`) emits, per alternative in order, `if (<precondition>) { handleValue(<operation>); continue; }` — so the runtime **evaluates alternatives top-to-bottom and the FIRST whose precondition holds supplies the value**; an alternative with no precondition always applies (an unconditional default); if **none** holds the field is **cleared** (computes nothing). A `commonPrecondition` guards the whole computation (false ⇒ cleared). This is the kernel's "tiered computation" feature.

**Probe-verified (2026-06-25)** — a `UnitPrice` computation with `[{precondition: "[Quantity] < 100", operation: "9.99"}, {precondition: "[Quantity] >= 100", operation: "0"}]`: `Quantity = 50` → `UnitPrice = 9.99` (alt 1), `Quantity = 150` → `UnitPrice = 0` (alt 2). So the precondition genuinely *selects* the operation — the discriminating-precondition case is NOT the redundant-guard equivalence above (§66, which is the narrow degenerate case of a single fill-style guard that empty-as-skip already subsumes). A precondition over an empty operand does not select its branch (it is not three-valued TRUE); a gap where **no** alternative's precondition holds → the field is **cleared** (probe-verified 2026-06-26).

**Mutual EXCLUSIVITY of tiered preconditions is a documentation-level contract — NO consistency-tier diagnostic flags an overlap (probe-verified 2026-07-10, the IG56 Set-S probe).** `checkConsistency` over a two-tier computation with OVERLAPPING preconditions (`[BaseFee] > 0` / `[BaseFee] > -100`) and even byte-IDENTICAL preconditions (`> 0` twice) emits an EMPTY diagnostic set — indistinguishable from the exclusive control. The BA's "mutually exclusive" requirement is enforced by no tier; the runtime resolves an overlap by the first-declared-wins selection above. *(Locked: `validate.laws.ComputationTierExclusivityLawsTest`.)*

**`MVK_ERROR_EMPTY_PRECONDITION` — an empty precondition is allowed only as the SOLE alternative (codegen-verified 2026-06-26).** A trailing **unconditional** alternative mixed with guarded ones is rejected at codegen — "Empty prerequisite is not allowed here." So a *tiered* computation's alternatives all carry preconditions; an unconditional operation is the ordinary **non-tiered** computation (one alternative, no precondition). The "default after guards" shape is therefore unauthorable — no consumer (or the interpreter) ever has to evaluate it. The **scale gate** also applies to an operation's assignment: a field operand whose derived scale is incompatible with the computed field draws `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`; a literal succeeds only where its signed scale/capability summary is compatible (e.g. scale-2 `9.99` matches directly and capable scale-0 `0` may pad to scale 2), so any other operation must match the target or round explicitly.

The **interpreter models this in full** (fixed 2026-06-26, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF21): `ComputationDef` carries the alternative list + `commonPrecondition`, and `DmInterpreter.compute` selects the first alternative whose precondition is 3VL-TRUE (null = unconditional), clearing the field when the common precondition fails or no alternative holds — matching the codegen selection above. dmtool's *authoring* side (rulekit `DraftComputation`, `computation add`) always supported it, so the earlier cross-stack divergence (authored-but-mis-evaluated) is closed. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputationPreconditionDiffTest` — tiered low/high select, no-match→cleared, common-precondition true/false, all interp == kernel.)*

**A SEMANTIC-INDEX lookup in a computation poisons on an INVALID index COLUMN; a clean column keeps the validation read's empty-as-0 (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** `Result = [Items/Price For LookupKey]` (a real-workspace idiom): with ANY invalid cell in the index column — a MALFORMED value (a 16-digit `stelligkeitZuLang`), a DUPLICATED key pair, or an EMPTY key cell — the kernel marks every reading computation "incorrectly calculated" (CLEARED), **even when a clean row matches the key** (the IndexFieldCache marking — the semantic-index analog of the §9/§11 parallel-iteration invalid-index rule). Over a CLEAN column the compute read equals the validation read: a no-match key and a matched-but-EMPTY target cell both read **empty-as-0** (`VALUE|0.00` into a min-frac-2 target), never cleared. Groovy-dynamic == java-static across all arms. *(Interpreter: `evalSemanticIndex` poisons compute-mode reads on an invalid column; the empty-as-0 no-match had been correct all along; locked `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … SemanticIndexComputeInvalidColumnTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF107.)*

**A matched formally-invalid indexed cell poisons the reading computation — per READ CELL, not per row (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** Over a CLEAN index column, a `[F For key]` compute read whose MATCHED cell is formally invalid (wrong scale, too long, …) clears the reading computation — a direct operand and a `FieldValueAsString(ref For key)` coercion alike, and a concatenation cannot rescue it as `""` (the same not-check-relevant read rule as a direct invalid field operand). The granularity is the **cell actually read**: a sibling computation reading a CLEAN cell of the same matched row stays VALUE, and an invalid target cell in an UNMATCHED row (never read) poisons nothing. The IF107 column marking also reaches a standalone `FieldValueAsString(… For …)` read — every indexed read goes through the same cache. *(Interpreter: one shared compute-guarded read for both sites; locked `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexMatchedInvalidCellDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF108.)*

**VALIDATION-side indexed reads are not-check-relevant PER LOOKUP, not per column (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10 — the validation counterpart of the compute column marking above).** A `[F For key]` read in a RULE (incl. the implicit computation-validation): a match on a CLEAN key cell resolves the read regardless of the other cells' states — a malformed key cell elsewhere does NOT suppress (where a computation would clear); a match on a DUPLICATED key cell is not-check-relevant (uniqueIndex marks both cells); a NO-match over a column carrying ANY not-check-relevant cell — malformed (a maybe-match), EMPTY (auto-required), duplicated — is unresolvable → np → the referencing rule is suppressed (the flag ORs through a concatenation, `VkString.add` → `isStringNotRelevant`); a CLEAN no-match stays the NOT-GIVEN read (na) — a referencing concat fires with `""` parts and OMISSION polarity. *(Interpreter: the shared guarded read's validation branch + the concat np gate; locked `adapter.laws.IndexedReadSuppressionDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF115.)*

**SEMANTIC-INDEX key identity is owned by the DECLARED INDEX-FIELD KIND — Number normalizes numerically, every other kind is exact stored text (source + differential, both strategies, SPEC-2026-07-23-08).** The kernel's `IndexFieldCache.normalizeValue` re-renders a key (both the admitted stored key AND the requested literal-or-field-valued key) through `NumberUtil.createBigDecimal` + `convertFromBasicType` **only** when the index field is a `FormatDefinitionZahl` (Number); every other format returns the raw XML token unchanged. So on a Number index field `5` and `5.00` select the same row, while a String index field keeps `5` and `5.00` DISTINCT. This projection is not the admission gate: a stored key first has to enter the call's index cache and pass built-in/custom formal validation. An over-limit row is outside that cache and neither keys nor compromises. An in-cache malformed token such as `05` or a partial-call non-relevant cell contributes no identity even when its raw text parses to the requested decimal and does compromise an unresolved lookup to UNKNOWN. Fixed in the interpreter as a lifecycle: call-local admission, one canonical projection, one ambiguity resolver, then selection/3VL/pointer.

*Corrected in place on 2026-08-09.* The first correction established that the same `normalizeValue` identity governs the parallel join, auto `uniqueIndex`, and computation invalid-index detection, not only the semantic index. The follow-up review showed that canonical projection was still one stage too late: raw placements entered several identity consumers before formal admission, semantic presence used a first-placement shortcut, the contained-field scan and error pointer were not tied to the selected row, registered custom rejection was absent from the typed starred column, and entity-valued starred value lists read raw cells. The separating rows are `05 -> 10` then `5 -> 20` (select admitted row 2), `05` plus `5` plus `5.00` (the admitted peers still collide), a registered custom-invalid key (project formal error remains; measured semantic presence, starred fill/count, and entity-valued value-list stay silent; callback once), a non-relevant matching key under partial validation (`Filled` and `NotFilled` both silent), an over-limit matching row (`FieldNotFilled` fires at `[-5]`, proving clean exclusion), and a clean no-match error pointer (`[-5]`). Pointer target identity is authored syntax after filtering to refs that target the repeated error field: sibling payload refs are irrelevant, while `"5"` and `"5.00"` remain two pointer targets even though they are one runtime Number key. Two distinct field-key expressions likewise stay distinct when their current values select one row. A semantic target may be below the indexed group: the key selects the nearest indexed ancestor row, while coordinates through the authored target group remain independently iterated and appear in the error pointer. Levels below that authored target default to row 1. When one exact key target appears through several error-containing selectors, the deepest selector wins independently of operand order; this separates an ancestor-group target, a descendant-group target, and an exact error-field target over the same selected indexed row. An ordinary message reference to the repeated error field, directly or through a containing group, vetoes semantic pointer reanchoring entirely, so matching and no-match semantic group operands remain value reads over every physical error-field context. A semantic computation resolves both a field-valued query key and its selected scalar/group payload below an indexed ancestor through earlier cascade results, never the raw absent cells. *(Interpreter: `ValueAdmissionCache`, `indexKeyOf`, `resolveIndexIdentity`, `nearestIndexFieldOf`, admission-aware `IndexColumn`, error-field target plan, overlay query/payload reads; locked by `NumericIndexKeyDiffTest`, `FirstFilledValueEntitySpecDiffTest`, `IndexIdentityCustomTypeDiffTest`, `SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest`, `IndexIdentityStagePropertyTest`, and `CustomFieldTypeMultiplatformTest`; [IF272](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if272).)*

**`FieldValueAsString` of a NOT-GIVEN read behaves per context — a standalone computation CLEARS, a concatenation gets `""` (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** The coercion of an empty/absent source (a plain empty field, a no-match key, a matched-but-empty indexed cell) is a not-given string, not a storable `""`: `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkString)` returns early on an empty value ("do like no calculation was done") → the standalone copy computes nothing (CLEARED — the generalization of the §11 bare-string-copy law to the coercion), while a `+`-concatenation maps it to `""` (the IF81 empty rule) and a comparison is suppressed. A formally-INVALID source read through the coercion poisons the computation (standalone and in-concat alike). **The operand must be a NUMBER/AMOUNT field** — `FieldValueAsString(stringField)` is model-rejected with `MVK_NO_NUMBER` ("Only numbers and amounts are allowed"; a string copies bare `[S]`). *(Locked `adapter.laws.FieldValueAsStringComputeDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF109.)*

**`FirstFilledValue` consumes its selection only UNTIL the first FILLED cell — an invalid cell poisons the computation only BEFORE it (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** `FirstValueCombiner.addValue` ignores every add once the first filled value is captured: an invalid TAIL cell (e.g. a leading-zero number after a filled row) is invisible — the computation stores the first value (`VALUE`), it does not clear. An invalid cell BEFORE the first given one sets `nonRelevantFieldBeforeFirstGiven` → the non-relevant result → CLEARED (also when an empty row precedes the invalid one); an empty cell before the first given sets only the displaceability flag, never a poison. This makes `FirstFilledValue` the one stop-at-first value combiner — the "any invalid selected cell poisons the computation" rule holds for the consume-every-row combiners (Sum/Min/Max/NumberOfDifferentValues fold each row). *(Interpreter: a compute-mode ordered scan in `firstFilledValue`; locked `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueInvalidTailDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF111.)*

**Computed values are checked with the BASIC format check — no charset / leading-blank / trailing-blank baseline (source-confirmed both strategies, 2026-07-10).** A computed result is bucketed "fehlerhaft berechnet" by `CalculationController.checkField` → `FormalChecker.checkFieldValueFormatBasic`, which runs ONLY `IFormatDefinition.validiereFormat` (type format + declared constraints). The baseline legal-charset / leading-blank / trailing-blank checks belong to the full `checkFieldValueFormat` that stored INPUT values get — so a computed string that starts or ends with a blank (e.g. an empty operand concatenated before a literal, `" G"`) stores as a plain VALUE. Both strategies share the one `CalculationController` store path. *(Interpreter: `computedOutcome` runs the extracted `typeFormatError` (Basic) instead of the full `formalError`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF110.)*

**The root store takes a SECOND decision on the FINAL result value — a final EMPTY String text stores NO value, whatever expression produced it (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-15; feedback F10 → IG69 → IF123).** `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkString value, id)` — shared runtime code BOTH strategies' generated alternatives call (`CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st`, groovy and java, emit the same `controller.handleBerechnetenWert(<calcFunctionCode>, id)`) — returns **before** the format check and the store when `value.isNichtPruefungsrelevant() || !value.hasValue()`, with `VkString.hasValue()` = non-empty text: *"for empty values, do like no calculation was done."* So an **all-empty concatenation** (`[A] + [B]`, both clean-empty → the parts substitute `""` per IF81 and the result reaches the root store as empty text) computes **nothing** — the same no-value decision as the bare empty copy (§2 refinement) and the `FieldValueAsString` coercion (above); the §2 "empty-as-`""` is the `+`-position's only" names the *substitution* rule inside the expression, not a storable final `""`. The gate is **per result type**: `VkDate`/`VkDateRange` no-value on a null date (an unevaluated date op — already "computes nothing"), while `VkBigDecimal` has NO empty arm (a number is empty-as-0 → a real `0`; its gate is `wertIstUngueltig` → the calc error). Consequences, each differentially pinned: **(1)** the empty decision **precedes `checkField`** — an empty final text into a `minLength` target CLEARS, never ERRORED (the kernel never checks a value it does not store); **(2)** a dependent reads the target EMPTY (nothing enters the calc cache) — *corrected 2026-07-29*: this consequence originally added "and a later computation targeting the same cell can still win", inherited from the then-current IF87 reading rather than measured. Both kernel strategies say otherwise for the shape this entry owns: an unguarded all-empty concatenation IS a **selected** alternative, so it ends the flattened per-target scan and a later computation never stores. The fall-through survives only for an **unselected** computation (see §11 "Multiple SEPARATE computations…", corrected the same day); **(3)** the delta granularity applies **afterward** (a stale-filled target reports CLEARED, an already-empty/absent one reports nothing); **(4)** poison is separate — compute-path reads of invalid cells THROW (IF85) and an ERRORED computed value stores WITH its error mark, so neither is swallowed by the empty gate. *(Interpreter: `toComputed` converts a final empty rendered TEXT to the no-value CLEARED decision before the format check, and the cascade bookkeeping keys on the OUTCOME — an empty result is a not-decided tentative clear; locked `adapter.laws.EmptyConcatStoreDiffTest` (tri-check: the receipt's three divergent shapes + controls + the minLength gate-order arm, raw AND `kernelComputeGranularity`-projected) + multiplatform `interpreter … EmptyStringComputationStoreTest` + the string-concat compute-fuzz family `ComputationFuzz.stringModel` (mutation-checked: the gate disabled diverges on 17/200 (model, doc) pairs); INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF123, from the a12-kernel-lean receipt `string-computation-2026-07-15`.)*

**Multiple SEPARATE computations can target ONE field (composed models only) — they FLATTEN into one first-match table and the first SELECTED alternative ends the scan, in document order (source + differential 2026-07-08; *corrected 2026-07-29*).** Distinct from the *tiered* case above (one computation, many alternatives): a single flat model **REJECTS** two computations for one field (codegen error — differentially confirmed). But model **COMPOSITION** (includes / subtypes — a base calc + a subtype override living in *different groups*) yields a field with >1 computation, which the kernel accepts and evaluates. **The mechanism is SELECTION, not storage** — this is the 2026-07-29 correction, from a12-kernel-lean SPEC-2026-07-26-03. `CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder` distributes each computation's common precondition into its alternatives and **concatenates every computation targeting one field, in supplied (document) order, into a single per-field first-match method**; `CodeGenCalculationAlternative` ends the target-instance scan once an alternative is selected. So the deciding event is that an alternative's guard held — **not** that it produced a storable value. `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert`'s *"for empty values, do like no calculation was done"* is real but sits one layer lower: it governs whether the selected result is STORED, and cannot resurrect the scan.

The two accounts agree everywhere the earlier differential looked, because every empty-first case there was empty through a FALSE GUARD, i.e. never selected. The separating shape needs the first computation **unguarded** with an operation that yields no storable value — only a STRING target can show it (`VkString.hasValue()` is non-empty text; a number renders empty-as-0 and stores a real 0). Measured on **both** kernel strategies: a first computation that bare-copies an empty String, or concatenates two empty Strings, leaves the target **CLEARED** even though a second unguarded computation supplies a nonempty literal. The false-guard fall-through survives unchanged as the opposite control, as does value-first and poison-first. *(Interpreter: `evalComputation` now returns a `Selection(selected, value)` and `computeAt` decides the cell whenever an alternative was selected; a CLEARED outcome falls through only when nothing was selected. Locked `adapter.laws.MultiComputationFieldDiffTest` (10 tri-checked cases) + `interpreter … MultiComputationFieldTest` (JVM+Node), INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF87/IF171.)* The earlier reading — "the first computation that yields a NON-EMPTY outcome wins" — is superseded; it over-reported, letting a later computation overwrite a selected-but-empty earlier result.

**A computation is scoped to its COMPUTED FIELD, and a non-repeatable group is implicitly present (source + differential 2026-07-09).** A computation iterates over its **computed field's** repetition scope — where it *writes*, not the group it is *declared* in (a `*_Rules`/subtype container group can declare a computation whose `computedFieldRelPath` reaches into a different subtree). When the computed field has **no repeatable ancestor** (an all-non-repeatable target), the kernel computes exactly **one** instance — and it does so **regardless of instantiation**, because a non-repeatable group is always present (index 1). Differentially confirmed in BOTH strategies (groovy-dynamic + java-static): a constant computation declared in an uninstantiated container group targeting an all-non-repeatable field in another subtree computes its single value on an instantiated document AND on an empty one. *(Interpreter: the compute scope anchors to the target's parent group and `Document.computeContexts` yields the implicit single context for an all-non-repeatable uninstantiated scope — compute-local; locked `adapter.laws.CrossGroupConstantComputeScopeDiffTest` + `interpreter … CrossGroupConstantComputeScopeTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF89.)*

## §11 supplement — a single-alternative `commonPrecondition` reads back FOLDED onto the alternative (probe + source-verified 2026-07-17) <a id="kf21"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — a stored-form/read-back representation fact (the kernel's normalized computation form), outside the canonical doc's evaluation scope.

The DM-JSON `commonPrecondition` key survives the WRITE (the model file carries it verbatim), but the kernel stores a computation as ONE rule condition in its "normalized form" and re-derives the common/per-alternative split on extraction (`CalculationService.extractCalculationInfo` → `NormalisierteBerechnungsFormHelper`, whose per-alternative precondition parts absorb the common parts — mechanism read from source, no expression copied). With a SINGLE unconditional alternative the split has no structural anchor, so the gate reads back as that alternative's own precondition: writing `{commonPrecondition: "[Subtotal] > 0", alternatives: [{operation: "[BaseShipping]"}]}` reads back `commonPrecondition = null` + one alternative `{precondition: "[Subtotal] > 0", operation: "[BaseShipping]"}`. **Semantically identical** (a whole-computation gate over one unconditional alternative ≡ that alternative guarded by the gate — both compute iff the gate holds, else cleared, per the tiered selection semantics above), so `commonPrecondition` survives a round-trip **distinctly only with ≥2 alternatives**. Consumer-visible wherever read-back serves: `computation read`, `model report` (MODEL-REVIEW-SPEC §4a). *(Locked: `adapter.ComputationCommonPreconditionFoldTest`; probe + source read, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-17.)*

## §11 supplement — compute reporting granularity: the RESULT has five channels; VALUE/CLEARED/ERRORED is the three-channel CHANGE PROJECTION we select (source + dual-strategy differential, 2026-07-08; Number and Time identity corrected 2026-07-30, SPEC-2026-07-26-05 / SPEC-2026-07-29-02) <a id="kf22"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

The kernel's `IDocumentComputationResult` is a **full result with five channels**, not a delta — the change view below is our *projection* of it (source: `IDocumentComputationResult` + `DocumentComputationResultImpl.init`; probe-verified on the modern `compute(DocumentV2, config)` path):

- **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithoutErrors`** — every successful (formally-clean, non-cleared) instance, **including unchanged successes**;
- **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithChanges`** — the **subset** of the above whose value differs from the input `DocumentV2` (`!fieldValuesEqual`);
- **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors`** — the erroneous instances;
- **`getClearedFieldInstances`** — instances filled in the input but not (re)computed: explicitly cleared, cascade-cleared, or cleared because of formal operand errors;
- **`getFormalErrorsInOperands`** — operand-level formal errors (`IMessage`), attached to no computed instance;
- and **`noErrorOccurred() == getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors().isEmpty() && getFormalErrorsInOperands().isEmpty()`** (channels 3 and 5 both empty).

**Our conformance corpus, adapter differential, and `applyTo` pipeline intentionally select the three-channel CHANGE PROJECTION** — `WithChanges → VALUE`, `getClearedFieldInstances → CLEARED`, `WithErrors → ERRORED` — because a report-all engine (the interpreter) is compared against a kernel capture, and `applyTo` writes the changed values and clears the errored/cleared ones. The projection's per-kind subtleties (unchanged by this correction):

- **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithChanges` (→ VALUE)** — only instances whose computed value **differs from the input document's value at that cell**, compared as **V2 objects**: `fieldValuesEqual(originalV2Value, computedV2Value)` delegates ordinary values to `Objects.equals` (with a `Date[]` array special case). On a **placement/harness-built** instance the cell values are raw *strings*, so equality can hold only for a STRING-kind computed target: a Number computes to `BigDecimal`, and even equal-looking source text `"7.00"` remains a change. On a typed source, `BigDecimal.equals` requires the same coefficient **and scale**: computed `7.00` is unchanged against source decimal `7.00`, but changed against source decimal `7`. A negative-scale source decimal is also always changed when the computed Number has the same plain rendering and amount, because computation reconstructs the result from its plain stored text and does not retain that negative source scale. These four separators agree on dynamic Groovy and generated Java *(locked by `NumberResultSourceIdentityLawsTest`; source owner: `DocumentComputationResultImpl` → `FieldInstanceImpl.fieldValuesEqual`)*. This resolves the IG44-era granularity probe: its seed-equal Number was reported because a Number never types-equals the harness's source String, not because numeric amount equality is the change rule.
- **TIME identity is a measured exception to the intuitive same-text control.** A clean computed `10:30:45` appears in `getComputedFieldInstancesWithChanges` whether the source Time contains `09:00:00` or the identical `10:30:45`; dynamic Groovy and generated Java agree. This is an observed public-result classification, not a causation claim: source inspection reaches `DocumentComputationResultImpl` → `FieldInstanceImpl.fieldValuesEqual`, but the exact separator that makes the typed Time objects unequal has not been discriminated. The behavior is settled without guessing that mechanism *(locked by `ComputedTimeTargetDiffTest.evenTheSameClockIsSourceClassifiedAsChangedAndApplied`)*.
- **`getClearedFieldInstances` (→ CLEARED)** — only instances that were **filled in the input document** (`getFilledComputedFieldInstances`, `valueExists`): clearing an already-empty computed cell reports nothing.
- **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors` (→ ERRORED)** — unconditional (not change-gated).

Those classifications are fixed **against the computation source**. `applyTo(DocumentV2)` may receive a separate model-compatible destination and consumes the existing classifications without recomputing equality against that destination: an unchanged successful source instance is not written even when the destination contains another value, while a source-classified change is applied. The same-clock TIME exception therefore overwrites a different destination clock because the source result was already classified changed. Validation remains a separate operation after application. *(Locked: `ComputeResultChannelsLawsTest.applicationUsesSourceRelativeClassificationsAgainstACompatibleDestination` + `ComputedTimeTargetDiffTest.evenTheSameClockIsSourceClassifiedAsChangedAndApplied`.)*

**Consequence for us:** `DmInterpreter.compute` and public `ComputationReport.results` deliberately report **every** computed instance (report-all — the more useful observation contract; result-envelope granularity is not behavior-parity territory, the IG47 BEST-gate ruling), so **every comparison surface** that diffs report-all results against a kernel change report projects the interpreter side first — `interpreter.conformance.kernelComputeGranularity`, used by the corpus capture/replay (JVM + JS), the real-corpus sweep, the model shrinker, and the compute fuzz oracle. Its input is the current harness's deliberate V1/String placement profile, where every Number VALUE remains changed; it must not be generalized into the typed-`DocumentV2` rule above. Public `ComputationReport.actions` is the separately maintained source-relative application subset: it uses typed Number coefficient-plus-scale identity and retains the measured source-identical Time action. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputeResultChannelsLawsTest` (the five channels, the `WithChanges ⊆ WithoutErrors` subset, and the `noErrorOccurred` predicate) + `NumberResultSourceIdentityLawsTest` (the typed Number identity matrix on both kernel strategies) + `ComputedTimeTargetDiffTest` (the Time-specific identity and application matrix) + `SeparateDestinationClearDiffTest` (both kernel strategies plus the standalone interpreter retained route) + multiplatform `RetainedComputationResultTest`/`ComputedTimeTargetTest` + `interpreter … conformance.ComputeGranularityTest` (JVM+JS) + `adapter.laws.CorpusCaptureTest` (string vs number seed-equal capture) + the committed corpus guard case `compute/order-name-seed-equal` (empty `computeExpected` must PASS). Contract: CONFORMANCE-CORPUS-SPEC §7.)*

## §11 supplement — compute eagerly formal-validates operands, WITHOUT a global abort (source + differential, 2026-07-22, SPEC-2026-07-22-12) <a id="kf23"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

Before scheduling, `IDocumentRtService.compute` runs an **eager prepass** over every non-computed field that is an OPERAND of a computation (source: the `compute` javadoc + `DocumentComputationResultImpl.init`):

- an operand holding an **invalid value** → reported in `getFormalErrorsInOperands`;
- a **required-EMPTY** operand → **NOT reported** (silent — mandatoriness is validation's concern, not compute's, and an empty numeric operand still reads empty-as-0 for its consumer);
- an **empty or non-unique INDEX** field in the operand set → reported.

Then, **regardless of the collected errors, computation processing continues** — there is no global abort. In the focused direct-dependency witness, the consumer of the invalid operand is CLEARED while an unrelated computation produces its VALUE. That observation does **not** make the eager inventory a general scheduling rule: exact operand-set extraction and whether a later computation skips or poisons remain dependency-, schedule-, and reached-read-sensitive, owned by the separate poison findings above. Finally, a represented **erroneous computed instance is ERRORED only**: `init` removes its pointer from the source-filled residual *before* the cleared branch, so it lands in `getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors` and **never also** in `getClearedFieldInstances`; only remaining source-filled instances clear. `noErrorOccurred` is true exactly when the errored and formal-operand channels are both empty (the §11 five-channel supplement above).

**Consequence for us:** the interpreter's public `compute` keeps its report-all outcomes and also surfaces the eager operand-error inventory as `ComputationReport.formalErrorsInOperands`. The direct consumer is CLEARED, an unrelated computation still produces a VALUE, and each represented instance carries a single outcome (ERRORED, never also CLEARED). The SPEC-2026-07-23-15 supplement below sharpens the index-field half (which indexes the inventory SELECTS, the shared default staging, and the preliminary index poison the -15 differential exposed on the interpreter). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputationEagerPrepassLawsTest` — invalid-operand reporting + direct-consumer classification + continued processing + interpreter parity, required-empty silence, empty/non-unique index reporting, and errored-not-cleared; `adapter.laws.PublicComputationDiagnosticsDiffTest` — the public result projection; the five channels themselves by `adapter.laws.ComputeResultChannelsLawsTest`.)*

## §11 supplement — computation SELECTS operand index fields (direct + induced) and applies full validation's index-default staging (source + dual-strategy differential, 2026-07-23, SPEC-2026-07-23-15) <a id="kf24"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

In an expanded A12 model, computation's eager formal-input inventory is a **checked-plan union across all computations** — directly referenced fields; every field contained by an **ordinarily referenced group** (e.g. a group predicate in a common/alternative precondition) or by a conditionally referenced ancestor group; and the **index fields required by semantic-index or parallel-iteration use** — traversing operations, preconditions, filters, and semantic-index key expressions, with computed fields then excluded from formal input checking (`CalculationOperandsCollector` → `CalculationDependencies.getExpandedOperandFieldsOfCalculations`; `CalculationCommand` filters the ingested data to that set). The generated **preliminary mandatory/uniqueness rules run ONLY for index fields retained by the inventory** (`CalculationCommand.preliminaryValidationOfIndexFields`), so:

- an **unused** bad index (its group untouched by every computation) is **silent** — no `formalErrorsInOperands` entry;
- a **selected** empty/duplicate index reports `mandatoryField`/`uniqueIndex` through `formalErrorsInOperands` — and the selection is broader than a read trace: **iterating an index group selects its index** even when no operand references it (a `[Qty] * 2` computation inside the indexed Items group still requires Sku — the target-scope iteration IS the parallel-iteration machinery), a group reference in a precondition selects the group's contained index, semantic-index and cross-group parallel-iteration references select the joined indexes, and a `Having`-filter-only reference selects too (differentially confirmed, both strategies);
- formal ingestion applies **the SAME full-call index-default staging as full validation** (the SPEC-2026-07-23-14 §12 supplement): an eligible clean-empty enum index receives its declared S-value BEFORE the preliminary rules, so it draws no `mandatoryField` and its consumer computes on the token (`[Sku]` echoes `STD`); an ineligible shape (multi-row, admitted explicit, mandatory) keeps its ordinary prepass error;
- the prepass-invalidated index cell **poisons a computation that READS it** (`MainValidatorController.preliminaryError` → `markFieldAsInvalidForValidation` → `convertInvalidFieldsForCalculation`): the kernel's change projection is silent for the never-source-filled consumer, the report-all interpreter documents the same instance as CLEARED — never a VALUE computed from an empty-as-"" or duplicated read. An ordinary required-empty operand stays empty-as-0 (the entry-12 silence above).

The inventory is neither a runtime read trace nor a dependency schedule — whether an invalid producer poisons a dependent remains decided by the reached reads (the poison findings above). The legacy unique-entity/H-model path (all-noncomputed input, early abort) is outside this contract.

**Interpreter reconciliation (SPEC-2026-07-23-15, public projection completed by IF241):** compute stages the same eligible index defaults as full validation (call-local, never mutating the document), and the compute path's formal gate carries the kernel's preliminary index invalidation — a DUPLICATED index cell is invalid for a reading computation, and an EMPTY index cell poisons its reader (unlike an ordinary required-empty field) — the class the -15 differential exposed (two sibling instances: empty and duplicate; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS). `ComputationReport.formalErrorsInOperands` now publishes the eager inventory without changing the report-all outcome projection. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputationEagerPrepassLawsTest` — the SPEC-15 arms: selected-vs-unused, iteration/group-ref/semantic-index/parallel-iteration/filter induction, absent + present-empty default staging with value parity, ineligible multi-row/admitted-explicit controls, channel separation, and the empty/duplicate poison projections — dynamic-Groovy · static-Java · interpreter; `adapter.laws.PublicComputationDiagnosticsDiffTest` — public channel parity; `interpreter.ComputationIndexDefaultMultiplatformTest` — the pure engine on JVM + Node.)*

## §9/§11 supplement — computations PARALLEL-ITERATE over sibling indexed groups; an invalid index anywhere marks every instance incorrectly calculated (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-09) <a id="kf25"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

A computation whose operands reference OUT-OF-SCOPE sibling repeatable groups (all joined groups carrying the same-named INDEX field) runs the §9 parallel iteration: the target row keyed K reads each sibling's row with the SAME key — join-by-key, never physical row position (differentially pinned with scrambled row orders). An **unmatched side reads EMPTY** (empty-as-0 for a missing key row and for a wholly-uninstantiated side group — the calc still computes). Two calc-specific rules: the calc **never creates a repetition** (`CalculationController.isWiederholungGesetzt` — "no additional repetitions should be created by calculation"; a side-only key computes nothing), and an **INVALID index in ANY joined group** (a duplicated or empty key cell, target or side) marks **EVERY instance** of the computation "incorrectly calculated" — `CalculationCache.markFieldsUsingInvalidIndexGroupsAsIncorrectlyCalculated`, deliberately *"marking more field instances as invalid than strictly necessary"* — so even clean-keyed instances CLEAR (and poison dependents). Both strategies agree (tri-checked). *(Interpreter: the compute path reuses the rule-side `parallelIterationContexts` anchored on the TARGET's scope + an `invalidIndexGroup` all-instances guard; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeParallelIterationDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … ComputeParallelIterationTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF96.)*

## §11/§12 supplement — validateFull does NOT run computations — validate and compute are separate operations (runtime, 2026-06-09) <a id="kf26"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

The kernel keeps **validation** (`IDocumentRtService.validateFull`) and **computation** (`compute`) as distinct, independently-invoked operations — confirmed in the kernel dev docs (`a12-kernel/documentation/_merged/kernel-dev.md` §"Validating a document / Computations on a document": *"the use of validation and computation is very similar, therefore only one is described"*, and the worked sequence `validateFull(doc)` … `compute(doc)` … `result.appliedTo(doc)`). There is **no combined call**: `validateFull` validates the document's values **as-is** and does **not** compute computed fields first, and `compute` returns a *result* (the input is never mutated — applying it back via `appliedTo`/`apply` is a separate, opt-in step the consumer orchestrates).

**`applyTo` semantics — the apply-computed-back step (source: `IDocumentComputationResult`).** The third operation is `result.applyTo(doc)` → the **updated document** (`old + computed values`). Its javadoc is precise: it **applies the `getComputedFieldInstancesWithChanges` values and CLEARS** the instances in **both** `getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors` **and** `getClearedFieldInstances`. So an **errored** computed value is *emptied* on apply, not carried as the bad value — same as a cleared one; only a clean VALUE is set. (`applyTo(DocumentV2)` returns a new doc, original unchanged; the deprecated `applyTo(IDocument)` mutates in place.)

**The post-application cell STATE, per result kind (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-17; separate-destination boundary added 2026-07-30, EXP-2026-07-30-02; IG72 → IF126).** What `applyTo(DocumentV2)` *leaves in the applied document* — the per-cell refinement of the paragraph above, and the contract behind the interpreter's `Document.cellStateAt`. The table's target input state is the **computation source also used as the destination**, the route exposed by `DmInterpreter.applyComputations`; retained-result application to another destination is the separate matrix immediately below. Mechanism (source: `DocumentComputationResultImpl.applyTo(DocumentV2)` folds cleared → errored → changed instances through `ComputedFieldInstanceImpl.applyTo(DocumentV2)`, which writes via `DocumentV2.withFieldValue` → `GroupDocCommonImpl.updateNested`/`withDirectFieldValue`; strategy-independent — `DocumentDynamicRtService` and `DocumentStaticRtService` share `DocumentAbstractRtService.compute` → `LowKernelApiUtils.callCalculator` → the same result impl):

| result kind (target's input state) | applyTo action | post-application state |
|---|---|---|
| VALUE, typed-unequal (`WithChanges`) — target **absent** | `withFieldValue(ptr, v)`: `updateNested` **creates missing ancestor rows**, `FieldInstanceV2.ofValue(v)` creates the cell | **present-value(v)** |
| VALUE, typed-unequal — target present (empty or stale) | `field.withValue(v)` | **present-value(v)** |
| VALUE **typed-equal** to the stored one (`WithoutErrors` only) | **not applied** (only `WithChanges` is) — untouched | present-value (indistinguishable from an applied write) |
| CLEARED (minted **only** for an input-FILLED cell — `getFilledComputedFieldInstances`, `valueExists` = non-null and non-`""`) | unguarded `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` — the instance **stays, value nulled** | **present-empty** — a clear is never a removal |
| computed-nothing — target present-EMPTY | **no instance minted** (not input-filled) → untouched | present-empty |
| computed-nothing — target **absent** | no instance minted → untouched | **absent** (the Set Y receipt's silence, as a state) |
| ERRORED (unconditional; non-empty computed value + error message) — target present | **guarded** `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` | **present-empty** |
| ERRORED — target **absent** | the guard `document.field(ptr) != null` fails → document returned **unchanged** | **absent** |
| untouched cells | never visited | placement state preserved (a stored `\r\n` stays — the IF124 normalization is eval-cache-side only, never the store) |

The errored branch's guard exists precisely because `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` would otherwise *create* a present-empty instance (`withDirectFieldValue` creates even for a null value) — so "errored is emptied like cleared" (above) holds only for a *present* target; an absent one stays absent. (The deprecated V1 `applyTo(IDocument)` clears errored instances *unguarded* via `DocumentUtils.clearFieldInstanceValue` — a V1-route difference outside the supported `DocumentV2` contract.) *(Interpreter: `Interpreter.applyComputations` composes one retained `ComputationReport` application back to its source and reproduces every same-document row; the low-level read-back remains `Document.cellStateAt` (IF126/IF239), while the public snapshot is encoded through its owning Document API. Locked: `adapter.laws.AppliedCellStateDiffTest` — tri-check groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter on all nine same-document shapes + the CRLF raw read — + multiplatform `interpreter … CellStateAtTest`.)*

**Retained result applied to a separate compatible destination (tri-differential, 2026-07-30, EXP-2026-07-30-02; interpreter completion IF239).** Classification remains fixed against the source, while action placement is resolved against the destination. A clean no-selection source with a filled row-2 target mints CLEARED; applying that action to a destination whose row 2 exists without the target creates a **present-empty** target, and applying it where row 2 itself is absent also creates the addressed row and present-empty target. The rival clear-existing-only account is therefore false outside same-document application. Four controls isolate the action: an absent source target mints no cleared instance and leaves the missing destination row/target absent; a source-classified ERRORED result leaves an absent destination absent because its branch is guarded; changed VALUE creates the missing row with exact `7`; CLEARED over destination value `8` leaves present-empty. Dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the standalone interpreter agree. The observation route exposes the addressed second-row cell but no repetition count, so predecessor padding was not promoted from source composition into a separate observation and no topology claim was added. The interpreter's compatibility boundary is same-`PreparedModel` ownership; that refusal is library safety around the kernel's caller precondition, not a kernel rule. *(Locked: `SeparateDestinationClearDiffTest`, using the kernel result/application route and the public standalone interpreter result/application route; multiplatform `RetainedComputationResultTest`.)*

**Consequence for us:** a *bare* `validateFull` sees a **computed field** as whatever the instance supplies — **empty** if absent (verified: `FieldFilled(CoverageWindow)` does **not** fire when only the `DateRange` operands are present), so a required-or-referenced computed field false-fires `mandatoryField` / misses its value. The **form-engine** answer applies the computation first. So our runtime-validate verbs **compose by default**: `model eval` / `rule eval` run **compute → applyTo → validateFull** (the form-engine flow), and **`--no-computations`** drops to the bare `validateFull`. The interpreter mirrors `applyTo` as the clean-room `DmInterpreter.applyComputations(doc)` (VALUE set, ERRORED/CLEARED emptied — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF45), the kernel side (the differential oracle) as `KernelAdapter.evaluateApplied`. `model compute` stays the pure `compute` (what does a field compute to?); the document file is never mutated by any verb (read-only — `compute`/eval report, never write `--instance`). The **engine-level** `DmInterpreter.validate == validateFull` parity stays foundational (the differentials rest on it); the *composed* path has its own differential. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EvalDocSeamLawsTest.validationDoesNotRunComputations` (the engine split) + `adapter.laws.ComputeApplyDiffTest` (interp compute-then-validate == kernel `compute().applyTo().validateFull()`).)*

## §12 supplement — Full vs Partial validation — `model eval` is FULL; partial may skip `*`-all-reps + `Having` rules (BA appendix; filter skip source + differential locked 2026-07-21) <a id="kf27"></a>

> Fold: folded → §12.

The kernel has two validation modes (BA "Full Validation and Partial Validation", L5303-5321). **Full validation** — our `model eval` = `validateFull` — formal-validates all fields and evaluates every rule. **Partial validation** is a page-level pre-check over an app-specified *relevant-field* set: it guarantees never to report an error fixable only *outside* that set, but **does NOT guarantee complete checking of the relevant fields** — some checks are skipped (performance/simplicity). Named skip examples: rules referencing **all repetitions** via `*` when only some are relevant (explicitly incl. `NotAllFieldsFilled`, `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled`), and **`Having`-filter** rules. The exact skip-set is an **unstable implementation detail** (may change any release; projects must not depend on it). **Consequence:** every law we lock and every catalog example we ship is *full-validation* behaviour — a rule that fires under full validation may *not* fire under a partial page pre-check. For the pinned kernel 30.8.1, the filter case is exact and executable: every rule containing a `Having` filter is skipped before iteration or condition evaluation, even when its error field and every referenced repetition are relevant (`PartialValidationFilterSkipDiffTest`). A future kernel upgrade must re-check the source templates and differential rather than treating this versioned skip as a permanent public promise. Related: the mandatory-"star" in a field's label (BA "the star method") is a *separate decidable heuristic* over a fixed allow-list of presence constructs — a UI affordance outside rulekit's scope.

## §3 supplement — a fired message's `getErrorText` is the *interpolated* end-user text (runtime, 2026-06-14) <a id="kf28"></a>

> Fold: folded → §13.

`IMessage.getErrorText()` returns the message with its tokens already resolved against the evaluated document, not the authored template (`$<fieldname>$` → the display string, `$<field.value>$` → the display value; no other substitution). Only the runtime can produce it. Treatment: `KernelAdapter.evaluate` surfaces it per message; the CLI returns it as `data.messages[].message` / `data.message` (FEATURE-GAPS G1). *(Locked: `adapter.MessageInterpolationTest`; CLI surfacing by `cli.RuleTestVerbTest.firesOnAViolatingInstance`.)*

## §3 supplement — `$field$` is the field's *display name*, and the kernel sources it from a caller-supplied `ILabelProvider` (runtime, 2026-06-23) <a id="kf29"></a>

> Fold: folded → §13.

The token resolves through a three-step cascade. The caller-registered `ILabelProvider` (`IDocumentDynamicServiceConfig.getLabelProvider()`) is consulted first, and a present result wins **exactly, including the empty string**. If the provider is absent or returns no result, a nonempty localized model label wins; only then does the kernel emit the resolved `fullName/idx.idx` path+index debug fallback. dmtool deliberately supplies its label-or-name provider profile; a standalone interpreter consumer may select a different profile or no provider. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DisplayNameDiffTest`; multiplatform `MessageDisplayPolicyTest`; source-grounded provider/fallback cascade.)*

## §3 supplement — a formal error silently suppresses the author's rule (runtime, 2026-06-06) <a id="kf30"></a>

> Fold: folded → §3.

All four claims are canonical in §3 ("Suppression is branch-scoped — and it reaches presence checks"); the evidence skeleton:

- **The silent suppression** — a malformed value raises only the kernel's own formal-error code; a referencing author rule never fires. Observed codes: `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen`, `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen`, `fuehrendesBlank` — German internal identifiers, not `MVK_*`, locked as the observed contract. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FormalErrorLawsTest`.)*
- **The sentinel attribution** — a formal error reports `rulePath = "formalePruefung"`; an author rule's is its slash-path — the discriminator in a validation report. *(Verified 2026-06-09; locked: `cli.EvalDocVerbTest.aFormalErrorIsReportedAsAKernelCheck`.)*
- **Branch-scoped, not rule-scoped** (RT-semantics drain, probe-corrected 2026-06-10): `healthyTrue Or [broken] > 0` still fires; the `And` twin is absorbed. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FormalErrorScopeLawsTest`.)*
- **Even a presence check is UNKNOWN on a not-check-relevant field** (fuzz-caught 2026-06-24): `isWertAngegeben` backs `FieldFilled`/`FieldNotFilled` AND the fill quantifiers, so a malformed operand makes them undecidable — the quantifier tally counts UNKNOWN in neither bucket. *(Interpreter fixed at the shared root — `filledTri` + the 3-state `quantCount`; locked: `adapter.laws.CurrentRepetitionDiffTest.malformedErrorFieldAtFiringRow` + `QuantifierMalformedDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — two more NUMBER sign formal errors: signed-zero and leading-plus (runtime, 2026-06-23) <a id="kf31"></a>

> Fold: folded → §3.

Surfaced by the **differential fuzz net** (`adapter.perf.InterpreterKernelFuzzTest` — random generated documents, assert interp == kernel), which fed a `-0`:

- **A signed zero is a formal error** — `-0`, `-0.0`, `-0.00` (any minus sign on a zero magnitude) → **`zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen`**. A non-zero negative (`-5`, `-0.01`) and a plain zero (`0`, `0.00`) are clean.
- **A leading plus is a formal error** — `+5`, `+0`, `+0.5` → **`zahlMitPlus`** (distinct from `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen`; the explicit `+` is its own error, not "invalid characters").
- **Ordering: signed-zero is checked BEFORE the decimal-separator/scale checks.** `-0.0` on a **scale-0** field is `zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen`, *not* `zahlHatDezimalTrenner` — so a malformed value can match several checks and the kernel's precedence matters. (Leading-plus likewise wins over signed-zero: `+0` → `zahlMitPlus`.)

Both ride `formalePruefung` (the sentinel rule path) like the other formal errors, and suppress a referencing author rule (branch-scoped). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.NumberSignFormalDiffTest` — interp vs kernel over a scale-2 and a scale-0 field; the precedence is part of the lock.)*

## §3 supplement — BASELINE value checks run for EVERY kind before the type format: charset + leading/trailing blank (probe + source, 2026-06-26) <a id="kf32"></a>

> Fold: folded → §3.

(The charset half reconciles with the "`modelConfig.supportedCharacters` is enforced at runtime" finding below: the *model-level* slot IS DM-JSON-declarable and runtime-enforced; what is not declarable is a *per-field* charset — the BMP default below is the no-slot case.) `FormalChecker.checkFieldValueFormat` runs three baseline checks on **every filled field value**, regardless of kind (string/number/date/…), **before** the type-specific format check — and they **pre-empt** it:

- **legal charset** → **`ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz`**. The legal set is `metaData.getLegalCodepoints()` — a **programming-language/codegen** config (`MetaModelImpl`, the codegen target's restricted charset; cf. the codegen `MVK_INVALID_CHAR_IN_RESTRICTED_CHARSET` "not permitted for the programming language"). It is **NOT a DM-JSON field/model property** (no `charset` key on `StringType`/`IStringType` in 30.8.1). With none configured — a plain DM-JSON model — the **default legal charset is the BMP** (`0x0000-0xFFFF`; `CharsetUtil`: "empty list ≡ 0x0000-0xFFFF"). So **any supplementary-plane character** (a UTF-16 surrogate, e.g. an emoji `😀` U+1F600) is illegal; BMP chars (`é`/`ü`/`€`/`™`/CJK) are legal. Probe-confirmed on a plain string field.
- **leading blank** → **`fuehrendesBlank`**; **trailing blank** → **`nachfolgendesBlank`** (distinct codes). Probe-confirmed across STRING, NUMBER, and DATE — and they pre-empt the type check (a ` 2024-01-01` DATE is `fuehrendesBlank`, not a date-format error).

Order: charset → leading → trailing → (type format). This **corrects** the earlier suspect §3 "string charset is not DM-JSON-declarable / no serialized key" note — the basis was about a per-field `StringType.charset` key (which indeed doesn't exist), but the runtime check fires anyway from the **default BMP** charset; the conclusion ("out of scope") was WRONG.

**Is the charset customizable? Yes — but at the CODEGEN/PROJECT level, not via the DM model.** A restricted charset *can* be declared (`KernelUtils.validateCharset`/`simplifyCharset` process a `restrictedCharset`), and the legal codepoints are **baked into the generated meta-model** by the codegen templates (`CodeGenMetaModel.st`) from the project's programming-language config — they do NOT travel with the DM-JSON model. The real-world case is a standard restricted set such as **DIN 91379** (the EU public-administration / e-government character set for names & addresses) — far NARROWER than the BMP: it would make `中文`/many symbols illegal. The probe confirms a DM-JSON model gets the **full-BMP default, NOT DIN** (`é`/`ü`/`€`/`™`/`中文` all legal), so a project-configured DIN charset is genuinely a separate input. A model loaded purely from DM-JSON (the interpreter's path **and** the dynamic-codegen `RuntimeLaws` differential) gets the BMP default, and the interpreter's BMP check matches the kernel for every such model. **That trigger has FIRED:** the interpreter carries the **`LegalCharset` SPI** (IF43 — default BMP, matching the DM-JSON-only kernel; a consumer-supplied set such as DIN 91379 is enforced instead), the `legalCodepoints` half of the customization-surface parity rule. *(Interpreter: a single cross-kind `baselineValueError` in `eval/FormalError.kt`, IF43; locked `adapter.laws.BaselineValueFormalDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — DECLARED field-type CONSTRAINTS are formal errors too (range / length / pattern / digit-count / enum-domain), source + probe-verified 2026-06-25 <a id="kf33"></a>

> Fold: folded → §3.

The format checks above (decimals, invalid chars, blanks, signed-zero) test whether a value is *well-formed for its kind*. But the kernel's `validateFull` **also** auto-validates the value against the field type's **declared constraints** — and these ride the **same `formalePruefung` mechanism**: an out-of-constraint value is a formal error, the field becomes **"unknown"**, and a referencing author rule is **suppressed** (branch-scoped, §3 / IF17). No rule is involved — the kernel emits these purely from the field-type declaration.

The constraints are declared on the field type (kernel `IStringType` / `INumberType`, source-read) and the formal-error codes are the kernel's full runtime set (`FormalErrorCodesProvider.getFormalErrorCodes`, ~55 codes). The constraint → code map (the ones a DM-JSON model can declare and a document can violate):

| field type | DM-JSON declaration | runtime formal-error code |
|---|---|---|
| **NUMBER** | `minValue` / `maxValue` | `zahlZuKlein` / `zahlZuGross` |
| | `minFractionalDigits` (too few / none) | `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen` / `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` |
| | `zeroNotAllowed: true` *(inverted key — NOT `zeroAllowed`)* | `zahlIstNull` |
| | **leading zeros** — forbidden BY DEFAULT; `leadingZerosAllowed: true` opts out | `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen` |
| | **> 15 TOTAL digits** (int + frac, sign/separator excluded) — a GENERIC parse check on EVERY number, not a declaration | `stelligkeitZuLang` |
| | `maxIntegerDigits`, OR `trait:"amount"`'s implicit `15 − scale` cap (only without explicit `maxIntegerDigits`) — the kernel's one `maxDigitsBeforeDecimalPlace` | `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` |
| | `minLength` / `maxLength` (value-string length) | `zahlZuKurz` / `zahlZuLang` |
| **STRING** | `pattern` (regex) | `stringFalschesMuster` |
| | `noValueValidation: true` — disables the WHOLE string check (pattern/length/newline) | *(skips all — only `maxLength` may accompany it, and it is not enforced)* |
| | `minLength` / `maxLength` | `stringZuKurz` / `stringZuLang` |
| | **newline** — forbidden BY DEFAULT; `lineBreaksPermitted: true` opts out | `stringMitNewline` |
| **ENUMERATION** | the value domain (out-of-domain stored value) | `stringFalschesMuster` |

**Two are kernel DEFAULTS, not declarations** (follow-up probe 2026-06-26): a plain number rejects a leading zero (`"007"` → `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen`; `"0"`/`"0.50"` clean) and a plain string rejects a newline (`"a\nb"` → `stringMitNewline`) unless the field opts in.

**More NumberType integer-digit / length constraints DO serialize (corrected 2026-06-26; the `stelligkeitZuLang`/amount attribution CORRECTED again 2026-07-07 — see the digit-check supplement below):** `maxIntegerDigits` → `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` (integer-digit count above the cap; sign + decimals excluded); `trait:"amount"` → an implicit integer-digit cap of `15 − scale` **firing the SAME `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`** (only without explicit `maxIntegerDigits`); NumberType `minLength`/`maxLength` → `zahlZuKurz`/`zahlZuLang` on the value-string length (sign + decimal point counted). All modeled by the interpreter (IF30/IF67). **Still out of scope (probe-confirmed):** a **charset** check (`zeichenNichtImZeichensatz`) is not declarable and does not fire by default (tab / non-ASCII / control chars on a plain string are all clean); a field-level `uniqueValue` is not DM-JSON-deserializable.

**Probe-verified (2026-06-25/26)**: `/Order/BirthDay` (`minValue:1, maxValue:31`) — `"99"` → `zahlZuGross`, `"0"` → `zahlZuKlein`; `/Order/Priority` (enum) — `"BOGUS"` → `stringFalschesMuster`; precedence — string pattern/newline fire **before** length; the **suppression** was confirmed (a rule `[BirthDay] == 99` with `BirthDay = "99"` does **not** fire — only `zahlZuGross`, the field is unknown). So an out-of-constraint value is observable two ways: the **auto-emitted formal error** *and* the **suppression of referencing rules**.

This is the same class as §94/§98 (format-error suppression) — only the trigger differs (a declared constraint / kernel default vs raw well-formedness). The **interpreter now models this dimension** (the per-cell family + the two defaults) — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF20, differential-locked by `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest`.

## §3 supplement — CRLF is normalized to LF at EVAL INGESTION — the format measure, rule reads, and compute reads all see `\n` (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-15; feedback F12 → IG70 → IF124) <a id="kf34"></a>

> Fold: folded → §3.

One normalization, `RuntimeUtils.normalizeLinebreaks` = replace the `"\r\n"` PAIR with `"\n"` (a **lone CR stays**), applied at two source loci with one observable effect — *every evaluation-side read of a valid string value sees the normalized text*:
