A star on it draws `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD_FOR_RULEGROUP` (the grammar admits the token solely for the nicer error). rulekit renders qualified group paths instead.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.DirectiveLawsTest`.)*

## Condition lexing — keyword case-variant families; whitespace rides a hidden channel; a bare name is never an arithmetic operand (source `LexerTerminals_en` + grammar, differential-locked 2026-07-06/09) <a id="kf76"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — concrete-syntax/lexer tier; the canonical doc defers grammar shape to the origin project's grammar doc.

Three lexer/grammar-tier facts about the condition DSL's concrete syntax (they have no KERNEL-SEMANTICS home — the canonical doc defers grammar shape; clean-room from the lexer terminals + the ANTLR grammar, behavior-locked):

- **Keyword case variants are FIXED per-keyword token families, not blanket case-folding.** English admits `And`/`and`/`AND`, `Or`/`or`/`OR`, `In`/`in`/`IN`, and `to`/`To`/`TO` (the `ValueNotConsistent` range form); arbitrary mixed case (`aNd`, `oR`) stays rejected. The configured German lexer has the measured peer families `Und`/`und`/`UND` and `Oder`/`oder`/`ODER`, while `In` remains language-independent. *(Interpreter: language-scoped `ConditionLanguage.accepts`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF55/IF223; locked by multiplatform `KeywordCaseVariantParseTest`/`ConditionLanguageParseTest`, the German real-Kernel include fixture, and both catalog populations in `ParserCorpusSweepTest`.)*
- **Whitespace (and `;;` comments, below) ride the lexer's HIDDEN channel** — insignificant between ANY two tokens. Observable in path syntax, where a naive parser is tempted to read spacing as structure: `Group* / Field` ≡ `Group*/Field`, `.. / seg` ≡ `../seg`, and whitespace **within a `..` up-run** (`.. ..` ≡ `....`) is insignificant too. *(Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF75/IF91; locked `interpreter … PathWhitespaceParseTest` + `ParentUpWhitespaceParseTest` (JVM+JS).)*
- **A bare identifier is never an arithmetic operand**, so `/` and `*` after a bare path segment are always PATH syntax (separator / wildcard), never division/multiplication: the grammar's arithmetic atoms (`einfacheOperation`) are **bracketed** `[entity]` references, constants, or named functions — a bare name cannot start one. This is what makes the whitespace insensitivity above unambiguous.

## Condition comments — `;;` line comments are concrete syntax: tolerated; rulekit authors them and preserves the leading one (grammar + 2026-06-12) <a id="kf77"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — concrete-syntax tier + rulekit treatment record; comments are not carried in the parsed AST, so there is no evaluation claim.

The condition lexer defines comments — `Base_ConditionLexer.Comment: ';;' ~('\r'|'\n')* …` — so `;;` begins a **line comment** (continued across lines that each restart with `;;`), parsed *around* a `teilBedingung` (GRAMMAR-LEDGER). So the kernel **validates a commented condition** — comments are read-tolerated. But a comment is **concrete syntax not carried in the parsed AST**: the kernel ships `ConditionFormatter.stripComments` to remove it, and rulekit's typed AST stores none.

rulekit's treatment of this kernel fact (detail: KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES "Condition comments"): comments live in a **side-channel** beside the AST (`rulekit.dsl.Comments`, keyed by node identity, covering both a `Condition` branch and a `Calculation` operation), so they can be **authored** — on a rule (`Rule.Builder.comment/commentAfter`) and on a **computation**, its operations *and* its preconditions (`Computation.Builder.comment(...)`) — rendered as `;;` lines the kernel accepts. A **rule's leading comment is preserved by default** through read→modify→write-back (`Comments.preserveLeading`, applied in `ModelWriter.replaceCondition`); a **computation's** leading comments (its common precondition + first operation) are likewise preserved on modify (`ModelWriter.replaceComputation` rebuilds the element, so the old slot comments are carried — per-alternative matching across a re-expression stays positional/out of scope). The residual **#3 lossiness** is now only *inline/trailing* comments and non-canonical formatting: an AST-based re-render still drops those (the AST has no node to anchor a mid-expression comment to across a re-expression). `KernelAdapter.diff` discloses exactly that — it carries the leading comment on both sides and flags *"inline/trailing comments are not preserved by re-rendering (the leading comment is carried over)"* only when such a comment exists. A rule you don't modify keeps its text verbatim (edits are surgical on the DM-JSON). The concrete-syntax-preserving (minimal-edit) re-render that *would* keep every comment in place is the parked Phase-5 residual (PROJECT-DESIGN §16); the side-channel is the cheaper, chosen alternative for the common (leading-comment) case.

*(Locked: `adapter.ConditionCommentLossinessTest` — a leading comment is preserved (carried in `diff.after`, not flagged lossy) and an inline/trailing one is dropped with the loss disclosed; `adapter.CommentRoundTripTest` — read→modify→write-back keeps the leading comment with no manual sourcing; `rulekit.dsl.CommentRenderTest`/`RuleCommentTest` — the side-channel render + typed authoring; `rulekit.dsl.ComputationAuthoringTest` — comments on a computation's operations + preconditions, kernel-accepted. rulekit treatment: KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES "Condition comments"; kernel API: KERNEL-RULE-API-SURVEY `stripComments`.)*

## §6 supplement — the date family (corpus item 9 slice 9a, 2026-06-04 experiments) <a id="kf78"></a>

> Fold: folded → §2 + §6.

(The empty-operand coercion row and the literal/arithmetic behaviors are canonical in §2's override table and §6; the bare-vs-bracketed authoring rule, the rejection codes, and the implementation/treatment log stay here.) `Today` (DE `Heute`), `DifferenceInDays|Months|Years` (DE `DifferenzIn{Tagen,Monaten,Jahren}`), `AddDays|Months|Years` (DE `Addiere{Tage,Monate,Jahre}`):

- **Date ordering needs no scale machinery** — dates have no scale (unlike the scale-gated numeric field-vs-field, §finding 18), so `[A] < [B]` / `[A] == [B]` between two date fields validates directly. `[Date] >= Today` and date-vs-date `==`/ordering all work.
- **`Today` composes everywhere** — as a comparison operand (`[OrderDate] >= Today`) and as a function operand (`DifferenceInDays(OrderDate, Today)`).
- **A date compared to a non-date is rejected** — `[Quantity] >= Today` → **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`** ("only allowed to compare dates with other fitting dates").
- **Bare vs bracketed by position** — a date field is bracketed `[OrderDate]` as a *value* (in comparisons) but **bare** `OrderDate` as an *entity argument* inside *any* function/predicate/aggregate's parentheses — `DifferenceInDays(…)`/`AddMonths(…)`, `DateRange(OrderDate, DeliveryDate)`, `StartOfDateRange(CoverageWindow)`, `Sum(Items*/Amount)`. Bracketing a call's argument is a parse error (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`), not extra safety. (rulekit's renderer has a `renderArg` for argument position.) **A cold-agent trap** — the N6 baseline showed agents carrying the comparison-bracket habit into `DateRange([…], …)`; now taught proactively by the catalog `AUTHORING` gotchas on the date-range functions and by the skill's field-path rule (the bracket rule generalized + the `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` hint corrected to name *too-many* brackets).
- `DifferenceIn*` returns a **number** (comparable/arithmetic); `Add*` returns a **date** (comparable). Semantics caveats (kernel, §6): differences truncate toward zero rather than counting calendar boundaries; `AddMonths` preserves the day while `AddYears` maps end-of-February — they disagree across leap years.
- **The value functions coerce an EMPTY operand to a fillable `0` and FIRE** (the string/date families' "empty-as-0 backdoor", the mirror of the number column — verified 2026-07-08). `Length(emptyString)` = 0 (`BigDecimalUtils.laenge`, **grow-only**: `kannKleinerWerden=false`); `YearFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/`DayFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate` and `HoursFromTime`/`MinutesFromTime`/`SecondsFromTime` over an empty date/time = 0 (`getDatumsTeil` — `DEFAULT_VALUE`, **symmetric**; Quarter special-cases month 0 → 0); `DifferenceInDays/Months/Years/Hours/Minutes/Seconds` = 0 when EITHER operand is empty (`getDatumsDiff`, symmetric). A **MALFORMED** operand (`nichtPruefungsrelevant`) → `NICHT_PRUEF_REL_ZAHL` → the comparison is SUPPRESSED (malformed is checked BEFORE empty, so it BEATS empty). Unlike a DIRECT comparison on an empty string/date (which is suppressed), these functions coerce — so `Length(EmptyCode) < 5` FIRES (0 < 5) where `[EmptyCode] == "…"` does not. Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF73; differential-locked by `adapter.laws.EmptyOperandFiringDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.EmptyOperandFiringTest`; `Length`'s empty-as-0 also by the pre-existing `EmptyValueLawsTest`.

**Date + time constant literals are implemented (2026-06-07, family 1) — and the earlier "deferred/rejected" claim was wrong, now corrected.** A **German-format** date literal (`"31.12.2020"`, `dd.MM.yyyy`) compares to a date field **regardless of the field's display format** — `[DispatchDate] > "01.01.2022"` is kernel-valid against *both* a `yyyy-MM-dd` and a `dd.MM.yyyy` field (re-verified 2026-06-07; the previous note claiming `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` against a `yyyy-MM-dd` field was a stale mismeasurement). The kernel stores the constant as an `IConstantOperation` typed **DATE**, German-format, with or without the year (`"13.07."`). A **time** literal (`"17:00:00"`) works the same way (also typed DATE). The literal also rides in argument position — `DifferenceInDays(DispatchDate, "01.01.2022")`. Two genuine limits remain: an **ISO-format literal** `"2022-01-01"` is lexed as a *string*, so ordering draws `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON` (correct in the old note); and a **DATE_TIME field can't compare to a bare string literal** (`[ScheduledAt] == "01.01.2022 12:00:00"` → `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`) — a datetime value must be built with `DateTime(...)` (family 3). Modeled as `DateLiteral`/`TimeLiteral` (Calculation, no operator-id — the quote syntax is intrinsic), wrapped in `DateExpr`/`TimeExpr`; authored via `Dsl.dateValue`/`Dsl.timeValue`. `Now`, `DateRange`/`DateRangesOverlap` were since landed elsewhere. *(Locked: `adapter.DateTimeExprReadTest.roundTripsDateAndTimeConstantLiterals` + `dsl.operators.DateConditionTest.rendersDateAndTimeConstantLiterals` + BA-corpus `DateLiteral`/`TimeLiteral`/`DateLiteralDiff` rules.)*

**`Date(d,m,y)` construction + `Valid`/`Invalid` are implemented (2026-06-07; declaration domain completed 2026-07-30).** `Date(day, month, year[, century, shortYear])` builds a date from position-compatible components. A field component is not Number-only: the complete serializable kernel gate admits (1) a Number with a declared nonnegative domain, no positive fractional-digit allowance, and either the position's exact stored-text `maxLength` or its accepted positional `maxValue`; (2) a String with the exact positional `maxLength` plus one of the six literal digit-pattern spellings `[0-9]+`, `[0-9]*`, `\d+`, `\d*`, `[0-9]{N}`, or `\d{N}`; and (3) only for the complete Year position, a Date field with exact format `yyyy`. A plain scale-0 number, `maxIntegerDigits` in place of `maxLength`, a regex-equivalent but differently spelled pattern, and the `yyyy` Date field in any other position draw the measured declaration errors. The client-SPI extensible-String arm has no DM-JSON property and is source-characterized rather than represented (KF63). `Valid(Date(…))`/`Invalid(Date(…))` test whether the constructed date is a real calendar date (the kernel's `IDateValidityCondition` — distinct from the custom-field-type validity overload). Components may also be quoted constants (a literal like `"01"`, preserved verbatim) or matching temporal extractions — including `MonthFromDate`/`YearFromDate` over a **`DATE_FRAGMENT`** source (a year-month partial date; `BoundModel.dateFragment`, a `DateOperand` — comparable like a date and usable as an extraction source, §6 supplement). Modeled as `DateConstruction` (Calculation) + `DateValidity` (Condition). *(Locked: `TemporalComponentDeclarationLawsTest` for every serializable declaration branch and near miss; `TemporalComponentFieldDiffTest` + `TemporalComponentFieldTest` for the field runtime; `adapter.DateConstructionReadTest` for construction/extraction read-back; N7 catalog examples for `Date`/`Valid`/`Invalid`.)*

*(Locked: N7 examples of Today + all three Difference*/Add* operators + the `Today` number-vs-date invalid; `dsl.operators.DateConditionTest` for rendering + the non-date-field handle rejection. The order fixture gained `OrderDate`/`DeliveryDate` DateType fields.)*

### §6 supplement — malformed date-ish VALUES: the `datumBereich*` code trio, calendar validity, malformed TIME/DATE_TIME (probe, 2026-06-25) <a id="kf79"></a>

> Fold: folded → §3 + §6.

(The formal-error class and the fragment-range completion are canonical in §3 and §6; the specific codes and side-parse rules stay as evidence.) What the formal check emits for a malformed date-family **value** (all probe-verified; each suppresses a referencing rule like any formal error):

- **DATE_RANGE — three distinct codes:** **`datumBereichTrennerFehlt`** when the value does not split into two on the declared separator; **`datumBereichFormatFalsch`** when a side is not a valid date for the format (including a calendar-invalid side); **`datumBereichNichtGueltig`** when the start is *after* the end — `start == end` is **allowed** (a one-day range).
- **Calendar validity is checked for every stored date** — the day must exist in its month (Gregorian leap Februarys): `2021-02-31`, non-leap `2023-02-29`, `2024-04-31` are all **`datumFormatFalsch`**, for DATE, DATE_TIME, and each side of a DATE_RANGE.
- **A malformed TIME or DATE_TIME value emits `datumFormatFalsch` too** — the same code as a bad DATE (VALUE-typed), not a time-specific code.
- **A range is equality-comparable but not orderable** — DATE_RANGE values and `DateRange(start, end)` constructions are admitted by `==`/`!=`; `<`, `<=`, `>`, and `>=` are authoring-rejected. Ranges are also consumed via `StartOfDateRange`/`EndOfDateRange` and the overlap predicates, but a `DateRange(…)` construction is not itself admitted in those argument slots.
- **A FRAGMENT-format range is well-formed (differential 2026-07-10):** a `DateRangeType` may declare a fragment format (`yyyy` — a year-range like `2024/2025`, a real workspace idiom), and each side is parsed **by the declared format at whatever granularity** — `2024/2024` carries no formal error; a non-year side is `datumBereichFormatFalsch`, start-year after end-year is `datumBereichNichtGueltig`, and the Gregorian floor applies to the completed start (`datumBereichDatumFalsch`, `1500/1600`). *(Interpreter: the side-parse completes fragments like a fragment DATE field; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF112, locked `adapter.laws.FragmentRangeFormalDiffTest`.)*
- **A FRAGMENT-format range's two endpoints complete ASYMMETRICALLY at EVAL — start = interval START, end = interval END (source + differential 2026-07-11).** When a `DateRangeType` fragment value (`yyyy` year-range) is read at runtime, the kernel completes each endpoint to a concrete date by OPPOSITE interval rules: `RuntimeController.datesAndFormatsToDateRange` calls `DateUtil.createDateFromDateFragment(dates[0], INTERVAL_START, …)` for the START and `INTERVAL_END` for the END, and `DateAndFormat.addMonthAndDay(intervalSpec)` fills a missing MONTH → `01` (start) / `12` (end) and a missing DAY → `01` (start) / the completed month's LAST day (end, via `getLastDayOfMonth`, leap-aware); a missing YEAR → the base year on both. So `2024/2025` (format `yyyy`) reads as the interval `2024-01-01 .. 2025-12-31` — the WIDEST dates the endpoints denote (a plain fragment DATE field, by contrast, always completes `INTERVAL_START` — `feldWertAlsDatumIntern` — so only a range's END side gets the latest-date completion). This is the runtime twin of the `StartOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 01.01 / `EndOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 31.12 rule below, and it is exactly what `StartOfDateRange`/`EndOfDateRange`, the overlap predicates, and `==`/`!=` read off the completed `VkDateRange`. Sub-year extraction (`MonthFromDate`/`DayFromDate`) over a `yyyy`-granularity range is AUTHORING-rejected (the granularity gate, KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 date-extraction), so the sub-year completion is observable only through overlap with a full-format range at the year's far ends (a `2024/2024` season covers a late-December window ⇒ end reached 12-31). *(Interpreter: `DateMath.parseRangeToIso` completes each endpoint per side; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF118, graduated from IG58, locked `adapter.laws.FragmentRangeEvalDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.FragmentRangeEvalTest`.)*

*(Interpreter treatment: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF8/IF12 — `formalError(DATE_RANGE …)`, `parseRangeToIso`, calendar-checking `buildIso`. Locked: `adapter.laws.DateRangeFormalDiffTest` (trio + `calendarInvalidPlainDateMatchesKernel`) + `TimeDateTimeFormalDiffTest` (formal message + suppression).)*

### §6 supplement — date-range overlap: `DateRangesOverlap` any-pair, `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` scalar-vs-list, the predicate-arg `Having` keep-law, filter-derived polarity (probe + source, 2026-07-08) <a id="kf80"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

Clean-roomed from `RuntimeController.datumsBereicheUeberlappend` / `mindestensEinDatumsBereichUeberlappendFL` + `DatumBereich.ueberlappt`, and (for the filter) the codegen `CodeGenFilterCondition` (a compiled boolean); confirmed on BOTH kernel strategies (groovy-dynamic == java-static).

- **`DateRangesOverlap(op0, op1, …)` is ANY-PAIR among ALL operands' kept, filled ranges** — one growing `HashSet<DatumBereich>` in operand order, fire on the FIRST range overlapping any already-seen member; there is **no distinguished first operand**. Consequences: (a) the same row's cell reached via a scalar ref AND via the star is two members → the **self-pair** fires (a per-row `DateRangesOverlap(Period, ../Group* / Period)` fires every filled row); (b) a **list-internal** pair fires even when the scalar operand is disjoint from every list cell.
- **`AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps(scalar In list…)` is genuinely scalar-vs-list** (the parser enforces a single unstarred first param): the scalar is compared against each list cell and is **NOT** a set member, so two LIST cells overlapping each other do NOT fire. A **not-filled, malformed, or formally unavailable scalar → FALSE** before the list scan (no fire — `isWertAngegeben != TRUE → FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`, not unknown-suppression), so a list-internal pair cannot rescue it. The two operators are **not** interchangeable (they differ exactly where the set semantics bites).
- **Empty/malformed cells are SKIPPED** (`isWertAngegeben != TRUE → continue`). A stored **INVALID range** (start > end) normally fails the earlier field formal check and is skipped; independently, `DatumBereich.ueberlappt` defensively returns false if either resolved operand is inverted. Intervals are **CLOSED** (`end1 ≥ start2 ∧ end2 ≥ start1`; touching ranges overlap).
- **A `Having` filter on a predicate's rep-list arg is legal and applied per cell.** The kernel compiles the filter to a **boolean**, so **keep iff the filter is TRUE** (`CurrentRepetition(G) <= 2` keeps rows 1–2; `x != x` drops all; a **malformed** filter operand → runtime-UNKNOWN → not-TRUE → **DROP**). A **`$`-correlation term has NO enclosing iteration** for a plain predicate's arg (unlike a quantifier's `Having`, whose star iterates), so the codegen cannot compile it and the whole comparison/predicate is **trivially TRUE** — hence `!= $RuleGroup` AND `== $RuleGroup` both KEEP every row (the self-pair then fires all rows), while `(x != x) And (== $RuleGroup)` = `FALSE And TRUE` = FALSE drops all.
- **Polarity is filter-derived through two different reached scans, not through authored filter presence.** `datumsBereicheUeberlappend` flips its sticky `containsFilter` only after a **kept, filled** cell of a filter-bearing operand is processed; an empty/dropped filtered container does not taint a later unfiltered firing, while a reached filtered range does taint a later pair even when it is disjoint from that pair. `mindestensEinDatumsBereichUeberlappendFL` instead returns OMISSION only when the **matched** list container carries a filter; an earlier filtered-but-disjoint or empty container does not taint a later unfiltered match.
- **Related consumers of a starred rep-list arg:** `FieldValuesNotUnique` also **accepts + evaluates** a `Having` (same keep-law — probed: `<=2` keeps rows 1–2, `>=2` keeps rows 2–3, changing distinctness); `SumOfProducts` **authoring-rejects** a filter (`MVK_WILDCARD_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_REQUIRED` — the lowest repetition must be an unrestricted `*`).

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateRangeOverlapSemanticsDiffTest` tri-checks the any-pair/scalar-vs-list shape split, empty and malformed-scalar early exits, inverted-range defense, predicate-argument keep-law, and both reached-filter polarity scans against Groovy-dynamic, generated Java, and the interpreter; `FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest` covers that operator's Having cases; multiplatform `interpreter.DateRangeOverlapTest` covers parser/evaluator parity on JVM+JS. Interpreter treatment: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF72.)*

### §6 supplement — DATE/DATERANGE/TIME field *format strings* are a fixed lowercase allowlist, locale-independent (probe 08, 2026-06-22) <a id="kf81"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier format-declaration rules (what `field add`/consistency accepts), outside the evaluation scope.

A field's declared date **format** must be a **lowercase Java date pattern** from a **fixed allowed set** — and that set is **independent of the model's display locale AND its `conditionLanguage`** (verified: `en_US` and `de_DE` conditionLanguage accept the identical set). The A12 editor's UPPERCASE preset labels (`YYYY-MM-DD`, `MM/MM`, …) are display labels, **rejected** as `format` values (`The date range format is invalid` / `The date format is invalid` — a kernel rejection carrying **no code and empty `where`**). Verified by `field add` against fresh `en_US`- and `de_DE`-conditionLanguage models:

- **DATE** — both `yyyy-MM-dd` and `dd.MM.yyyy` validate; `YYYY-MM-DD` rejected.
- **DATERANGE** (`format` + `separator`) — the **(format, separator) pair** is allowlisted, not free: verified-accepted **`dd.MM.yyyy`+`-`** and **`yyyy-MM-dd`+`/`** (identical under en_US and de_DE). Rejected: a spaced `" - "`; a mismatched separator (`yyyy-MM-dd`+`-`, `dd.MM.yyyy`+`/`); a non-standard format (`MM/dd/yyyy`). The separator is a single char that goes with the format.
- **DATEFRAGMENT** — lowercase `MM`/`yyyy`/`yyyy-MM`/`MM-dd` accepted; uppercase rejected. (Partial dates are DATEFRAGMENT, not DATERANGE.)
- **TIME** — the **full `HH:mm:ss` is required**; `HH:mm` (no seconds) is rejected.
- **DATE_TIME** — the **only date-bearing format is the ISO `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`** (probe-enumerated 2026-06-26); every other date-bearing form is rejected `The date format is invalid` — `dd.MM.yyyy'T'HH:mm:ss`, `MM/dd/yyyy'T'HH:mm:ss`, `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss` (space, no `'T'`), and `…'T'HH:mm` (no seconds) all fail. The degenerate **time-only `HH:mm:ss`** is also accepted as a DATE_TIME format, but it cannot receive a full computed datetime (next paragraph). So a DATE_TIME field that holds a date is effectively format-locked to ISO.

**A full `DateTime(...)` result can only target the ISO DATE_TIME field — the compatibility is enforced at CODE GENERATION (probe, 2026-06-26).** Because every computation auto-generates an implicit validation rule `FieldFilled(F) And [F] != <op>` (the "every computation is also a validation rule" finding), a computation whose operation is a full `DateTime(D,T)` but whose target `F` is the **time-only `HH:mm:ss`** DATE_TIME field is rejected at codegen with **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`** ("only allowed to compare dates with other fitting dates") — the implicit compare pits a full datetime against a time-only one, whose date-component sets don't fit (the same `formatsConsistentRegardingFormat` gate as the `Now` finding above). Net for **`compute()`**: every *authorable* computed-datetime target is the ISO `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss` field, so the stored datetime is always ISO — exactly what a clean-room interpreter emits. There is no custom datetime target format to reformat to; a computed-DATE_TIME re-formatter would be dead code. *(This is why the IG19 residual closed as parity-by-construction, not a fix — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF33; locked by `adapter.laws.ComputeTargetFormatDiffTest.computedDateTimeUsesTargetFormatLikeKernel`.)*

**A computation cannot target a partial DateType in kernel 30.8.1 (terminal static matrix, 2026-07-30; correction to SPEC-2026-07-27-01).** A modelkit/rulekit-authored computation that copies one FULL date into each precision passes for a FULL target and is rejected for `DAY_OPTIONAL`, `MONTH_OPTIONAL`, and `YEAR_OPTIONAL`, each with `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`. The discriminating mechanism is the computation's generated implicit validation comparison `[target] != operation`: `CheckFeldWertOpImpl` rejects reading any partially-known Date field as a plain value before runtime, even though each precision's stored-value domain itself admits fully known dates. The only supported warning suppression is `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`; attempting `errorCodesToSuppress: ["MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE"]` is rejected as `MVK_INVALID_SUPPRESSED_WARNING`. Therefore no maintained dynamic/static runtime matrix for a partial Date computation target can exist at this kernel version; treating the runtime store implementation in isolation as reachability was the error. *(Terminal kernel `RuleValidator.check` matrix plus full-model suppression defeat: `ComputedPartialDateTargetAdmissionDiffTest`; the diagnostic's actual terminal code is retained by API-FINDINGS 107.)*

**`DateType.youngerThan1900Check` is an opt-in additional basic check shared by stored and computed dates (tri-engine, 2026-07-30).** With the flag absent/false, `1899-12-31` remains valid because it is above the always-on Gregorian floor. With it true, a real date strictly before `1900-01-01` is `datumFalsch`; the boundary itself is valid. A FULL computed target retains the exact rendered pre-1900 attempt but classifies it ERRORED, while `1900-01-01` is a VALUE. Dynamic Groovy and generated Java agree on classification; the dynamic result and interpreter also agree on the exact attempted text. *(Kernel read-back: `FieldConfigExtrasTest`; runtime: `DateBefore1900DiffTest`; JVM+Node shared formal owner: `DateGregorianFloorFormalTest`; interpreter treatment IF237.)*

**Caveat (don't conflate two validators):** the kernel-rt **runtime value** validator `…rt._30_8.internal.formatdef.FormatDefinitionDatum#validiereFormat` *does* key off the language (`convertPruefFormat2FehlermeldungFormat(pruefFormat, param.getSprache())`, German `'d'→'T'`/`'y'→'J'`) — but that validates a date **value** entered against a format at form-fill time, NOT the field-format **declaration** at model-consistency time (the `field add` check here), which is locale-independent. An earlier "language-keyed" reading of this was a measurement artifact (a reused field name made later iterations fail on `RK_NAME_EXISTS`, misread as a format rejection). Found by probe `08-booking-date-ranges`, where an agent burned 23 `field add` attempts brute-forcing the format because the `field-add` schema mislabeled the editor presets as usable `format` values (now corrected: the schema states the lowercase fixed-set rule and ships a CI-executed working example). *(Locked: the `field-add.schema` dateRange example `dd.MM.yyyy`+`-` is CI-executed by `SchemaExamplesGuardTest`; the reject + locale-independence facts by `FieldVerbTest.dateAndTimeFieldFormatsAreFixedAndLocaleIndependent`.)*

### §6 supplement — `Now` is a date-time; ordering admits a plain date while equality does not (corrected 2026-08-02) <a id="kf82"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

`Now` (DE `Jetzt`) is the current **date-time** — the date-time sibling of `Today`. **Corrected 2026-08-02:** the previous claim that every comparison with a plain DATE is rejected during code generation was false. The exact `[OrderDate] >= Now` model is accepted by both `RuleValidator.check` and the kernel's Java validation-code generator. This follows the direct-comparison partition in §6: ordering requires matching year presence and the same date/no-date class, so DATE and DATE_TIME are compatible; `==`/`!=` additionally require matching time presence, so `[OrderDate] == Now` is rejected with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`. A number-vs-`Now` mismatch is rejected with the same code. The typed rulekit surface now exposes cross DATE/DATE_TIME ordering while deliberately withholding cross-kind equality. *(Locked by `TemporalComparabilityGateLawsTest`, the catalog trigger/control pair, `DateConditionTest`, and the dynamic-Groovy · generated-Java · interpreter `NowDateOrderingDiffTest`.)*

`Now` renders with whole-second syntax but retains the validation clock's exact **epoch-millisecond identity**. `RuntimeController` copies `ValidationMode.currentDateForTest()` into its zone-set calendar without clearing milliseconds and returns that `Date` through `getJetzt`; `VkDate` equality/order delegate to the `Date`, and `BedingungsOperatorHelper.getDifferenceIn*` divides its `getTimeInMillis()` deltas by the selected unit in milliseconds. Therefore a stored `12:00:00` is earlier than `Now = 12:00:00.999` even though their rendered whole second matches, a ±999 ms `DifferenceInSeconds` truncates to zero, and a sub-day shift of `Now` preserves the `.999` remainder. Stored and constructed temporal values remain whole-second. *(Source-verified in `RuntimeController`, `VkDate`, and `BedingungsOperatorHelper`, with both generator strategies checked through their source templates; locked across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, JVM, and Node by `adapter.laws.NowMillisecondIdentityDiffTest` + `interpreter.NowMillisecondIdentityTest`. Interpreter treatment: IF206.)*

### §6 supplement — date-time sub-unit arithmetic: day-carry on add, TRUNCATE-toward-zero on difference (probe, 2026-06-24) <a id="kf83"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

The date-time arithmetic family over DATE_TIME operands — `AddHours`/`AddMinutes`/`AddSeconds` and `DifferenceInHours`/`DifferenceInMinutes`/`DifferenceInSeconds` — operates on epoch milliseconds, so:

- **`Add*` carries across the day boundary.** `AddHours(2024-06-15T22:00:00, 5)` → `2024-06-16T03:00:00` (probe-confirmed: the resulting DATE part is the 16th, the TIME part 03:00). `AddMinutes`/`AddSeconds` likewise.
- **`DifferenceInUnit(a, b) = (epochMillis(b) − epochMillis(a)) / unitMillis`, TRUNCATED toward zero — not floored.** Probed decisively with a reverse difference: `DifferenceInHours(later, earlier)` of −5h30m15s is **−5, not −6**. So it is plain integer division (magnitude = the forward floor, sign by direction) — the same truncate-toward-zero the `DifferenceInMonths`/`DifferenceInYears` "whole periods" implementation already produces (`wholePeriods` computes the positive count then negates). The arg order matches `DifferenceInDays`: `b − a`; a `Now` operand may supply a non-zero millisecond remainder.

*(Interpreter: `DateMath.epochSecond`/`isoFromEpochSecond`/`addDateTimeSeconds` + `Interpreter.dateTimeShift`/`dateTimeDiff`. Locked: `adapter.laws.DateTimeArithmeticDiffTest` — the add day-carry cases + the forward/reverse difference sign cases.)*

### §6 supplement — `Date(...)` construction value + `Valid`/`Invalid` 3VL & polarity (probe, 2026-06-24) <a id="kf84"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

Runtime semantics of the date construction family (the catalog/DSL modeling is the §6 date-family supplement above; this is the *evaluation* behavior, probe-confirmed):

- **`Date(day, month, year)` of an UNREAL date has no date value** — it does **not** roll over or keep the out-of-range label. Consumer behavior is reason-sensitive: direct numeric date/time extractors and date differences receive the kernel's numeric default **0** for that present-but-valueless `VkDate`; malformed construction remains non-relevant/Unknown. The direct date-component family is Day/Month/**Quarter**/Year: for a real month `m`, Quarter is `((m - 1) / 3) + 1`; the zero sentinel stays zero instead of passing through that formula. A guard comparing an extractor to another number can therefore distinguish unreal-present from malformed even though neither construction carries a date value.
- **`Valid(Date(...))` = all parts present (non-empty) ∧ all well-formed ∧ the date is real.** Fires VALUE.
- **`Invalid(Date(...))` = present-and-well-formed ∧ (not real ∨ some part empty)** — and its **polarity is directional**: an **empty (fillable) part → OMISSION** (filling it could make the date real), a **present-but-unreal date → VALUE**. It even fires (OMISSION) on an **all-empty row** (every part unspecified is invalid — so `Invalid` "can fire empty", unlike `Valid`).
- **A MALFORMED part (a formal error, e.g. a non-numeric in a number field) suppresses BOTH** `Valid` and `Invalid` (each goes UNKNOWN → quiet; the `zahl…` formal error fires instead). At the strong-Kleene truth projection they are exact complements: REAL gives TRUE/FALSE, UNREAL_PRESENT and HAS_EMPTY give FALSE/TRUE, and MALFORMED gives UNKNOWN/UNKNOWN. What cannot be recovered from `Valid` alone is `Invalid`'s full verdict polarity: HAS_EMPTY is OMISSION while UNREAL_PRESENT is VALUE.

So the interpreter classifies a `Date(...)` construction as REAL / UNREAL_PRESENT / HAS_EMPTY / MALFORMED, and drives the constructor value, the two predicates, and Invalid's polarity off that one classification.

*(Interpreter: `Interpreter.analyzeDate`/`dateStatusOf` + constructor-specific `DateMath.isConstructibleDate`; locked by `adapter.laws.DateConstructionDiffTest`, `DateExtractDiffTest`, `ConstructedDateCalendarDiffTest`, and multiplatform `ConstructedDateCalendarTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — `Date(...)` reality and every legal direct consumer use the model-zone legacy hybrid calendar (source + both-strategy differential, 2026-07-20) <a id="kf85"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

Both generated strategies call `RuntimeController.constructDatum`, which delegates to a cleared, non-lenient default `GregorianCalendar` in `modelConfig.timeZone`. Its default cutover is Julian through `1582-10-04`, skips `1582-10-05..14`, and is Gregorian from `1582-10-15`; therefore UTC `1500-02-29` is real. Zone discontinuities also participate: the kernel rejects `Pacific/Apia` `2011-12-30`, while the interpreter deliberately refuses that unsupported zone before evaluation.

The resulting `VkDate` is an instant, so legacy-calendar provenance must survive composition. Numeric extractors preserve `1500/2/29`; `DifferenceInDays(1500-02-29, 1500-03-01) = 1`; AddDays crosses `1582-10-04 → 1582-10-15`; and the same provenance flows through `DifferenceIn{Months,Years}`, `Add{Months,Years}`, direct date `Min`/`Max` operands, and `DateTime(Date(...), Time(...))`. A valueless unreal/incomplete construction is skipped by date `Min`/`Max`, while malformed non-relevance still poisons. This positive operand acceptance does not widen the component-extractor grammar: `YearFromDate(Min(...))` is rejected. The constructor rule is separate from the proleptic-Gregorian helpers used by stored values and from the later always-on `1583-10-16` stored/computed-value floor. *(Locked across Groovy-dynamic, generated Java, JVM, and Node by `ConstructedDateCalendarDiffTest` + `ConstructedDateCalendarTest`.)*

**4-arg century form `Date(day, month, century, year)`** (probe-verified 2026-06-26): the year is split into a 2-digit century + 2-digit year, full year = `century*100 + year` (`Date(15,6,20,24)` → 2024). The grammar accepts up to 4 `datumParam` (`datumKonstrukt`), and the runtime evaluates it as a real date through the same REAL/UNREAL/HAS_EMPTY/MALFORMED classification. **Operand constraints:** each `Date` param must be a **field reference** (integer literals are rejected), and the field must carry a **date-compatible NumberType range** or the model fails to build (`MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`). Interpreter: arity-flexible `analyzeDate` (IF31); locked by `adapter.laws.Date4ArgConstructionDiffTest`.

### §6 supplement — `BaseYear` is polymorphic by the other operand: base-year-START date vs the year number (probe, 2026-06-24) <a id="kf86"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

`BaseYear` (DE `Referenzjahr`) is the model's configured base year (`content.modelInfo.baseYear`, e.g. `2020`) — the third point-in-time constant alongside `Today`/`Now` (kernel `PointInTimeOperationName`). It is **polymorphic, resolved by its typed use** (probe-confirmed against the order fixture's `baseYear = 2020`):

- **vs a date field → the base-year-START date `2020-01-01`.** Pinned: `[OrderDate] >= BaseYear` fires at `2020-01-01` (and `< BaseYear` does not), and does not fire at `2019-12-31` — so `BaseYear` as a date equals exactly Jan 1 of the base year.
- **vs a number field → the year NUMBER `2020`.** Pinned: `[Quantity] == BaseYear` fires at `2020`; `> BaseYear` fires at `2021` but not `5` or `2020`.
- **inside ordinary numeric arithmetic and a Number computation → the fixed scale-0 year NUMBER.** `BaseYear`, `BaseYear + 1`, and `Input + BaseYear` compute as `2020`, `2021`, and `Input + 2020`. The parser defaults this simple operation to NUMBER; Java and Groovy generator templates materialize `makeDecimal(metaDataValidierung.getBaseYearAsString())` outside explicitly date-typed paths. It has no document-field read and no expandable range; missing model configuration remains `MVK_NO_BASE_YEAR`.

The operation-form numeric wrappers accept that direct numeric role too: `RoundDown(BaseYear)` and `Abs(BaseYear)` are legal in both validation and computation and produce the fixed scale-0 year. This corrects the tempting but false inference that the wrapper's immediate-constant rejection includes `BaseYear`: `CheckOpUtils.isConstant` recognizes only `SINGLE_OP.einfacheOperation().konstante()` (and bracketed forms thereof), while `BaseYear` is the separate `konstanteReferenzjahr` grammar branch; `isOpLikeANumber` explicitly includes `BASE_YEAR`. The derived temporal-number sources are also ordinary legal wrapper children: Date/Time/DateTime component extraction, `YearFromDate(BaseYear)`, and Date-only month/year difference all return scale-0 numbers. Rounding assigns authored scale and preserves their fill directions; `Abs` retains scale 0 and transforms by sign. Empty stored components/difference operands are symmetric zero (rounding stays bidirectional, `Abs` becomes grow-only), a Base-Year source is fixed, and formal unavailability remains unknown/poison. A signed date difference is completed before `Abs`. *(Source-verified in `CheckOpUtils`/`CheckRundenOperOpImpl`/`CheckFeldOperOpImpl`/`CheckDatumExtractOpImpl`/`CheckDatumDiffOpImpl`; dynamic Groovy + generated Java + interpreter locked by `TemporalNumericWrapperDiffTest`, authoring by `BaseYearArithmeticLawsTest`, JVM/Node by `TemporalNumericWrapperTest`.)*

VALUE polarity (a constant, not fillable). It is **model config, not the clock** — so the interpreter resolves it from `ModelDef.baseYear` (no `EvalClock` needed, unlike `Today`/`Now`). A model that uses `BaseYear` without a declared base year is a kernel `MVK_NO_BASE_YEAR` error, so a valid model always carries one.

*(Interpreter: `ModelDef.baseYear` ← `modelInfo.baseYear`; `Interpreter.evalCmpOperand` resolves comparisons by the other operand's evaluated type, context-free arithmetic takes the kernel's numeric default, and temporal consumers select the date form explicitly. Locked across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, JVM, and Node by `adapter.laws.BaseYearDiffTest`, `BaseYearArithmeticDiffTest`, `BaseYearArithmeticLawsTest`, and `interpreter.BaseYearComputationTest`. Interpreter treatment: IF207.)*

**A point-in-time as a temporal SOURCE (source-read, 2026-07-05).** Beyond a plain operand, a point-in-time can be the *source* of an extraction/range op, and the F10 converter reads all such forms (was `Today`-only):

- **Date extraction source** (`YearFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/…): `Today`, `Now`, and `BaseYear` are all valid — `YearFromDate(BaseYear)` (kernel short-circuits a `YEAR` extraction when a base year is declared), `MonthFromDate(Now)` (Now is a date-time, so it has a month). The converter resolves any `IPointInTimeOperation` extraction source via the shared date-operand path.
- **Date-range source** (`StartOfDateRange`/`EndOfDateRange`): **`BaseYear` is the ONE point-in-time the grammar allows here** (`datumBereichAngabe: entitySpec | konstanteReferenzjahr`). It reads the base year as a range: **`StartOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 01.01, `EndOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 31.12** of the reference year (kernel `CodeGenCreator` picks the `monatTag`). The discriminator is the enum `operatorName == BaseYear` — its `getType()` reports NUMBER in the range-source position (vs DATE in the extraction-source position), so never branch on the type there. rulekit authoring: `Dsl.startOfBaseYear()`/`endOfBaseYear()`. *(Locked: `adapter.PointInTimeSourceReadTest` — kernel-valid + round-trip for both extraction and range sources.)*

### §6 supplement — a quoted date LITERAL is typed by its own SYNTAX, not by context (probe, 2026-06-24) <a id="kf87"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

A German-format date literal (`"15.06.2024"`, `dd.MM.yyyy`) is typed a **DATE constant by the kernel from its own syntax** — NOT by the operand it is compared to (clarifies the earlier date-literal family note above). Probed decisively: `[CustomerName(string)] == "15.06.2024"` is **rejected** with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` (string vs a date constant) — so there is no valid "string field vs a `dd.MM.yyyy` literal" comparison. Confirmed behaviors the interpreter must match:

- A `dd.MM.yyyy` literal compares chronologically to a DATE field even though the field's stored format differs (`[OrderDate(yyyy-MM-dd)] == "15.06.2024"` fires for `2024-06-15`).
- The literal also rides in **argument position** — `DifferenceInDays(OrderDate, "01.01.2022")` (the second arg is the date constant).
- A date-looking value inside a value LIST (`FieldValueIncludedInValueList(field, "15.06.2024")`) is a plain **string** value, not a date — so canonicalization must be **position-scoped** (a comparison operand opposite a date, or a date-function arg), never a blanket literal rewrite.
- **The TIME constant is the same story, at `HH:mm:ss` (measured 2026-07-29, reconciling lean SPEC-2026-07-27-06).** A quoted time is classified by its own syntax: exactly ASCII `HH:mm:ss` with real-clock bounds, locale-independent and context-free (`CheckDatumAngabeImpl.checkDatumString` → `DateUtils`: an eight-character ASCII shape, the fixed format, a **non-lenient** parse). `10:30:45`, `00:00:00`, `23:59:59` are accepted; `24:00:00`, `23:60:00`, `23:59:60` (no leap second), `1:30:45`, `10:3:45`, `10:30`, `10.30.45`, `10-30-45`, Arabic-Indic digits, and a trailing space are all rejected — **all with the one code `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`**, so width, punctuation, digit repertoire, whitespace and range are one gate rather than five. Context-freedom is separately witnessed: the same literal is a Time in the `DateTime(date, time)` time argument. **The runtime carrier is transport-only.** A Time constant is constructed on a **1970-01-01 model-zone** instant, and that instant must never reach a Time consumer: what is compared is the decoded clock. The separator makes the literal meet a Time derived from a *zone-parsed* DateTime, the only place the two accounts diverge — `2024-06-01T10:30:45` is CEST (+2) under `Europe/Berlin`, so `TimeFromDateTime(ScheduledAt) == "10:30:45"` fires while `== "08:30:45"` (the same instant's UTC decoding, i.e. the failure mode) does not, and **both outcomes are identical under model zone `UTC`** — which is what establishes zone-independence rather than merely asserting it. *(Locked: `rulekit.validate.laws.TimeLiteralLawsTest` for the static syntax gate; `adapter.laws.TimeLiteralCarrierDiffTest` tri-checks each case over BOTH model zones on dynamic-Groovy, static-Java, and the interpreter — a Time constant lowers through the per-dialect `ConstantOperation_datum.st`, so codegen parity is measured, not inherited — and `interpreter TimeLiteralCarrierTest` covers JVM+Node. The typed `TimeLiteral` carries the text verbatim and lets the kernel arbitrate: there is deliberately no client-side pre-check to keep in step.)*

**Interpreter consequence:** since a date literal in a *valid* comparison always faces a date, the interpreter canonicalizes a `dd.MM.yyyy` `StrLit` to ISO exactly where it is used as a date — opposite a date-producing operand (`Interpreter.evalCmpOperand` + `producesDate`, which reads the new `EvalContext.declaredKind`) and inside the date functions' date-arg reads (`coerceDate` in `epochOf`/`dateComponent`/`dateShift`). An ISO-format literal (`"2022-01-01"`) is a string (kernel `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`), so `coerceDate` only rewrites the German form. The **omitted-year** form (`"13.07."`, day.month., trailing dot) IS modeled (IF52): it is a **reference-year** feature — the kernel injects the model's `baseYear`, so `"13.07."` means `13.07.<baseYear>` (probe-confirmed: on `baseYear = 2020`, `[OrderDate] > "13.07."` fires for 2024/2020-07-20 but not 2019-07-20 or 2020-07-10 — exactly `baseYear`, **not** a floor). A model with NO base year **rejects** it at validation (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`), so the interpreter only sees it with a base year present; `coerceDate` appends `baseYear` before the ISO parse. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateLiteralDiffTest` — ==, ordering, arg-position, the value-list non-canonicalization guard — + `adapter.laws.OmittedYearDateDiffTest` for the omitted-year base-year injection.)*

### §6 supplement — partial DateType values use a zero-component suffix; `ValueAsDate` selects a known-year boundary (probe 2026-06-24, completed 2026-07-30) <a id="kf88"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

A partial `DateType` uses literal zero components governed by the monotone precision enum. `DAY_OPTIONAL` admits a full value or an omitted day (`00.06.2024`). `MONTH_OPTIONAL` adds an omitted month only after the day (`00.00.2024`). `YEAR_OPTIONAL` adds an omitted year only after both (`00.00.0000`). Wider modes retain every narrower legal shape. Probe-confirmed:

- **The omission markers are exact and suffix-ordered.** A lone `0`, a missing component, or a wrong-width token is `datumFormatFalsch`. A lexically shaped but illegal suffix such as `15.00.2024` or `15.06.0000` is instead `datumFalsch`. Formal checking temporarily substitutes `01.01.2000` only to validate an admitted partial shape; that synthetic date is not an evaluation result.
- **`ValueAsDate(field, FirstDay|LastDay)` resolves a known-year partial value**. It selects the month boundary before the day boundary: `00.00.2024` becomes `01.01.2024` / `31.12.2024`; `00.06.2024` becomes `01.06.2024` / `30.06.2024`; a full value resolves to itself. An unknown year is immediately non-relevant, so an enclosing direct comparison is UNKNOWN and does not leak year 2000. `FirstDay`/`LastDay` are operand-less day-specification keywords (the second argument).
- **`ValueAsDate` has its own operand alternative and is admitted in FOUR positions, not only a direct comparison.** *(**Corrected 2026-07-29** — the original bullet read "only valid as a direct comparison operand", generalized from the extractor rejection alone; lean SPEC-2026-07-27-02 named the grammar rule and a probe matrix confirmed it.)* The kernel grammar defines `datumAngabe2 : datumAngabe | feldOpMitDatumSpecial` and uses that wider alternative in exactly three rules — `datumAddOp`, `datumDiffOp`, and the **DATE** argument of `dateTimeKonstrukt` — while every other Date position keeps the narrower `datumAngabe`. Measured: **ACCEPT** `AddDays`/`AddMonths`/`AddYears` base, *both* `DifferenceInDays`/`DifferenceInMonths` operands, `DateTime(ValueAsDate(…), time)`, and the direct comparison; **REJECT** (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`) an extractor (`DayFromDate(ValueAsDate(…))`), `DateTime`'s **time** argument, a `Date(...)` component, and a `Min`/`Max` operand. The discriminator that keeps the account falsifiable: `AddHours(ValueAsDate(…), 1)` *is* in position (it is a `datumAddOp`) and fails the ordinary Date-format gate — `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`, not `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`. Its headline form still compares to a date literal (`> "01.01.2020"`), which is why date literals (above) had to land first.

**Interpreter consequence:** all four DateType precisions load into the same `DatePrecision` axis. `DateMath.partialDateParts` is the sole suffix parser used by the formal gate and value resolution; `resolvePartialDate` selects known-year boundaries and preserves unknown-year non-relevance. `FieldDef.datePrecision`, `EvalContext.dateFormatOf`, and `datePrecisionOf` carry the inputs. The nested positions need no separate evaluator: `evalTemporal`'s leaf path coerces the resolved date like any other Date-valued expression. *(Locked on all three required routes: `rulekit.validate.laws.ValueAsDatePlacementLawsTest` for placement legality; `adapter.laws.ValueAsDateDiffTest` for day-optional nesting; `PartialDatePrecisionDiffTest` for the full suffix/boundary/formal matrix on dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter; and multiplatform `ValueAsDateNestedPlacementTest` / `PartialDatePrecisionTest`. Reconciles SPEC-2026-07-27-03; graduated from IG106.)*

### §6 supplement — `AddYears`/`AddMonths` calendar corrections (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain) <a id="kf89"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

KERNEL-SEMANTICS §6 carries the exact corrections; the drained source (`BedingungsOperatorHelper.addiereJahre/addiereMonate`) pins the exact corrections, both probe-confirmed:

- **`AddYears` corrects the Feb-28 boundary**: a Feb-28 in a non-leap year shifted into a leap year lands on the **leap day** — `28.02.1999 + 1 year = 29.02.2000`, *not* the plain-`Calendar.add` answer `28.02.2000`.
- **`AddMonths` clamps end-of-month overflow**: `31.01.2008 + 1 month = 29.02.2008` — clamped to the shorter month's last day, never a carry into March.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateArithmeticLawsTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — date ranges are closed intervals: touching ranges overlap (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain) <a id="kf90"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

`DateRangesOverlap` / `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` treat both the start **and** the end day as belonging to the range (`DatumBereich.ueberlappt`: overlap ⇔ `end1 ≥ start2 ∧ end2 ≥ start1`). Consequences, both probe-locked:

- **Back-to-back periods sharing a boundary day overlap** — a window ending 31.01 and a coverage starting 31.01 fire the overlap. Seamless non-overlapping periods must start the day *after* the previous end.
- Adjacent-day ranges (end 31.01, start 01.02) do not overlap; a contained range does.
- Source note: an *inverted* range (start after end) silently never overlaps — defensively handled, though the format tier normally prevents one from existing.

**Historical correction (2026-07-08):** the 2026-06-24 probe correctly established the `IN` syntax and closed-interval scalar-vs-list behavior, but its “semantically identical / shared evaluator / always VALUE” conclusion was false. IF72 above is authoritative: `DateRangesOverlap` is any-pair with a sticky reached-filter flag, while `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` is scalar-vs-list with matched-container polarity and scalar-first early exit. The grammar distinction remains: value-list operators and `NumberOfValueInFields` use `In`, this overlap predicate uses `IN` (the interpreter accepts the kernel's `In`/`in`/`IN` token variants). *(Locked by `adapter.laws.DateRangeOverlapSemanticsDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.DateRangeOverlapTest`.)*

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateRangeOverlapLawsTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — partially-known-date extraction gating + the *FromDate date-time source (source + probe, 2026-06-17) <a id="kf91"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier gating (parse/consistency codes over precision × operator), outside the evaluation scope; the datetime-source acceptance is canonical in §6.

`DayFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/`YearFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate`, `AddDays`, and `DifferenceInDays` accept a **DateTime** source, not only a Date (kernel date table: "X can be Date or DateTime"); `DifferenceInDays` also accepts mixed date/date-time pairs. `DifferenceInMonths`/`DifferenceInYears` and `AddYears`/`AddMonths` stay Date-only. On a **partially-known date** (a `DateType` carrying a `datePrecision` other than FULL): **`DayFromDate` is rejected outright** — `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE` (probed on a DAY_OPTIONAL field; distinct from `ValueAsDate`'s `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE_FOR_OP`) — while `MonthFromDate`/`YearFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate` are **accepted on a DAY_OPTIONAL** date (probed). **The full precision gradient is now source-confirmed and locked** (was BA-stated, IG66→graduated): `DateUtils.checkOperationConsistentToPartialKnown` rejects extracting a component the field's `datePrecision` allows to be unknown, keyed on the fragment (`d`/`m`/`y`). The 4×3 matrix (op × precision, all rejects `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`): **DayFromDate** — rejected on DAY/MONTH/YEAR_OPTIONAL (never usable on a partial date); **MonthFromDate** & **QuarterFromDate** (Quarter maps to the MONTH component) — accept DAY_OPTIONAL only, reject MONTH/YEAR_OPTIONAL; **YearFromDate** — accepts DAY_OPTIONAL + MONTH_OPTIONAL, rejects YEAR_OPTIONAL. Monotone: a component is extractable iff guaranteed present under the declared optionality (MONTH_OPTIONAL ⇒ day also unknown; YEAR_OPTIONAL ⇒ all unknown), so YEAR_OPTIONAL rejects every extraction. Confirms BA L4123-4125 exactly. **Add\* on a date fragment is gated by the format's granularity** — `AddDays` needs a day, `AddMonths` a month, `AddYears` a year, else **`MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`** (probed: `AddMonths(MM-fragment)` and `AddYears(yyyy-fragment)` accept, `AddDays(MM-fragment)` rejects; BA L4076-4091). **Source:** the extract gating is `CheckDatumExtractOpImpl` → `DateUtils.checkOperationConsistentToPartialKnown` (rejects extracting the field's *optional* component → `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`); the Add\* granularity is `CheckDatumAddOpImpl.check` → `DateUtils.checkOperationConsistentToDateFormat`, gated on `format.hasComponent(dateFragment)` → `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`. *(Locked: `validate.laws.DateExtractionLawsTest` + `validate.laws.DateFragmentArithmeticLawsTest` + `adapter.laws.DateFunctionsOverDateTimeDiffTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — Valid/Invalid truth projections are exact complements; full verdict polarity is not derivable (runtime, corrected 2026-07-20) <a id="kf92"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

`Valid(Date(...))` fires only when **all referenced parts are specified AND** they form a real calendar date; `Invalid(Date(...))` fires when the date is impossible (e.g. 31 February) **OR any referenced part is unspecified**. Their strong-Kleene truth projections are exact complements: real → TRUE/FALSE, unreal or incomplete → FALSE/TRUE, malformed → UNKNOWN/UNKNOWN. The non-derivability is at the full verdict level: incomplete makes `Invalid` an OMISSION error, while present-but-unreal makes it VALUE; `Valid` is false in both cases. To fire `Invalid` only on a complete-but-impossible date, prefix `AllFieldsFilled(...)`. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateValidityLawsTest` + `DateConstructionDiffTest`.)*

**The BA's "1583 Gregorian floor" (L3833) is NOT a runtime `Date(...)`-construct criterion (probed 2026-06-17, probe-evidence).** `Invalid(Date(1,1,1500))` does **not** fire — the runtime treats `01.01.1500` as a valid constructed date (the same rule fires for an impossible date, so the mechanism works). The floor **is** enforced — at the *value* tier: the later §3 supplement ("a DATE value has an always-on lower bound", 2026-07-09) verified and locked the formal check on stored/computed values (`datumFalsch`, `ComputedDateGregorianFloorDiffTest`). (A 4th BA-vs-source/runtime discrepancy found this review, after `INVALID_INTERVAL`, the semantic-index operator, and the fragment-comparison code.)

### §6 supplement — date-fragment comparability partition (probe-confirmed, 2026-06-17) <a id="kf93"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

Without a base year, two date(-fragment) values compare (`==`/`!=`/ordering) **only if both carry a year or neither does** — a year-bearing fragment/full date and a non-year fragment are incomparable, rejected with **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`** ("only allowed to compare dates with other fitting dates"). Probed: `[YearOnly:yyyy] == [FullDate]` accepts; `[MonthOnly:MM] == [YearOnly:yyyy]` rejects. **Note the code:** a *direct comparison* gives `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`, **not** the BA's `MVK_DATE_WITH_AND_WITHOUT_YEAR` (that is the `DifferenceIn…`/overlap-op code for mixed-year args). A declared **base year** relaxes the partition (a missing year reads as the base year — BA L4069-4074, not probed here). (Source `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl.checkDateType` → `DateFormat.formatsConsistentRegardingFormat(fmt1, fmt2, hasBaseYear())`; the same method also fires `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` for a BASE_YEAR vs a non-date-specification operand — confirming the code is this comparison path's, not the `DifferenceIn…` path's.) *(Locked: `validate.laws.DateFragmentComparabilityLawsTest`.)*

**rulekit treatment — a date fragment IS a `DateOperand` (read + author, 2026-07-05).** Because the kernel collapses a fragment to a plain `Date` for parse/check/render/compare, `DateFragmentRef` is a full `DateOperand` (not the earlier "source-only" kind): it compares with all six operators against a date field, another fragment, `Date(...)`, `Today`/`Now`, `Base Year`, or a date expression, rendering as a bare `[field]`. F10 read converts a fragment comparison through the same date machinery — on the left (`fieldValueComparison` `case DATE_FRAGMENT`) and as a right/date operand (`convertDateOperand`, which previously threw `IllegalArgumentException: … is not a date field (kind=DATE_FRAGMENT)` when a fragment sat in a date-operand slot). rulekit does **not** re-enforce the year-class partition on read — the kernel already accepted the rule. *(Locked: `adapter.DateFragmentComparisonReadTest` — kernel-valid + round-trip for fragment-vs-date, date-vs-fragment, and fragment-vs-fragment.)*

**§6 supplement — date-fragment RUNTIME completion: earliest concrete date, base-year for the year (probe-confirmed, 2026-07-07 — canonical §6).** The *evaluation* companion to the comparability partition above (that is the authoring gate; this is what a valid fragment then does at runtime). A `DateFragmentType` value is stored in its `formatOfFragment` (one of `yyyy-MM`, `yyyy`, `MM`, `MM-dd`); when the runtime reads a fragment field for a comparison / `Add*` / extraction it **completes the missing components to the *earliest* concrete date** and compares as a plain `Date`:

- **missing MONTH → `01`, missing DAY → `01`, missing YEAR → the model base year** (`modelInfo.baseYear`, default 2000). Probed by comparing against a full DATE field of a known value: `[CardExpiry:yyyy-MM = "2024-06"] == [Full = "2024-06-01"]` **fires** (not `2024-06-15`); `[WarrantyYear:yyyy = "2024"] == [Full = "2024-01-01"]` fires (not `2024-06-15`); `[SaleWindow:MM-dd = "06-01"] == [BillMonth:MM = "06"]` fires (`MM` completes day → `01`).
- The **base year is observable**: with `modelInfo.baseYear = 2024`, `[BillMonth:MM = "06"] == [Full = "2024-06-01"]` fires but `== "2023-06-01"` does not (the year-less fragment completes its year to the *declared* base year, which is also what makes it comparable to a year-bearing date — the partition's base-year relaxation).
- **Both kernel strategies share the completion** — it lives in `RuntimeController.feldWertAlsDatum` (kernel-core-runtime), and the codegen's `FieldIdOperation` emits `controller.feldWertAlsDatum(idx, …)` for a date field — so runtime and codegen agree by construction (`DateUtil.getValueAsDate` + `PartiallyKnownDatesUtil.replaceMonthAndYearIfUnknown(…, INTERVAL_START)`). A malformed fragment value (bad month, non-numeric) is `datumFormatFalsch` and suppresses the referencing rule, like any date.

*(Interpreter: `DatePrecision.FRAGMENT` + `DateMath.completeFragmentToIso` + `Document.resolveValue`/`FormalError` — IF57. Locked: `adapter.laws.DateFragmentComparisonDiffTest` — the three-way differential incl. the java-static codegen leg — + multiplatform `interpreter.DateFragmentEvalTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — TIME and DATE_TIME value kinds (implemented 2026-06-07) <a id="kf94"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

(The kernel truths — TIME orderable like DATE, DATE_TIME decomposing via the extraction lattice — are canonical in §6/the field-types table; the modeling/treatment log stays.) **`TIME` is orderable exactly like `DATE`** (no scale, no D13 machinery): `[ServiceWindowStart] >= [ServiceWindowEnd]` is kernel-valid (a time field vs another time field). Modeled as `TimeRef`/`TimeOperand`/`TimeExpr`, obtained from `BoundModel.time(path)`; the same six order operators as dates. Time constant literals (`"17:00:00"`) are implemented (family 1, see the §6 supplement above).

**`DATE_TIME` decomposes into its parts and components** — the kernel's `DateExtractionOperationName` covers the whole lattice, and **the extractions accept a `DATE_TIME` source, not only their nominal kind** (verified against the workspace `DateTimeValues_DM` model, where a single `DateTimeType Source` feeds every extraction):

| operator | source kind(s) | result |
|---|---|---|
| `YearFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/`DayFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate` | DATE **or** DATE_TIME | number |
| `HoursFromTime`/`MinutesFromTime`/`SecondsFromTime` | TIME **or** DATE_TIME | number |
| `DateFromDateTime` | DATE_TIME | date |
| `TimeFromDateTime` | DATE_TIME | time |

rulekit models the numeric ones as `DateExtraction` (kind NUMBER), the part-extractions as `DateTimePart` (kind DATE/TIME), with a common `TemporalOperand` source supertype (`DateOperand`/`TimeOperand`/`DateTimeOperand`). DATE_TIME is both a decomposition source **and** (since family 3) a directly comparable kind — see the construction supplement below. *(Locked: `adapter.TimeAndDateTimeReadTest` — render + kernel-valid + round-trip for TIME ordering, time-of-day extraction, year-from-DATE_TIME, and Date/TimeFromDateTime; N7 catalog examples for all five new operators. The order fixture gained `PickupTime` TimeType + `ScheduledAt` DateTimeType fields; modelkit gained `timeField`/`dateTimeField`.)*

**`Time` / `DateTime` construction + direct DATE_TIME comparison (implemented 2026-06-07, Time arity/extractor domain completed 2026-07-30, family 3).** Two kernel constructors build temporal values from parts: `Time(hour?, minute?, second?)` (DE `Zeit`, the `ITimeConstructionOperation`) → a TIME; and `DateTime(date, time)` (DE keyword is the untranslated `DateTime`, the `IDateTimeConstructionOperation`) → a DATE_TIME. `Time` accepts a zero-to-three-component **prefix**: `Time()` is midnight, `Time("10")` is 10:00:00, `Time("10","30")` is 10:30:00, and the full form supplies seconds; every omitted trailing component defaults to zero. Present-component grammar mirrors `Date(...)`: a component is a bare declaration-compatible field, a quoted constant, or the position-matching `HoursFromTime`/`MinutesFromTime`/`SecondsFromTime` extraction — a bracketed field value `[H]` is a parse error (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`). The extraction source is the ordinary nonliteral Time/DateTime expression grammar, so direct fields, shifts, and `Now` are admitted, while a direct temporal literal beneath it is `MVK_NO_CONST`. A quoted Time component has a real-clock range gate but no width gate: `"1"` is accepted as the hour while `"24"` is refused.

**The nested source is evaluated before extraction, and its no-value reason survives to the constructor.** An empty source contributes numeric zero with missing provenance, so an enclosing `Time(...)` is incomplete rather than manufactured midnight. A formally unavailable source stays unavailable. A reached temporal expression that has no value—discriminated by `AddHours(DateTimeField, field / 0)`—contributes fixed zero instead, so `Time(HoursFromTime(theShift))` is midnight. `AddHours` across Berlin's spring transition is resolved before the hour is read (`01:30 CET + 1h` → wall hour `03`), and changing the injected `Now` changes the nested result. The Date constructor has the same empty/formal propagation for matching component extractors; `Invalid(Date(...))` over an empty extractor source is OMISSION, not the VALUE polarity of a present unreal zero.

For `DateTime`, the date operand is any admitted date value (field / German date literal / `Date(...)` / the `ValueAsDate` placement) and the time operand any admitted time value (field / time literal / `Time(...)` / `TimeFromDateTime`). `TimeFromDateTime` requires a complete DateTime source, preserves a valueless source's reason, and projects only its **model-zone Hour/Minute/Second wall clock**; its internal 1970 carrier date and the source's original date/offset occurrence do not escape. In `DateTime(ValueAsDate(partial, LastDay), TimeFromDateTime(source))`, Date is evaluated first, then the projected clock is composed onto that resolved date; a formally unavailable Date side short-circuits before the Time arm. This also unlocks **direct DATE_TIME comparison**: `[ScheduledAt] == DateTime(OrderDate, PickupTime)` is kernel-valid, so a date-time field compares (`==`/`!=` and ordering) to another date-time value — a field, a `DateTime(...)`, or an `AddHours`-style shift. (Still rejected: a date-time field vs a *bare string literal* — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`; build the value with `DateTime(...)`.)

**A computed Time retains that clock identity through storage and application (measured 2026-07-30, SPEC-2026-07-29-02).** `Time(Hour, Minute, Second)` and `TimeFromDateTime(2024-06-01T10:30:45)` both compute exact `10:30:45` under UTC and Europe/Berlin. The applied typed `DocumentV2` exposes the clock through a 1970-01-01 model-zone `Instant` (`1970-01-01T09:30:45Z` in Berlin), but a rule run after application matches `10:30:45` and does not match the carrier's UTC-decoded `09:30:45`; that `Instant` is transport, not Time identity. An impossible or missing hour and a formally invalid component clear a stale target. The five-channel result has one separate quirk: even a source target already containing `10:30:45` is classified in `getComputedFieldInstancesWithChanges` and is therefore applied to a different destination. Dynamic Groovy and generated Java agree on every arm; the exact cause of that same-clock classification remains deliberately unstated because source inspection alone does not discriminate it. *(Locked: `ComputedTimeTargetDiffTest` dual-kernel + interpreter matrix and multiplatform `ComputedTimeTargetTest`; interpreter finding IF238.)*

Modeled as arity-preserving `TimeConstruction.components` / `DateTimeConstruction` (Calculation) + a `DateTimeOperand` sealed interface (`DateTimeRef`/`DateTimeExpr`, orderable like dates/times) folded under `TemporalOperand`. Authored through the closed `TemporalComponent` union and the zero-to-three-argument `Dsl.time(...)` overloads, plus `Dsl.dateTime(date, time)`. *(Locked: `TemporalExtractorComponentLawsTest` for the exact placement/source gates; `TemporalExtractorComponentDiffTest` across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter; multiplatform `TemporalExtractorComponentTest`; the constant/arity owners `DateComponentConstantLawsTest`, `DateComponentConstantDiffTest`, and `DateComponentConstantTest`; `DateTimeExprReadTest`; `DateConditionTest`; BA-corpus `TimeConstruct`/`DateTimeConstruct`; N7 catalog examples. Reconciles SPEC-2026-07-27-12/-09/-07 and SPEC-2026-07-27-05.)*

**Point-in-time constants `Now` and `BaseYear` (implemented 2026-06-07; `Now` comparison domain corrected 2026-08-02).** The kernel's `PointInTimeOperationName` has three members — `Today` (a date, long modeled), `Now` (DE `Jetzt`), and `BaseYear` (DE `Referenzjahr`) — all carried as `IPointInTimeOperation`. `Now` is the current **date-time**; temporal ordering admits both date and date-time operands, while equality requires another date-time. `BaseYear` is the model's configured base year and is **polymorphic by typed context** (verified): an explicitly date-typed source uses the base-year start, while a number comparison, arithmetic expression, or Number computation uses the fixed scale-0 year. `BaseYear` is only valid in a model that declares a base year — `MVK_NO_BASE_YEAR` otherwise — so **modelkit gained `baseYear(int)`** (emits `modelInfo.baseYear` as a string; the kernel default is 2000). Modeled as `Now`/`BaseYear` (Calculation); the converter handles `Now` as a date-time operand and a date-ordering operand, and `BaseYear` as a date, date-time, and number operand. Authored via `Dsl.now()` (→ `DateTimeExpr`), `Dsl.baseYear()` (→ `DateExpr`), `Dsl.baseYearNumber()` (→ `NumberExpr`). *(Locked: `adapter.DateTimeExprReadTest.roundTripsNowAndBaseYear`, `NowDateOrderingDiffTest`, `NowMillisecondIdentityDiffTest`, `BaseYearArithmeticDiffTest`, and N7 catalog examples. The order fixture declares a base year.)*

**Sub-day time arithmetic over date-times (implemented 2026-06-07, family 2).** The kernel's `Add*`/`DifferenceIn*` families each carry three sub-day members alongside the date ones: `AddHours`/`AddMinutes`/`AddSeconds` (DE `Addiere{Stunden,Minuten,Sekunden}`) and `DifferenceInHours`/`DifferenceInMinutes`/`DifferenceInSeconds` (DE `DifferenzIn{Stunden,Minuten,Sekunden}`). They operate on **DATE_TIME** operands: `Add*` shifts a date-time and **returns a date-time** (it can roll the calendar day over — distinct from the date-returning `AddDays`/`AddMonths`/`AddYears`) while preserving an exact millisecond remainder supplied by `Now`; `DifferenceIn*` returns the signed epoch-millisecond delta divided by the unit in milliseconds, truncated toward zero. The shifted date-time composes as an extraction source — `HoursFromTime(AddHours(ScheduledAt, 2))` is kernel-valid. Modeled by widening `DateShift.Unit`/`DateDifference.Unit` with the three sub-day members (the shift unit carries its result kind, DATE vs DATE_TIME); the converter routes a DATE_TIME field through a `temporalOperand` helper (a `DateTimeRef` is a `TemporalOperand`, so it renders bare in argument position) and `convertTemporalSource` now accepts a shift as the extraction source. Authored via `Dsl.addHours`/`differenceInHours`/… over a `TemporalOperand` (the date `Add*`/`DifferenceIn*` factories widened from `DateOperand` to `TemporalOperand` too). *(Locked: `adapter.DateTimeExprReadTest.roundTripsSubDayTimeArithmetic`, `adapter.laws.DateTimeArithmeticDiffTest`, `NowMillisecondIdentityDiffTest`, `dsl.operators.DateConditionTest.rendersSubDayTimeArithmeticBilingually`, and N7 catalog examples.)*

## §6 supplement — min/max over DATE, TIME, and DATETIME ride a dedicated `DateCombiner`: NULL-date empty identity, valueless-operand skip, symmetric omission (source + differential, 2026-07-07; TIME/DATETIME added 2026-07-22 / SPEC-2026-07-21-07) <a id="kf95"></a>

> Fold: folded → §2 + §12.

(The NULL-date empty identity is canonical in §2 "aggregate identities"; the symmetric date polarity in §12's directional machinery; the combiner-dispatch mechanism stays as evidence.) The kernel dispatches `Min`/`Max`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue` **statically by operand type onto two combiner families** — numbers → `NumberCombiner` (`min`/`minWert`), **date-like (DATE, TIME, DATETIME)** → **`DateCombiner`** (`minDatum`/`minWertDatum`; the only non-number family — min/max over strings is not authorable) — and the two differ in three observable ways:

**All three date-like kinds ride the SAME `DateCombiner`** (`CheckFeldListenOperOpImpl`/`CheckFeldListenOpImpl` admit homogeneous DATETIME-base operands and mutually comparable component formats). The common fold selects by **resolved temporal order, never a rendered label**: **TIME** by its decoded **time-of-day** coordinate (time-only operands share one implicit model-zone anchor and have no standalone instant), **DATETIME** by its **resolved physical instant**. So a seconds-level TIME separation and two **equal-looking** `Europe/Berlin` fall-back-overlap DATETIME labels (`02:30` daylight vs. standard, 60 min apart) remain distinct candidates. The three observable behaviors below (NULL empty identity, valueless-operand skip, symmetric omission) hold identically for TIME and DATETIME.

The **typed surface** covers both temporal extremum shapes. For a repetition-list `MaxValue`/`MinValue`, author with `Dsl.latestTimeOf`/`earliestTimeOf` (→ `TimeExpr`) and `Dsl.latestDateTimeOf`/`earliestDateTimeOf` (→ `DateTimeExpr`), both wrapping the shared `DateListAggregate`; the adapter read boundary lowers the kernel extremum to the matching operand (`ConditionConverter.temporalExtremumKind`/`temporalListAggregate`), round-tripping through the DSL (locked by `adapter.TimeDateTimeAggregateReadTest`). For operand-list `Min`/`Max`, the overloaded `Dsl.min`/`max` preserve the homogeneous DATE/TIME/DATETIME family, while `ConditionConverter` recovers the exact kind hidden behind the kernel transport's common `OperationType.DATE` and converts the list on either side of a comparison (locked by `TemporalOperandMinMaxConditionTest` + `adapter.TemporalOperandMinMaxReadTest`; DG18 / API finding 92).

- **Empty identity.** The number combiner seeds `0` (§1 supplement — `MaxValue()` over all-empty == 0). The date combiner seeds `VkDate.NULL_DATE` — *no value* — and `BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleicheDATUM` returns **false when either side's date is null**, so a comparison against an all-empty (or zero-row) date aggregate **never fires** (`FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`), and a computation of it **clears** the target.
- **Operand-list emptiness.** `minDatum(d1, d2)`'s `VkDate.combine` **keeps the date-bearing side** when the other operand is valueless — an empty date operand is SKIPPED (`Min([OrderDate], [DeliveryDate])` with `OrderDate` empty is `DeliveryDate`), unlike the number form where an empty operand arrives as the substituted `0` and COUNTS. A malformed / required-empty date operand is nicht-prüfungsrelevant → the whole aggregate is suppressed.
- **Symmetric polarity.** `DateCombiner` tracks no directional `kannGroesserWerden`/`kannKleinerWerden` — only the symmetric **`nichtAngegeben`** (ORed over the operands' emptiness, plus `getValue(containsFilter)` marks any filtered result, plus the omitted-tail flag). A fired date comparison types **`TRUE_AF` (OMISSION) iff either side is nichtAngegeben — regardless of the comparison direction** (`RuntimeController.vergleicheDATUM`), where the number star's polarity is directional (§1/§3 supplement).

Comparison **typing** stays static and nominal: a quoted literal beside a date-valued min/max is a DATE literal (`"20.03.2026"`), and no date/text-vs-number comparison is authorable at all (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` / `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`) — so a runtime date-vs-number pair can only ever be evaluator-made, never input-legal. *(Interpreter: kind-aware fold identity + `minMaxOperandVals` + the `starFill` date branch + `producesDate` over the min/max family; the operand-list temporal fold keys a pure TIME by its decoded time-of-day and any dated value by its instant, IF204; locked by `adapter.laws.DateAggregateDiffTest` incl. a codegen tri-check — IF58 — and `adapter.laws.TemporalExtremaDiffTest` for the TIME/DATETIME extension incl. the Berlin overlap.)*

## §6 supplement — direct temporal comparison (coarse) vs temporal-extremum admission (exact) are TWO gates (source + kernel-route laws, 2026-07-22 / SPEC-2026-07-22-01) <a id="kf96"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

The admission format check for a temporal comparison and for a temporal **extremum operand** are deliberately different strengths — conflating them either rejects legal direct comparisons or admits incomparable extremum operands.

- **Direct comparison — coarse** (`CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl.checkDateType` → `DateFormat.formatsConsistentRegardingFormat(fmt1, fmt2, hasBaseYear())`). After optional Base-Year supplementation it requires only **matching YEAR presence** and **matching date/no-date class**; `==`/`!=` additionally require **matching time/no-time class**. It does **not** require identical component sets. Consequences (all kernel-locked): a `yyyy-MM` fragment `==` a `yyyy-MM-dd` date is **accepted**; a plain date `<` a date-time is **accepted** but `==` a date-time is **rejected** (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` — time-presence differs); a date `==` a time is **rejected** (date/no-date class differs); a year-less `MM` fragment vs a year-bearing date is **rejected without** a Base Year but **accepted with** one (the model year supplements the fragment).
- **Temporal extremum operand — exact** (`DateFormat.hasSameComponents` via `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkOpTypesAreComparableDatetimeType`). A multi-operand `Min`/`Max`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue` requires **identical component sets**: the SAME `yyyy-MM` + `yyyy-MM-dd` pair that compares directly is **rejected** inside `Min(...)` with **`MVK_DATEFORMATS_NOT_COMPATIBLE`**; DATETIME extrema require all six components.

This is an authoring-admission distinction only; it does not touch resolved chronology or the extremum fold (that fold is the §6 min/max supplement + IF204). a12-dmkits **delegates both gates to the kernel** (the DSL's single-field extremum scope cannot express the multi-format operand list that trips the exact gate). *(Locked: `validate.laws.TemporalComparabilityGateLawsTest` — the coarse/exact separator, date-vs-date-time ordering-vs-equality, date-vs-time rejection, and Base-Year supplementation, all against the real kernel; neighbor `DateFragmentComparabilityLawsTest` owns the year-presence partition.)*

## §14 supplement — CustomCondition's two forbidden contexts (corpus item 8, 2026-06-04 experiments) <a id="kf97"></a>

> Fold: folded → §14.

`CustomCondition <Name>` (DE `ApplikationsBedingung`; grammar `applikationsBedingung: 'ApplikationsBedingung' nameOhnePunkt`) is a bare predicate — a host-registered name, no operands. KERNEL-SEMANTICS §14 names two bans; both verified and now **pre-checked** (the constructs are representable):

- **Inside a `Having` filter** → **`MVK_DISALLOWED_CONSTRUCT_IN_FILTER_CONDITION`** ("The language feature 'CustomCondition' is not supported for filter conditions"). *(Isolating it needs a filter that already references the iteration — otherwise `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` fires first.)*
- **In computation rules** (§14) — pre-checked in `Computation.build()` by walking preconditions for a `CustomConditionRef`.
- It does **not** satisfy the error-field rule on its own (a bare `CustomCondition X` → `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`); pair it with a field reference — rulekit's generic `Rule.build()` error-field pre-check already covers this.

*(Locked: N7 `CustomCondition` valid; `dsl.operators.CustomConditionTest` for both bans + the bare-token name rule, including digit/underscore starts; `CatalogKernelClaimEvidenceTest` for the kernel admission boundary.
