| `.` on Java line terminators and supplementary code points | **different default/code-unit behavior** | NEL U+0085 and a supplementary code point are concrete whole-match discriminators |
| unmatched backreferences | **different match behavior** | `(a|(b))\2c` over `ac` is false in Java and true in JS |
| `[a-z&&c-e]` | **different class interpretation** | the blacklist catches a nested `[` form, not every Java intersection |
| `\x{…}`, `\N{…}`, `\X`, `\b{g}` | **throws or is silently misread** | these Java-valid forms are not in the textual blacklist; uppercase `\P` is excluded by the direct-oracle correction above |

Anchoring: `^(?:…)$` (the non-capturing group is load-bearing — a naive `^…$` mis-anchors top-level alternation, e.g. `^ab|cd$` matches `"abX"`). Full-region match on both hosts, matching JVM `matcher.matches()`.

**Consequence (owner decision, IF164, graduated from IG90).** The normative semantics are **dynamic-Groovy = Java `Pattern`** (IF136); the kernel-TS raw-`new RegExp` limitation is a recorded, never-adopted strategy divergence. IF164 reproduces the admitted dialect through one ES2018 parser/translator and one engine-owned distinct-pattern registry.

**Relevant to AUTHORING too (rulekit / modelkit / dmtool).** A Java-invalid or kernel-rejected condition/declared-field pattern gets `MVK_INVALID_PATTERN` at `rule check` / `model check`. `KernelPatternPolicy` single-sources the typed rulekit/modelkit preflight; the catalog, schemas, and diagnostic enrichment explain the same boundary without claiming portability (API-FINDINGS finding 73).

## §6 supplement — a Date/DateTime `Add*` AMOUNT is Java `BigDecimal.intValue()`: truncate toward zero, then keep the low signed 32 bits (source + differential, 2026-07-29) <a id="kf166"></a>

> Fold: pending — newly recorded date-arithmetic fact; folds into §6 (dates and time) on the next canonical KERNEL-SEMANTICS pass.

The shift amount of `AddDays`/`AddMonths`/`AddYears`/`AddHours`/`AddMinutes`/`AddSeconds` is not a bounded integer literal: grammar `datumAddOp` keeps it as a complete `operation` and `CheckDatumAddOpImpl` only requires the result type to be Number. The runtime then converts that decimal in **two stages** — `BedingungsOperatorHelper.addTime` → `VkBigDecimal.intValue`, which delegates straight to `java.math.BigDecimal.intValue()`:

1. **truncate toward zero** — the fractional digits are discarded, never rounded (`1.9` → `1`, `-1.9` → `-1`, `0.9` → `0`);
2. **keep the low signed 32 bits** — an amount outside `int` is neither rejected nor saturated (`2147483648` → `-2147483648`, `-2147483649` → `2147483647`).

The boundary is observable **inside** the Date domain, which is what makes it lockable: `AddDays(d, 4294967303)` (= 2³² + 7) lands exactly 7 days later, and `AddDays(d, 4294967296)` lands on `d` itself. Statically every one of these amounts is accepted (`rule check`, order fixture), so nothing warns.

Three neighbouring behaviors were measured in the same differential rather than reasoned about, because each had a plausible rival reading:

- **An empty direct Number amount field supplies a concrete `0`**, so the source date is stored unchanged — it does *not* make the result valueless. Kernel and interpreter agree on the stored value.
- **An empty field inside an amount expression supplies a concrete result** — `AddDays(d, [Shift] + 1)` with `Shift` empty stores `d + 1`, not "no value".
- **A domain-invalid amount supplies no amount at all** — `AddDays(d, [Shift] / 0)` leaves the target valueless (distinct from the two cases above).

*(Interpreter: `eval/IntNarrowing.plainDecimalAsJavaInt`, applied in `ExprEval.shiftTemporal`; the sub-day units narrow **before** multiplying by the unit. Before this the amount was parsed with Kotlin `toLongOrNull()`, so a fractional amount became NO VALUE and the sub-day units multiplied the un-narrowed `Long`. Locked: `adapter.laws.DateShiftAmountDiffTest` (7 dual-engine cases, each also pinning the kernel value so agreement can't be two nulls) + `interpreter DateShiftAmountTest` (JVM+JS) + `A12ShiftAmountNarrowingTest`, which oracles the lexical narrowing against `java.math.BigDecimal.intValue` itself over 26 literals including the wrap boundaries and amounts beyond every host integer type. Reconciles lean SPEC-2026-07-27-04.)*

## §5 supplement — the operand-list NUMERIC WRAPPERS refuse an IMMEDIATE numeric literal with `MVK_NO_CONST`, and only the immediate child (kernel-oracle matrix, 2026-07-31) <a id="kf167"></a>

> Fold: pending — newly recorded numeric operand-grammar fact; folds into §5 (numbers and decimals) on the next canonical KERNEL-SEMANTICS pass.

`Abs`, `RoundDown`, `RoundUp`, and `RoundAccounting` — the **operand-list** forms, not the field-only `AbsValue`/`Round*Value` family — reject a literal written **directly** as their first operand, in **both** consumers: a rule condition (`rule check`) and a computation operation (`computation add --dry-run`). The refusal is `MVK_NO_CONST`, and the kernel's own summary carries a missing separator after the quoted operator: `At 'Abs'It is not allowed to use constants.`

| operand shape | verdict |
|---|---|
| `Abs(1)`, `RoundDown(1.250, 1)`, `RoundUp(1.250, 1)`, `RoundAccounting(1.250, 1)` | `MVK_NO_CONST` |
| the same wrappers over a field — `Abs([BasePremium])`, `RoundDown([BasePremium], 1)` | accepted |
| a literal **inside** an independently admitted arithmetic subtree — `Abs([BasePremium] - 1)`, `RoundDown([BasePremium] * 2, 1)` | accepted |
| a literal as a **sibling** operand of an outer call — `Min(Abs([BasePremium]), [Adjustment], 1.250)` | accepted, subject to that operator's own one-literal budget |

**So the boundary is a POSITION gate on the immediate child, not "the source must be a field".** That broader reading — which `MVK-LEDGER`'s row carried until this entry corrected it — is wrong in both directions: the operand is an *expression*, so a literal below the wrapper is fine, while a *field* is not required.

**The code is shared by a second, unrelated operand gate, and conflating them is the trap.** A direct temporal literal beneath a `Date(...)`/`Time(...)` component **extractor** (`HoursFromTime("10:30:00")`, `MinutesFromTime`, `SecondsFromTime`) also raises `MVK_NO_CONST` — that is the extractor's *nonliteral-temporal-grammar* rule, recorded in §6 and locked by `TemporalExtractorComponentLawsTest`. **Corrected 2026-07-31 (review):** this entry claimed the **date** extractors are a *third* gate that refuses a quoted literal as `MVK_INVALID_DATE_STRING` rather than `MVK_NO_CONST`. That is **false**, and it was false against a law already committed here — `TemporalExtractorComponentLawsTest` had been asserting `Date(DayFromDate("01.01.2020"), …)` → `MVK_NO_CONST` all along. The claim was generalized from one ISO-formatted example: an observation dressed as a mechanism. **The discriminator is CONVERTIBILITY, and nothing else** — measured on the discriminating pair and locked by `TemporalLiteralFormatGateLawsTest`: a literal that parses as *some* temporal value in the model format (`"01.01.2019"`, `"10:30:45"`) reaches the constant-operand rule → `MVK_NO_CONST`; a literal that parses as nothing (`"2019-01-01"` — ISO is not the model format) trips the string gate first → `MVK_INVALID_DATE_STRING`. Neither the extractor's **family** nor the literal's **kind-match** with it discriminates: `HoursFromTime("01.01.2020")` — a date literal in a time extractor — still converts and is still `MVK_NO_CONST` (that cell was measured *because* a first draft predicted the opposite, from the same over-generalizing habit). So there are **two** gates here, ordered by convertibility, not three. The conclusion the entry drew survives its premise: the *code alone* still cannot say which construct rejected, which is why the operator-coverage guard keys on *operator + operand shape + code + consumer context*.

*(Provenance: matrix run through the real kernel consistency oracle via the `dmtool` binary — `rule check` and `computation add --dry-run` — against a purpose-built two-number-field model, 2026-07-31, kernel 30.8.1. Surfaced by external field feedback whose own account, "sources must be fields", the matrix refutes. Locked: `NumericWrapperConstantLawsTest` (per-wrapper refusal in both consumers, the field operand, the arithmetic-subtree literal, the outer-sibling literal, and the admitted computation forms). Catalog + enricher propagation and the coverage guard are tracked in [`RESULT-CONTRACT-PROPOSAL`](archived/RESULT-CONTRACT-PROPOSAL.md).)*

## §7 supplement — `MVK_NO_INDEX_FIELD` has exactly ONE raise site, so the code alone identifies the construct (source enumeration, 2026-08-01) <a id="kf168"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — a property of the kernel's error-code inventory, not of the DSL's semantics.

**`MVK_NO_INDEX_FIELD` is raised from a single place in the kernel:** `kernel-core-parser`'s `CheckSemanticIndexImpl:125`. Enumerated over the whole consult-only checkout (kernel 30.8.1), every other hit classified: the `ParserErrorTypesEnum` declaration (`:210`), the EN/DE message bundles, and the BA documentation. Nothing else raises it.

**Why that is worth recording as a kernel fact.** Our diagnostic enricher may only attach an operator's meaning to a rejection when the owning construct is *positively established*, after four repairs of the opposite habit (see the class registry in `EnricherOwnershipEvidenceTest`). Two evidence sources were available — the AST operator id, and the operand the kernel **delimits** in its message — and neither reaches this code: the EN template is `For '%s' there is no repeatable group with index field.`, whose only delimited span is a **path**. So tightening election dropped an `explain` that had been **right**, because the discarded sole-*documenter* rule happened to name the correct owner here.

**A unique raise site is a sound basis for the same answer, and it is a different claim from the unsound one.** The sole-documenter rule reasoned from *our catalog's* coverage — a partial map, so one documenter is equally consistent with an undocumented sibling having raised the code (measured: `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` is documented for `MaxValue` and not `MinValue`, and a `MinValue` rejection was duly explained as "the largest of the listed values"). This reasons from the *kernel's source*: if the kernel raises a code from one site, the code determines the construct. Same shape of conclusion, different artifact, and only the second is evidence.

**Admission bar for any further entry** (`DiagnosticEnricher.SOLE_RAISE_SITE`): a shown enumeration over the kernel source with every hit classified as raise site / enum declaration / message-or-doc template, `/build/` and `delombok` trees excluded. Without that, adding a code reproduces the boilerplate-election defect with extra steps.

**Note the coincidence, because it is instructive rather than reassuring:** the unquoted `For` in that same message *is* `SemanticIndex`'s English keyword, which is exactly the fixed-prose match proven not to be evidence (it explained a boolean-vs-string comparison with the meaning of the constant `True`). Here it would have given the right owner for a reason that is wrong everywhere else — the definition of a coincidence, and why the basis had to be replaced rather than kept.

*(Locked: `EnricherOwnershipEvidenceTest.aCodeWithAUniqueKernelRaiseSiteIsOwnedByThatConstruct` — discriminating by construction, since the message delimits no operator; verified to fail with the table entry removed. Catalog side: N7 executes `SemanticIndex`'s `invalidExample` for this code.)*

## §7 supplement — the enricher's collision surface, MEASURED: a keyword-named field is unreferenceable, so the de-election surface is 7 codes and 13 ids (bundle + catalog enumeration + kernel probes, 2026-08-01) <a id="kf169"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — a bound on our own evidence mechanism, derived from the kernel's message inventory.

Four residual doubts about [`KernelMessageOperands`](../rulekit/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/KernelMessageOperands.java) were raised as plausible-but-unverified. Each was settled by one targeted run rather than reported open; **two are refuted outright**, and the surviving one is bounded and safe-by-direction.

**The counts (EN bundle, kernel 30.8.1).** 144 templates carry a `%s`; **52** carry more than one, so a second delimited span can name something other than the operator. Of those 52, only **7** are codes our catalog documents with **≥2** operators — the only situation where a second span can *de-elect* by making `namedAmong` return two candidates: `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1`, `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2`, `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, `MVK_INVALID_PATTERN`, `MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`. 14 have exactly one documenter (a second span can only re-name the same owner) and 30 have none (nothing to elect).

**The entity-position span is a RESOLVED PATH, which is what actually removes the surface.** *Corrected 2026-08-01, same day, before this entry was a day old — see the note below.* Probed on a model with a `DATE` field literally named `Date`: `HoursFromTime(/Order/'Date')` yields `At 'HoursFromTime': Wrong date format at '/Order/Date'.` The second delimited span is the field's **resolved path**, and a path contains `/`, so it can never match `catalog.byId`/`byKeyword` — which take bare identifiers. That holds regardless of what the field is named, so the de-election concern is closed for *every* entity-position template rather than only for keyword-named fields.

> **Correction (2026-08-01):** the first version of this entry claimed "a field named after a DSL keyword cannot be referenced at all", from measuring only the **unquoted** forms (`HoursFromTime(Date)`, `[Date]`, `[/Order/Date]` — all `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`). That is a claim about *my probe's syntax*, not about the language: A12 **quotes** a keyword-named reference, and `['Date']` and `[/Order/'Date']` were then measured **accepted**. The error was reading a parse failure as a reachability result without trying the form the language provides for exactly that case — the "attack every link, don't just measure each one" clause of the reachability oracle. Caught by the `a12-kernel-lean` spec, whose §08 states the quoting rule plainly; the conclusion survived because the real bound is stronger than the one claimed.

The 13 ids that differ from their EN keyword — `Equal`, `NotEqual`, `LessThan(OrEqual)`, `GreaterThan(OrEqual)`, `Arithmetic`, `Concatenation`, `EnumCategory`, `FromScope`, `SemanticIndex`, `ValidType`, `InvalidType` — can name a field *without* quoting (`Abs([Equal])` measured accepted), and the other 97 can with it. Neither matters for election, because neither reaches the catalog through a path-valued span.

**`At '+'` is refuted as reachable.** Every code the catalog documents for `Arithmetic` has a template that delimits nothing (`MVK_INVALID_NUMBER_FOR_EXP`, `MVK_TOO_MANY_DIV_FOR_CALC`, `MVK_TOO_MANY_POW_FOR_CALC`), `Concatenation` documents none, and the mixed-type addition rejection `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_NUMBER_FOR_ADD` — measured live — has no `%s` in its template at all. So `byKeyword("+")`'s ambiguity (`+` is the keyword of *both* `Arithmetic` and `Concatenation`, and the lookup takes the first) cannot surface through the election: no message the catalog can join ever delimits `'+'`.

**The `SpanResolver` false-veto worry is refuted by the veto's own shape.** Measured: `HoursFromTime(Today)` → `At 'HoursFromTime': Wrong date format at 'Today'.` — both spans resolve to catalog operators, so the fear was that `Today` would veto a correct `HoursFromTime` pin. It cannot: `contradicts` is `!named.isEmpty() && !named.contains(candidate)`, so a candidate the message *also* names is never vetoed. **Residual, recorded rather than engineered against:** a template that delimits an entity and *not* the operator, where that entity is one of the 13 legal-as-field-name ids (e.g. `MVK_INDEX_VALUE_INVALID`, `The specified value '%s' for the index field '%s' is invalid.`, over a model with an index field named `Equal`) would veto a correct pin. The failure direction is *withholding* a claim, never asserting a wrong one — which is why the veto is one-directional by design.

**Corrected 2026-08-02 — this was found live and is now closed.** At discovery, `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP` had zero catalog documenters, so `At 'HoursFromTime': Wrong date format at 'Today'.` carried neither `fix` nor `explain`. The measured pre-fix shape remains: 43 of 110 entries carried a primary `invalidExample.error`, covering 27 distinct codes, and `operatorsForError` read only that singleton while ignoring the already-plural, code-keyed, kernel-executed `correctives[]` lane. The root fix made primary invalid examples and correctives one rejection-evidence set for lookup, enrichment, and span fallback; populated executable probes for every `MVK_*` claim then present in gotcha prose; and added a guard deriving its denominator from those authored claims. `HoursFromTime(Today)` now resolves the extractor's own date-format corrective and meaning. The earlier conclusion that the catalog needed a new many-invalid-examples shape was overstated: the plural shape already existed, but its consumers were incomplete.

*(Provenance: enumeration over `kernel-core-parser`'s `error_messages_en.properties` (ISO-8859-1 — a plain `grep` reads it as binary) and the shipped operator catalog, plus five live `RuleValidator` probes against purpose-built models, 2026-08-01, kernel 30.8.1. The keyword-collision and `+` results are enumerations with their exclusions stated; the two refutations are probe-measured.)*

## §4 supplement — the EMPTY `DocumentPointer` is A12's ROOT pointer, not a malformed one (kernel source + display-name evidence, 2026-08-01) <a id="kf170"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — an addressing-type legality fact, not a DSL evaluation semantic.

**`DocumentPointer.of("")` is legal and denotes the document root.** `kernel-md-document-v2`'s `DocumentPointerImpl.parseFullName` (`:95`) returns `Collections.emptyList()` for an empty `fullName` after stripping the redundant leading slash, so the pointer carries **zero** path parts, and `toString` (`:154`) renders that value as the literal `(empty DocumentPointer)`. A zero-arity pointer is therefore a value the kernel constructs, names, and prints.

**The display name is the discriminating evidence, and it is what a length check alone would miss.** An empty part list is by itself ambiguous — it is equally consistent with "the root" and with "a degenerate value nobody meant to build". The kernel writing a *human-readable name* for it is not: no implementation spends a branch labelling a value it considers malformed. That is the observation that separates the two readings, and it is available from source without a probe.

**Why it is recorded rather than assumed.** An abandoned defect-prevention draft listed `format("", [])` → `""` as one of `A12PointerFormat`'s defects and proposed rejecting zero arity — which would have made A12's root pointer unrepresentable at our boundary, breaking CLAUDE.md's first interop rule *inside a document about preventing defects*. The claim was reached by pattern-matching the other four asymmetries (all genuine) rather than by asking what A12 does, which is the exact failure mode the client-side-refusal rule exists for: **a refusal we invent is invisible, because the kernel never gets asked.**

*(Locked: `interpreter … A12PointerSymmetryTest.theRootPointerIsLegalOnBothEntryPoints` pins it on all four entry points of the two pointer types, and `formatCanExpressEveryPointerParseAccepts` fails on any over-rejection of the class — verified red when zero arity is refused. `A12PointerSyntax.splitNames` carries the reason inline so the absent special case is not read as an oversight. Amended 2026-08-01: the root's **other** spelling, a bare `"/"`, was over-rejected — see [kf171](#kf171).)*


## §4 supplement — A12's MESSAGE pointer is a THIRD addressing type, and two of its rules are enforced only by a Java `assert` (kernel-factory-measured, 2026-08-01) <a id="kf171"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — an addressing-type legality fact, not a DSL evaluation semantic.

**A12 has three pointer types, not two, and the third is the one messages use.** `DocumentPointer` (exact) and `DocumentMultiPointer` (wildcard `0` at every part) were already recorded in [kf1](#kf1). The third is **`PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer`**, and it is what `IMessage` actually returns from `getErrorFieldPointer()`, `getReferencedFieldsPointers()` and `getRefOmissionErrorResponsiblePointers()`, and what `ICustomCondition.check` receives as its error entity. Its state is **not** a part list: `PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointerImpl` stores a name list and an index list, and publishes exactly `fullName()` and `repetitionIndexes()`. Its domain is an index `>= 1`, `WILDCARD` (`0`) at **any** part, or `UNKNOWN` (`-5`, `IIdentifier.UNKNOWN`) at any part. `toDocumentPointer()` converts only when every index before the last is `> WILDCARD` and the last is `>= WILDCARD`.

**`fullName()` differs between the two types for the root, and that mattered.** `DocumentPointer.fullName()` maps each part to `"/" + name` and joins, so the root is `""`. `PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointerImpl.fullName()` uses `joining("/", "/", "")`, so the root is `"/"`. A shared splitter that refuses a bare `"/"` therefore cannot read back the partially-known type's own accessor — which is how the over-rejection below was found, by building the type rather than by reading either class.

**A bare `"/"` is legal input, and refusing it is an over-rejection.** `parseFullName`'s trailing-slash guard is `if (!"/".equals(fullName) && fullName.endsWith("/"))` — it excludes exactly that one string by name, then strips the leading slash and reads the empty remainder as the root's zero parts. Our shared splitter had `require(path != "/" && !path.endsWith("/"))`, refusing it. Measured, not read: `A12PointerKernelDomainDiffTest` decides `"/"` on both sides.

**Two of the partially-known type's rules are enforced by a bare Java `assert`, i.e. not at all in production.** `checkValidity()` asserts `noneMatch(val -> val < 0 && val != UNKNOWN)`, so a `-1`/`-4`/`-6`/`-7` index is refused under `-ea` (Gradle's test JVMs) and **accepted silently** with assertions off. And the constructor never compares the name count against the repetition count, although `toDocumentPointer()`'s own comment asserts the opposite — "we do not need to check the length of both lists, as we only construct valid instances" — and would then throw inside a `try` whose `catch` says the throw is not expected. Both are measured, and our own type is **deliberately stricter on both**, which is allowed only because the reason is written down (CLAUDE.md "A client-side refusal needs a measured kernel basis"): we enforce the documented domain and the invariant the owner's comment claims.

**Amended 2026-08-02 — A12 gives its pointer entry points DIFFERENT domains, and the first version of this entry did not say so.** Measured against the factories themselves rather than read:

| entry point | validates | example it accepts |
|---|---|---|
| `PathPart.of(name, index)` | non-empty name, `index >= 0` — **nothing else** | `PathPart.of("a/b", 1)`, `PathPart.of("Order$", 1)` |
| `DocumentPointer.of(List<PathPart>)` | the position rule only | `of(List.of(PathPart.of("a/b",1)))` → `fullName` `/a/b` |
| `DocumentPointer.of(String)` | `([\w_-]+)(\[(\d+)?\])?` **plus** the position rule | — (`of("Order$")` raises `IllegalStateException: No match found`) |
| `PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer.of(fullName, reps)` | splitting and normalization; **no** name rule | `"/Order$"`, `"/Order Item"`, `"/Order[2]"`, `"/Order//Items"` |

So `[\w_-]+` is the **string codec's** precondition — what may be *written* so a pointer parses back to the same value — and not a statement about which names a pointer value may hold. Applying it to the value factories was an over-rejection at four entry points, and the sharper half is the READ path: an over-strict part factory makes a legal kernel value **unreadable**, not merely unauthorable.

**Amended 2026-08-02 — `toDocumentPointer()` RAISES on the root.** It evaluates `repetitions.subList(0, repetitions.size() - 1)` inside its `if` condition, outside its own `try`, so an empty index list raises before any `Optional` is built: measured `IllegalArgumentException: fromIndex(0) > toIndex(-1)`. `PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer.of("", List.of())` itself is accepted, so the value is constructible and only its conversion is unreachable. Our counterpart raises too; repairing the edge would make the method something other than the counterpart it is documented as.

**Amended 2026-08-02 — `toDocumentPointer()` is ASSERTION-DEPENDENT on exactly one shape, and its own `catch` is reachable.** The kernel comments that the `catch` cannot fire ("not expected to fail after our checks succeed"); it can. `PathPartImpl`'s constructor raises a real `IllegalArgumentException` for an **empty part name**, and an empty name is reachable from the partially-known type because its factory validates splitting only — `of("/Order//Items", [1,1,1])` is a legal value whose middle name is `""`. The conversion catches that, runs `assert false`, and returns `Optional.empty()`. Measured in both modes (child classloader with `setDefaultAssertionStatus(false)`, from inside an `-ea` suite):

| input | `-ea` | `-da` (production) |
|---|---|---|
| `of("/Order//Items", [1,1,1]).toDocumentPointer()` | `AssertionError` | `Optional.empty()` |
| `of("//Items", [1,1]).toDocumentPointer()` | `AssertionError` | `Optional.empty()` |
| `of("/Order//Items", [1,0,1]).toDocumentPointer()` | `Optional.empty()` | `Optional.empty()` |
| every other corpus case, root included | identical | identical |

The third row is the one worth keeping: it looks like the same interior-empty shape but its interior `0` fails the **position rule first**, so the method answers without constructing a part and never reaches the assert. The two guards are ordered. A case list derived by reading the source got this wrong; running both modes got it right — which is why the mode split is now a pinned assertion rather than prose. **Production is the contract**: our `toExactPointer` answers `null` for an unnameable level, and the `-ea` observation is recorded here rather than lent to it.

**Amended 2026-08-02 — arity is per-entry-point too.** A12's *exact* factory takes the same-looking `(fullName, repetitions)` pair and **does** pair them — `PathPartImpl.toPathPartList` refuses "path and repetitions have different length" — while the partially-known factory accepts `("/Plan/Demand", [1])`. So "does A12 require one index per part?" has no single answer; it is checked at one entry point and not the other, which is the same lesson as the name grammar one row up.

**Amended 2026-08-02 — A12 defines NO pointer string for this type.** Enumerated over the whole checkout (`grep -rn 'PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer' --include='*.java' . | grep -v /build/ | grep -v delombok`): ten files mention the type, and filtering those hits for `parse|toString|render|format|valueOf|of(String` returns **nothing**. Its four factories take a `DocumentPointer`, `(String fullName, List<Integer>)`, `(String fullName, int[])` or an `IEntityInstance`; its only `String` producers are `fullName()` (names only, no indices) and a debug `toString`. A `format`/`parse`/`render` trio we shipped for it on 2026-08-01 was therefore an invented serialization of an A12-owned domain object, and is deleted.

**The consequence for us, and the defect it explains.** One type cannot serve both channels. A single hybrid `A12Pointer` had admitted the *union* — `-5` on a pointer documented as exact, which `DocumentPointerImpl`'s own part pattern (`([\w_-]+)(\[(\d+)?\])?`, unsigned) cannot even read — while still enforcing the exact type's last-part-only `0`. That union is a shape A12 has nowhere, so it belonged to neither channel, and no test could see it because neither side had ever asked A12 what it accepts.

*(Provenance: source read of the consult-only `../a12-kernel` checkout, kernel 30.8.1 — `PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer`, `PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointerImpl`, `DocumentPointer`, `DocumentPointerImpl`, `PathPartImpl`, `DocumentMultiPointer`, `IMessage`, `MessageImpl`, `DocumentPointerConverter`, `ICustomCondition` — with every accept/reject and shape claim then **decided by the real factories** rather than by the reading. Locked: `adapter … laws/A12PointerKernelDomainDiffTest`, **partitioned by entry point** (2026-08-02) so no factory is used as the oracle for another — `PathPart.of`, `DocumentPointer.of(List)`, `DocumentPointer.of(String)`, `PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer.of(fullName, reps)` and `toDocumentPointer` (root included) each get their own corpus. It also asserts the two deliberate divergences **as** divergences so a kernel upgrade that closes them fails the build, records whether `-ea` is actually in force, and carries a positive control proving the corpora discriminate the entry points. Shape parity and the interpreter-side rules: `interpreter … A12PointerDomainTest`, `A12PointerSymmetryTest`.)*

## §9/§12 supplement — starred `FieldValuesNotUnique` is one anchored aggregate message, not one message per duplicate row (runtime differential, 2026-08-04) <a id="kf172"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — this rich-message cardinality, address, reference-set, and polarity contract is outside the truth-semantics summary.

For `FieldValuesNotUnique(/Order/Items*/Count)` over `Count = [7, 5, 5]`, both dynamic Groovy and generated Java emit exactly one `VALUE_ERROR` at `/Order[1]/Items[1]/Count`. Its `getReferencedFieldsPointers()` contains `/Order[1]/Items[0]/Count`: the star remains A12's wildcard coordinate in the rich message channel even though the error is pinned to the first row. Moving the rule from `/Order/Items` to `/Order` while retaining the same error entity does not change this result. A `Sum(/Order/Items*/Count) > -1` sibling proves that the wildcard reference is an aggregate-message law rather than a uniqueness special case.

dmtool converts the retained exact message address to canonical A12 exact syntax, which has no leading slash and omits `[1]`, so `/Order[1]/Items[1]/Count` appears as `Order/Items/Count`. The separate interpreter house form retains `/Order[1]/Items[1]/Count`. The canonical spelling is still the first-row cell, not a declaration-only pointer. `RepetitionNotUnique` is the operator for per-row duplicate attribution and emits at the duplicate rows instead.

A `Having` filter and the starred value operand both contribute to `getReferencedFieldsPointers()`, all at the wildcarded aggregate level. `CurrentRepetition` is structural and adds no pointer. A group-star recursively contributes its contained fields rather than a group pointer, and the filtered category `EntitySpec` shape contributes its source and filter fields. The collection is set-valued; its `HashSet` iteration order is incidental and not part of the contract. A firing filtered `FieldValuesNotUnique` over a **sole** starred operand is `OMISSION_ERROR` (`TRUE_AF`) even when the kept values are filled; the unfiltered form is `VALUE_ERROR`. **Qualified 2026-08-05:** that holds for the sole-starred shape measured here because the first kept value necessarily precedes the duplicate. The escalation is not a property of the operand list — [KF175](#kf175) measures it as positional, so a filter authored after the duplicate leaves the firing `VALUE_ERROR`.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest.starredDuplicateEmitsOneFirstRowMessageWithItsWildcardReferenceInBothPlacements` covers both placements, ordinary and `CurrentRepetition` filters, cardinality, polarity, error pointer, and rich reference-set content across both kernel strategies; `StarOnlyIterationScopeDiffTest.ownGroupStarOnlyCarriesItsWildcardReference` is the independent aggregate control; `OuterCorrelationDiffTest.outerCorrelationMessageReferencesKeepTheOuterRowAndWildcardTheInnerScope` decides the `$`-outer/current-row versus inner-wildcard projection; `GroupStarValueAggregateDiffTest.validationMessageOverGroupStarRecursivelyReferencesExpandedFieldsNotGroups` and `FirstFilledValueEntitySpecDiffTest.filteredCategoryEntitySpecRetainsItsHavingStarAndFallbackMessageReferences` lock the two AST-shape siblings. Interpreter reconciliation: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF247.)*

## §9 supplement — `FieldValuesNotUnique` operand admission: more than one operand, one comparability CATEGORY, and one identical declared FORMAT among temporal operands (kernel `rule check`, 2026-08-04; corrected + extended 2026-08-05) <a id="kf173"></a>

> Fold: folded → §9.

Canonical in §9 "Other repetition rules". [KF100](#kf100) recorded this operator's *runtime* rules and left its **admission** gates unstated; the reconciliation requests that surfaced them are the lean project's `SPEC-2026-08-04-02` and `SPEC-2026-08-05-02`, and every verdict below was reproduced here against the real kernel rather than accepted as reported.

**Arity — more than one operand, unless one starred slot already supplies many.** A single **direct** field is rejected **`MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`**; a single **starred** slot (`Counts*/Counted`) is admitted, because it expands to a row stream. A mixed direct-plus-starred list of one kind is admitted. This is the param law already recorded for the count aggregates ("Param law is shared with the count aggregates"), now measured for this operator too.

**Which kinds at all. Corrected 2026-08-10:** **Boolean, Confirm, and DateRange** are refused outright, with **`MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_DATE_ALLOWED`**; every other field kind is admissible, Custom included. A Boolean or Confirm beside a String reports *that* code and not the mixing one, so the kind gate runs ahead of the category gate. A singleton Confirm still reports the earlier arity gate, `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`. DateRange is refused although it carries a declared format, so it is excluded by kind rather than by the format rule below.

**One comparability CATEGORY — and this gate is STRICTER than token comparability.** Mixing categories draws **`MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`**. The measured categories are **{String, Custom}**, **{Enumeration}**, **{Number}**, and the temporal family **{Date, Time, DateTime, DateFragment}**: Number beside String, String beside Enumeration, Enumeration beside String, Date beside String and Date beside Number are all rejected (both orders measured for the Enumeration pair, because a gate reading only the first operand would pass one of them), while Custom beside String is **admitted** and two Custom fields of *different* registered type names are admitted — the type name is not part of the gate. Differing Number **scales** stay admitted, which is what makes KF100's cross-scale equality claim (a scale-0 `5` equals a scale-2 `5.00`) reachable at all.

**Inside the temporal category, one identical declared FORMAT string** — else `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_DATE_ALLOWED` again. Two Dates at `dd.MM.yyyy` are admitted; the same pair differing only in declared format is refused; two DateFragments at `MM` are admitted and `MM` beside `yyyy` is refused. A Date beside a Time is refused on this gate and **not** on the category gate (measured as a pre-emption, both codes checked), which is what places Time and DateTime inside the temporal category rather than outside it.

**Corrected 2026-08-05:** the `SPEC-2026-08-05-02` reading — "DATE is the only admitted temporal kind" and the same-format rule is Date-local — is **refuted at the observable**. Time, DateTime and DateFragment pairs are all admitted, and the format gate spans the whole temporal family. The heading's earlier "ONE declared kind" is likewise corrected to one *category*: Custom beside String is admitted, so declared-kind equality is not necessary.

**Each gate's SCOPE is the whole operand list, so authored position never selects the reported class (added 2026-08-06, Confirm sibling added 2026-08-10).** The pre-emption above says which gate wins; this says over what span each runs. An operand offending the kind gate, or the format gate, still reports **`MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_DATE_ALLOWED`** when an earlier-authored operand already mixes categories, and `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED` is absent. `(Date, String, Boolean)`, `(Date, String, Confirm)`, and `(Number, String, Boolean)` report the kind code although operand 2 is the category mismatch; `(Date@dd.MM.yyyy, String, Date@yyyy-MM-dd)` reports the format code across an intervening mismatch; each has a reordered control. A fail-fast reading reports the mixing class on the non-reordered rows and is therefore refuted. The consequence is diagnostic-only, which matters to an importer deciding repairability and to an Explain surface naming the failure.

**The two discriminating cases, and why neither gate is a shared field-list contract.** (1) `NumberOfDifferentValues(BinCode, Condition)` — the *same* String-beside-Enumeration pair over the *same* two fields — is **admitted**, so the two field-list operators do not share one comparability rule; the rejection alone would not have shown it, since a kernel that refused the mix everywhere satisfies the rejection half equally well. (2) A **Date declared `yyyy` beside a DateFragment declared `yyyy` is ADMITTED** — two different kinds, one identical format — while two Dates differing only in format are refused. That pair is what decides the temporal gate reads the **format** and not the kind; a kind-equality reading gets both rows wrong. The Number cross-scale admission is the third control: it keeps the format rule temporal-local rather than a general same-declaration rule.

**A GROUP-scope operand is ADMITTED, and the two gates disagree about what a group is (added 2026-08-12).** The operator takes a group operand exactly as the entity-list aggregates do (`AggregateOperand`, `4b244b84`): a non-repeatable group whose expansion is two Strings, and a starred repeatable group whose expansion is two same-scale Numbers, are both accepted. The split is the finding, and it needed two discriminators because the homogeneous admissions alone are equally consistent with a kernel that type-checks nothing it reaches through a group:

- **Arity reads the authored slots and treats a group as already-many.** A group whose expansion is a *single* field is **admitted**, while that same field authored directly is `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`. So the arity gate does not count the expansion — a group slot satisfies the param law alone, exactly as a starred slot does.
- **The category gate reads the expansion.** A group expanding to a Number beside a String draws the ordinary `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`, so the expansion really is type-checked.
- **At RUNTIME the expansion is RECURSIVE and crosses a nested repeatable subgroup's ROWS, with no star written anywhere (added 2026-08-13).** A non-repeatable group holding one direct String plus a repeatable subgroup carrying one String fires on a duplicate pairing the direct field with the subgroup's **second** row, and equally on one lying between two of the subgroup's rows — both on both kernel strategies. One direct field cannot duplicate itself, which is what makes the fixture's verdict a statement about the extent rather than about the direct fields. So the compared set is every `(row × field)` cell reachable below the operand, and an implicit iteration is introduced by the *model's* repeatability, not by the authored `*`. This is the case [IF287](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if287) was extended for: the interpreter read the nested repetition at a single row-1 coordinate and missed both duplicates. **Extended the same day: the recursion has no measured depth limit and applies to an authored star too.** Two stacked repeatable levels below the operand fire on a duplicate at `Handovers[2]/Scans[2]` — neither level at its first row — and on one spanning two `Handovers` rows; a `Consignments*` operand whose group holds a repeatable `Marks` subgroup fires both within one outer row's later `Marks` row and across two outer rows' `Marks`. Negative controls on both fixtures stay silent, so the compared set is bounded by the operand rather than by any level. **And the scope does not depend on where the RULE sits (measured the same day).** With the rule authored on the repeatable `/Shipment/Depot/Handovers` and the operand naming its ancestor `/Shipment/Depot`, a duplicate spanning two different `Handovers` rows fires; the compared set is bounded by the **operand**, not by the rule's own row, with a within-one-row control to show the rule evaluates. So the clause needs no qualifier for depth, for an authored star, or for rule placement.

This was measured because rulekit *could not author it*: `Dsl.fieldValuesNotUnique` took a `FieldOperand`, which permits `FieldRef`/`FieldListRef` but not `GroupListRef` — a **type-level erasure with no kernel basis**, one `permits` clause away from the slot `4b244b84` widened for the aggregates. The entry was written expecting a mirror of `NumberOfFilledGroups`' `MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`; the kernel refuted that. The same measurement also contradicts a refusal the a12-kernel-lean project had scoped as its next unit, handed back 2026-08-12.

**Both halves are closed the same day, and the authoring half was blocked on a real evaluator defect rather than on effort.** `Dsl.fieldValuesNotUnique(GroupListRef, …)` now authors the shape (DG33), but only after the interpreter was measured on it: both kernel strategies fire on a duplicate among the expanded cells and the interpreter returned FALSE, so widening first would have admitted a form that was silently answered wrong — [IF287](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if287) carries the two causes and the fix. Admitting an authoring shape whose evaluator disagrees is worse than the erasure it replaces, because a wrong answer is silent where a missing factory is not.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.FieldValuesNotUniqueAdmissionLawsTest`: arity, category admissions/rejections, the three outright-refused kinds, Confirm singleton precedence, Boolean/Confirm beside String pre-emption, temporal formats, the cross-kind same-format discriminator, the `NumberOfDifferentValues` contrast, whole-list scope in both operand orders, and the four group-operand rows with their two field-authored controls. The scope discriminators use `rejectsModelInsteadOf`, so the pre-empted code is asserted absent rather than merely unmentioned. The catalog carries the gates as constraints plus a `FORMAT-not-kind` gotcha, with CI-executed corrective and invalid-example probes.)*

## §11 supplement — the computed-target scale gate is the COMPARISON predicate, not equality (kernel `checkConsistency`, 2026-08-04) <a id="kf174"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

Canonical in §11 "Computations". The reconciliation request is the lean project's `SPEC-2026-08-04-01`; every verdict below was reproduced here rather than accepted as reported.

**The defect was an internal contradiction, not a missing fact.** §11 stated that a computed field's declared scale must *equal* the calculation's scale, while §5's scale summary in the same document already defined the constant-capability model the comparison gate uses. Both cannot hold, and the mechanism says which: a computation *is* also a validation rule (§11's first bullet), so its admission runs the implicit `[target] != <operation>` comparison through the ordinary `==`/`!=` gate. Equality was the observed special case mistaken for the rule.

| operation (`Rate`, `Factor`, `Surcharge` all scale 2) | derived scale | capable | declared 0 | declared 2 | declared 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[Rate] * 100` (integer literal strips to signed scale −2) | 0 | yes | admitted | **admitted** | **admitted** |
| `[Rate] * 1.50` | 4 | yes | rejected | rejected | admitted |
| `[Rate] * [Factor]` | 4 | no | rejected | **rejected** | admitted |
| `[Rate] + [Surcharge]` | 2 | no | rejected | admitted | rejected |

**Two discriminating pairs, because two rival readings each explain part of the table.** Row 1 versus row 2 kills "any mismatch is fine while capable": the same capability admits the *smaller* derived scale and rejects the *larger*, so direction is load-bearing and padding only ever adds digits. Row 1 versus row 4 **at declared scale 4** kills "the gate reads the scales": both derive a scale *smaller* than declared, and the verdict turns purely on capability — the capable `[Rate] * 100` is admitted, the capability-free `[Rate] + [Surcharge]` rejected. Row 4 also shows why addition is capability-free at all: it needs *both* operands capable, so two plain fields have none. *(Corrected 2026-08-11 after review: this sentence named **row 2 versus row 3 at declared scale 2** and said the verdict flips there. It does not, and this entry's own table already said so — derived 4 against declared 2 is a *larger* scale, so both rows are rejected, exactly as `ComputedTargetScaleLawsTest` asserts. The capability fact was always measured; the prose named a pair that does not discriminate it.)*

Every rejection is `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` **alone** (the lock asserts the whole emitted code list, not its membership), which is also why the computation's `errorCodesToSuppress` escape hatch reaches the no-fit store arm described in the §11 five-channel supplement.

**Nothing was over-rejected client-side.** `Computation` defers scale to the kernel by design (D13) and carries no scale check, so a capability-carrying computation has always been authorable through the typed surface; the wrong statement lived only in the prose.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.ComputedTargetScaleLawsTest` — the four operations across the three declared scales, including both discriminating pairs. The pairs' names in that file are `aCapableOperationIsAdmittedAtItsOwnDeclaredScale`/`aCapableSmallerDerivedScaleIsAdmittedAtALargerDeclaredScale` versus `aCapableLargerDerivedScaleIsRejected` for direction, and the same capable row versus `anAdditionOfTwoFieldsIsAdmittedOnlyAtItsExactScale` for capability.)*

## §9 supplement — `FieldValuesNotUnique` consumes its operand list as an ORDERED scan that returns at the first duplicate, and an unconvertible cell is SKIPPED rather than suppressing (runtime differential, 2026-08-05) <a id="kf175"></a>

> Fold: folded → §9.

Canonical in §9 "Other repetition rules". The reconciliation request is the lean project's `SPEC-2026-08-05-01`, which reported the OMISSION escalation as **positional**; that half is **confirmed**, and measuring it turned up a second fact the entry does not raise. Both were reproduced here against the real kernel, and both were live defects in the interpreter ([IF249](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if249)).

**The escalation is positional.** A `Having` on an operand escalates a firing to `OMISSION_ERROR` only through a cell that **actually yielded a compared value**, and only when that happened **before** the duplicate the scan returned at. Measured on a mixed direct-plus-starred list, whose admission [KF173](#kf173) establishes:

| list order | the filtered operand contributes | measured | the whole-list reading predicts |
|---|---|---|---|
| duplicate first, filter after | (never reached) | `VALUE_ERROR` | `OMISSION_ERROR` |
| filter first, duplicate after | present values | `OMISSION_ERROR` | `OMISSION_ERROR` |
| filter first, duplicate after | only empty cells | `VALUE_ERROR` | `OMISSION_ERROR` |
| filter first, duplicate after | only unconvertible cells | `VALUE_ERROR` | `OMISSION_ERROR` |

Rows 1, 3 and 4 are the discriminators; row 2 is the positive control, without which "always `VALUE_ERROR`" would satisfy the other three. Row 4 additionally fixes where the escalation sits relative to the skip below: a **present but unconvertible** cell does not escalate, so the flag follows the compared value and not mere specifiedness. Every previously measured shape had one starred operand and nothing after it, which is why the positional and whole-list readings had never separated.

**An unconvertible cell is SKIPPED, exactly like an empty one — it does NOT suppress the rule.** This corrects the "a formally-invalid operand suppresses" claim carried by [KF100](#kf100) and §9. A stored value that cannot be converted to the declared type contributes nothing, in a starred operand and in a direct one alike, and a duplicate elsewhere in the list still fires. What makes this a skip rather than a fire-anyway is that **two *equal* unconvertible values do not duplicate** — neither ever enters the comparison. Measured on Number and on Date, so the rule is not Number-local.

The earlier claim was not contradicted by evidence but never separated from it: the case behind it (`FieldValuesNotUnique(Quantity, BackorderQuantity)` with `Quantity = "abc"`, `BackorderQuantity = "5"`) has no duplicate once `"abc"` leaves the comparison, so "does not fire" is the same observation under both readings, and the lock asserted only a boolean.

**Two rival mechanisms were refuted rather than left standing.** The kernel's runtime branches on whether the *first* operand is a direct Number field, which would predict stored-text comparison when a starred operand leads; measured both ways round, a scale-0 `5` duplicates a scale-2 `5.00` regardless of operand order, so no order-selected comparison mode is observable. And the skip is not temporal-specific: the Date rows behave exactly as the Number rows.

**The temporal compared identity IS the stored text; its agreement with the decoded date is an ASSUMPTION that holds for every format probed (added 2026-08-06, amended the same day on the entry's second pass, `SPEC-2026-08-05-03`).** The entry reports the runtime's non-Number branch as collecting `feldWertIntern`, the stored value text, which differs from the decoded date `NumberOfDifferentValues` reads over the same lists; it names the separating class as *two distinct stored texts under one identical declared format whose decoded values are equal*, and asks for a witness or a report that none is storable. **None is storable.** The kernel's date input check is width-exact and blank-intolerant, so every candidate is refused at the cell before any comparison: a single-digit day or month is `datumFormatFalsch` under `dd.MM.yyyy` and under `yyyy-MM-dd`, a leading blank is `fuehrendesBlank`, a trailing blank `nachfolgendesBlank`, a padded or signed year `datumFormatFalsch`. Under one declared format — which this operator's admission gate requires of a temporal list ([KF173](#kf173)) — the surviving texts map one-to-one onto decoded dates, so the two accounts agree on every storable temporal list. The stored-text reading therefore stays a **source-level reading, not a measured mechanism**, and an implementation is conformant either way; this is recorded rather than asserted so a future reader does not mistake the agreement for evidence. *Correction to the wording this project handed back at `ddaf2e13`: the cross-scale row above refutes an order-selected comparison **mode**, and that is all it does — it says nothing about which domain a temporal operand is compared in, because no Number operand is involved in a temporal list. The entry is right that those are two claims and only the first was measured; the paragraph above is scoped to what the row shows.*

**Amended 2026-08-06 (the entry's second pass).** Saying this was "not decidable from behavior" over-claimed in the other direction: that is an unreachability claim, and what is measured is a refusal at every format probed, not a proof that no witness exists. Two candidates the first pass never reached are now measured. At the **widest legal temporal format** — a bare `yyyy`, legal per [KF176](#kf176) — `2020` beside `02020`, `+2020` and ` 2020` is refused (`datumFormatFalsch`, `datumFormatFalsch`, `fuehrendesBlank`), with identical text firing and a different year silent as controls. And across the **cross-kind** pair the format gate admits — a `Date` declared `yyyy` beside a `DateFragment` declared `yyyy` ([KF173](#kf173)) — identical text fires and both near-misses are refused, so the two kinds' input checkers do not differ in the leniency a witness would require; that also confirms KF173's cross-kind admission at RUNTIME rather than only at authoring. The **static-Java** leg the entry asked us to co-check agrees with dynamic Groovy on all eight rows, as does the interpreter — whose own keying compares the **decoded ISO form**, not stored text, making it a characterized non-divergence rather than a match: unobservable for every storable input probed, and the reason the assumption is stated rather than assumed. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest.noStorableTemporalPairSeparatesStoredTextFromItsDecodedValue`.)*

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest.noNearMissTemporalTextIsStorableSoTextAndDecodedIdentityAgree` — the five near-miss rows with their exact refusal codes, plus the exact-format fire and the different-date silence as positive and negative controls, so "every row reports a formal error" cannot be satisfied by a fixture whose rule never fires.)*

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest.theOperandScanIsOrderedAndReturnsAtTheDuplicate` — a 15-row matrix, each row pinning the fired message type against dynamic Groovy and requiring the full `field|type|referenced` triple to agree across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter. `everyAuthorableTemporalKindComparesOnItsValue` and `customTypedOperandsCompareOnTheirAcceptedValue` cover the remaining admissible kinds — Time, DateFragment and Custom — each with an equal/distinct pair whose equal half must fire, so the agreement cannot be satisfied by two engines that both never compare the kind.)*

## §6 supplement — a `DateType` whose declared FORMAT omits calendar components is legal, and its value DECODES to a completed date (kernel `checkConsistency` + runtime probe, 2026-08-06) <a id="kf176"></a>

> Fold: folded → §6.

Canonical in §6 "Dates and time". Found while reconciling the lean project's `SPEC-2026-08-05-03`, whose near-miss probe needed a `yyyy`-formatted date field — the entry itself does not raise this.

**The shape, and how it differs from the two partial shapes already recorded.** A `DateType` may declare a format carrying only some components — `yyyy`, `yyyy-MM`, `yyyyMM`, `MM` — with a value that is *complete for that format* (`2020`). That is neither a `DateFragmentType` (a different declared field type, §6 "a date fragment IS a DateOperand") nor a `datePrecision` of `DAY_OPTIONAL`/`MONTH_OPTIONAL`/`YEAR_OPTIONAL` (whose format is complete while the stored value omits components as literal zeros, `00.06.2024` — §6's partial-`DateType` paragraph). All three exist independently, and only the first two were recorded.

**The admitted format set is a WHITELIST, not a compositional rule.** Measured on ten candidates through the kernel consistency gate: `yyyy`, `yyyy-MM`, `yyyyMM`, `MM` are admitted alongside the complete `yyyy-MM-dd`/`dd.MM.yyyy`, while `MM.yyyy`, `MM-yyyy`, `yyyy/MM` and `dd.MM` are refused *"The date format is invalid"*. The discriminator against every compositional reading is that the **separator** flips the verdict on the same two components — `yyyy-MM` admitted, `yyyy/MM` refused — while a complete format uses `.` freely; component order alone fails too, since `MM` is admitted where `dd.MM` is not. So the domain must not be inferred: a consumer takes whatever format a kernel-accepted model declares. The refusal is a **model-consistency** failure, not a rule diagnostic, and the draft builder does not screen it.

**An extractor for an omitted component is refused at codegen**, `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_…`: `yyyy` admits only `YearFromDate`; `yyyy-MM` and `yyyyMM` admit `YearFromDate` and `MonthFromDate`; `MM` admits only `MonthFromDate`; `DayFromDate` is refused by all four. The complete-format control admits all three, so the refusals are not a broken fixture. This is why the completion is **not** observable through the extractors — the components a completion would supply are exactly the ones no extractor may read.

**The value decodes: the field behaves as a concrete date.** On a `yyyy` field holding `2020`, the kernel evaluates `[A] == [B]` (fires for `2020`/`2020`, silent for `2020`/`2021`), `[A] < [B]` (fires for `2020` < `2021`), `YearFromDate(A) == YearFromDate(B)`, and `FieldValuesNotUnique(A, B)` — none of which a non-relevant cell would reach. The oracle is the dynamic-Groovy runtime through `RuntimeLaws.messages`. **The completion fills month and day to `01` and a missing year from the model's BASE YEAR** — measured against a full-format sibling, since the extractor gate above closes the cheap route: `yyyy` `2020` → `2020-01-01`, `yyyy-MM` `2020-03` → `2020-03-01`, and `MM` `03` under a non-default base year 2011 → `2011-03-01` while 2000, 1970 and 2026 all fail, so the year source is discriminated rather than assumed. Second instance of the same mechanism: with no base year declared, comparing a `MM` field is refused outright (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`), so the base year is a precondition and not a mere default.

**Interpreter status: reproduced in full** ([IF250](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if250)). It previously matched neither half, and the contradiction was inside this project: `FormalError`'s FULL branch carried an exact `fmt == "yyyy"` completion (added for `SPEC-2026-07-27-13`'s `Date(...)` year-component source, with a comment declining to infer the others) while `Document.resolveValue`'s FULL branch called `parseDateToIso` alone — so the formal gate admitted a `yyyy` cell that resolution could not produce, suppressing every referencing rule, and the other three formats drew a false `datumFormatFalsch`.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.IncompleteDateFormatAdmissionLawsTest` — the ten-format whitelist table and the fifteen-row extractor-admission matrix, each asserted as a whole table so a kernel upgrade reports every shifted row at once, plus the where-does-it-refuse pin. `adapter.laws.IncompleteDateFormatDiffTest` — the runtime half: the completion-target table with its year-source discriminator, the admitted extractor, two-directional ordering, presence, and a malformed-for-its-own-format control, each asserting kernel/interpreter agreement AND what the kernel itself reported.)*

## §11 supplement — in COMPUTATION a fixed multi-group filled count counts a formally-unavailable group as FILLED, the opposite of its validation reading (tri-engine compute probe, 2026-08-06) <a id="kf177"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — the canonical §11 prose states the poison rule generally; this is an operator-specific exception whose scope is one count family, and folding it would make the general rule read as conditional. Cite it from §11 instead.

**Answers a12-kernel-lean `EXP-2026-08-06-01`, and the answer is none of the three outcomes it weighted.** For a **seeded** Number target computed *directly* from a fixed multi-group `NumberOfFilledGroups(Alpha, Beta)` with one operand group carrying malformed-only content, the target takes **`VALUE|2`** — the unavailable group is counted as **filled**. Not `VALUE|0`, not `CLEARED`, and not the `ERRORED` the general third-state rule predicts. Both required controls are present and both behave: all-clean-filled gives the exact `VALUE|2`, all-clean-empty the exact `VALUE|0`, so the malformed row cannot be an inert fixture; and a separate validation control on the *same* document confirms the cell really is formally unavailable (`zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` under `fractionDigits(1, 1)`), without which the whole observation would be worthless. Dynamic Groovy, generated Java and the interpreter agree on every row — no codegen split.

**This INVERTS the validation reading of the same operator on the same document.** In validation an erroneous operand group makes the plain multi-group count *unavailable* rather than counting the admitted subset (the §9 group-presence finding, `GroupPresenceFormalInvalidDiffTest`). So the two operations disagree about whether a malformed-only group is filled, and a consumer that carries one reading into the other gets it backwards.

**The mechanism, source-supported and now discriminated rather than asserted.** `CalculationCache.convertInvalidFieldsForCalculation` resets the erroneous-entity marking for the calculation pass — in the kernel's own words, the invalid entities are converted "so that nothing is treated as irrelevant, but causes exceptions to be thrown instead" — so during `compute()` the malformed cell is merely *present*, and a count that tests presence never reads it and never trips the throw. The discriminating case is in the same model and the same compute run: a **value-reading twin** over that same cell (`EchoQty = AlphaQty`) parts company with the count, yielding no value while the count yields `VALUE|2`. A rival account in which the count resolved the operand before the poison mechanism observed it predicts nothing about that twin; presence-versus-read predicts exactly the split measured.

**Left uncalibrated deliberately:** the reading twin reports **`CLEARED`**, not the `ERRORED` §11's general prose predicts for a read of a formally invalid cell. That is an observation, not a mechanism — this probe was not built to separate a clearing read from an aborting one, and no claim here rests on which it is. It is recorded so the next reader does not mistake the general rule for a measured one at this shape.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.FixedGroupCountComputeDiffTest` — the three-row channel matrix over both kernel strategies plus the formal-invalidity control.)*

## §9 supplement: runtime V1-to-V2 conversion pads sparse repetition predecessors before evaluation (kernel source + tri-engine differential, 2026-08-09) <a id="kf178"></a>

> Fold: folded → §9.

The mutable V1 document does **not** itself materialize a sparse address: `DocumentImpl.addEntityInstance` stores only the exact entity instance. Every runtime entry point converts that V1 input through `DocumentV1V2ConverterImpl.ofDocumentV1`, whose batch inserts reach `ImmutableRepetitionsImpl.Builder.insertInPlace`; inserting repetition `n` appends `GroupInstanceV2.empty()` until positions `1..n-1` exist. Missing ancestors are likewise created along the inserted pointer. At nested levels the padding belongs only to the concrete parent being traversed, so addressing `Outer[3]/Inner[4]` creates `Outer[1..3]` and `Inner[1..4]` below `Outer[3]`, not child rows below the synthetic predecessor parents.

The runtime discriminator separates conversion from raw-builder behavior. On a model with a per-row `Qty < 5` rule, an explicit `Lines[5]` V1 group makes dynamic Groovy and generated Java fire OMISSION on rows 1 through 5; placing only `Lines[5]/Qty = 7` fires on rows 1 through 4. The addressed row is content in the first case and non-firing in the second, while every padded predecessor is an instantiated empty repeatable row. The interpreter formerly kept only the addressed row and therefore missed those outputs.

Canonical Document JSON is not a competing sparse representation. An array of length five contains five row positions; `{}` is an explicit empty row, and both readers already instantiate it. The corrected interpreter placement seam now builds the same V2-effective group tree for group and field inputs without changing JSON encoding or ingress.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.RepeatableRowContentDiffTest.sparseGroupAndFieldPlacementsMaterializeV2PredecessorRows` across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter; `interpreter.eval.DocumentPlacementsSnapshotTest` on JVM and Node for nested parent scoping and exact linear materialization work. Interpreter treatment: [IF276](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if276).)*

## §9 supplement: nested validation iteration has implicit child row 1, distinct from stored V2 structure (tri-engine differential, 2026-08-09) <a id="kf179"></a>

> Fold: folded → §9.

Validation owns a row domain that is not identical to the immutable document tree. Under every existing repeatable parent iteration row, a nested repeatable validation scope with no concrete child contributes one implicit child at coordinate 1. The rule composes recursively: placing only `/Root/Outer[3]/Middle[2]/Inner[4]` makes `Qty < 5` fire OMISSION at `Outer[1]/Middle[1]/Inner[1]`, `Outer[2]/Middle[1]/Inner[1]`, `Outer[3]/Middle[1]/Inner[1]`, and the concrete `Outer[3]/Middle[2]/Inner[1..4]`. An explicit `Middle[1]` with no `Inner` likewise yields `Inner[1]`. With no outer repetition at all, no nested row is invented.

The implicit row is an evaluation context, not stored content. `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)` and starred group operands see only concrete groups; mandatory and `uniqueIndex` auto-checks see only stored fields; parallel index identity does not gain an empty compromising key; and computation creates outcomes only for concrete target rows. `RepetitionNotUnique` is the deliberate exception among identity-like consumers because it is itself a validation relation: its optional-empty key tuple includes implicit rows, so two missing child branches beneath equal filled ancestor keys produce two OMISSION duplicate messages.

The previous §9 nested-iteration claim was based on positive-guard silence and said that a parent with no child contributed no evaluation. That oracle could not separate no context from an empty context whose guard was false. The empty-as-zero rule is the discriminator that rules out the old reading; the stored-consumer matrix rules out the rival mechanism that materializes a real child row.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.RepeatableRowContentDiffTest.missingNestedBranchesContributeOneImplicitChildRowPerRepeatableLevel`, `.implicitRowsAreValidationAndRnuContextsButNotStoredGroupOrAutoCheckRows`, and `.computationsRemainBoundedToConcreteNestedRows`, all dynamic Groovy + generated Java + interpreter; multiplatform `ReferenceDrivenIterationScopeTest` for the host iteration projection. Interpreter treatment: [IF277](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if277).)*


## §8 supplement — a category projection is admitted in the VALUE-EXPRESSION positions too, not only as a quantifier member (kernel probe, 2026-08-10) <a id="kf180"></a>

> Fold: not-foldable — a static admission fact about where `[field -> Cat]` may appear, not an evaluation semantic.

The category projection's admission surface was only ever measured for the value-list *quantifier members* (SPEC-2026-07-22-05). A cold `RuleValidator.check` matrix over a model whose `RequestedTier`/`FallbackTier` carry a `ServiceTier` category over `SelectedTier`'s own value domain settles the value-expression side:

| shape | verdict |
|---|---|
| `[SelectedTier] == [RequestedTier -> ServiceTier]` (projection as the comparison's RIGHT operand) | **accepted** |
| `[RequestedTier -> ServiceTier] == [SelectedTier]` (projection as the LEFT operand, bare field right) | **accepted** |
| `FieldValueIncludedInValueList(SelectedTier, [RequestedTier -> ServiceTier])` (projection as a value-list ENTRY) | **accepted** |
| a computation `SelectedTier = [RequestedTier -> ServiceTier]` | **accepted** (the field report's original observation) |

So the class of positions an `IEntityId` can carry `getCategoryName()` through is wider than the quantifier members, which is what makes a per-site conversion fix insufficient and the `verifyCategoryPreserved` backstop necessary (API-FINDINGS finding 119). The one shape the matrix could NOT decide is category-vs-category on this fixture: it fails on `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED` because neither operand is the rule's error field — a fixture artifact, not a statement about the shape, and it is left explicitly undecided rather than reported as a rejection.

*(Locked: `adapter … EnumCategoryValueExpressionReadTest` — the computation and value-list-entry rows round-trip; the comparison-right-operand row is the backstop's does-fire case, since that operand is not category-threaded and now degrades to `Opaque` instead of converting to a different condition.)*


## §11 supplement — the implicit target-scale comparison is decided BEFORE the target-self-reference refusal (kernel `checkConsistency`, 2026-08-11) <a id="kf181"></a>

> Fold: pending — the operand-kind and target-scale axes added 2026-08-11 are not in §11's prose yet; the ordering claim itself remains folded there.

Canonical in §11 "Computations". The reconciliation requests are the lean project's `SPEC-2026-08-10-02`, plus `SPEC-2026-08-11-01` (the differing-scale field operand) and `SPEC-2026-08-11-02` (the denominator this entry originally over-claimed); every verdict below was reproduced here rather than accepted as reported.

Two rejections can apply to the same computation — the scale gate [KF174](#kf174) describes, and the ban on reading the computed field inside its own operation. The kernel reports exactly one of them, and it is the scale gate. Over a scale-0 target, changing only the operand that decides the derived scale flips the reported class:

| operation into the scale-0 target | reported code list |
|---|---|
| `[Total] * 2`, `[Total] + [Units]` (derived scale 0 — matched) | `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD` |
| `[Total] * 1.5`, `[Total] + 1.5`, `1.5 * [Total]`, `[Total] - 1.5` (derived scale 1) | `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` |
| `[Total] + [Rate]`, `[Total] * [Rate]`, `[Rate] - [Total]` (a scale-2 FIELD operand — derived scale 2) | `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` |
| the same mismatched operations under `errorCodesToSuppress: ["MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES"]` | `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD` |

Each row's list is complete and a singleton (the locks assert the diagnostic *count* beside the codes, so a kernel notification carrying no `MVK_` token could not hide in an "empty" row), so this is an order and not a co-report. **The suppression row is what makes it an order rather than a coincidence:** the kernel's own escape hatch cannot legalize a computation that still reads its target, so what surfaces underneath the suppressed gate is the refusal the gate had been reported ahead of. **What "independent" is measured over, exactly** (narrowed then re-measured on the lean project's `SPEC-2026-08-11-02`, which caught this entry claiming a cross-product it had sampled): the complete grid of **2 target scales × 3 operators × 2 operand positions × 4 operand cells** — a matched and a mismatched *literal*, and a matched and a mismatched *field* — is measured, 48 rows, with a suppression row under each of the 16 mismatched ones. It is the **derived** scale that decides the reported class, not the operand's kind, not which side it sits on, and not the operand's own scale. **The discriminating cell is `[TotalScale2] * [Rate]`:** the operand's own scale (2) equals the target's exactly, so a rival "the operand must match the target" account predicts the self-reference, while the §5 derivation (`*` sums operand scales → 4) predicts the gate. The kernel reports the **gate**, and `[TotalScale2] + [Rate]` — same operands, `max` instead of sum, derived 2 — reports the self-reference. A scale-0 target cannot separate those accounts at all (`0 + s == max(0, s)` for every operand scale here), which is why the second target scale is in the grid rather than a convenience. *(Corrected 2026-08-11 after review: this paragraph first asserted the derived-scale mechanism from the scale-0 grid alone, where it was an inference the rival account fit equally well.)* The order is also independent of which slot carries the self-read — a self-reference in a *precondition* also yields to a scale mismatch in the operation, and with the scale matched that same precondition self-reference is what surfaces. It also holds over the projected self-read class of [KF182](#kf182): `[Legs/Distance For "L-1"] * 1.5` into `Legs/Distance` reports the scale gate, while the same read with a matched factor reports the projected class.

Controls, because a same-code pair proves nothing without them: a scale mismatch with **no** self-reference reports the same gate, so the gate is not self-reference-specific; and the scale-matched operation over a different field is accepted with an empty diagnostic list, so the model itself is not the rejection.

The practical consequence for an authoring surface is that a scale-mismatched self-referencing computation is a **two-repair** rejection reported one repair at a time: fixing the scale exposes the self-reference. Nothing generalizes to *why* the kernel orders them this way — that is unmeasured, and this entry claims only the reported class per shape.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.ComputedTargetScaleLawsTest` — `theOrderHoldsAcrossEveryTargetScaleOperatorPositionAndOperandKind` enumerates all 48 target × operator × position × operand cells plus the 16 suppression rows under `assertAll`, so a shifted order reports every moved cell at once; each expectation is derived from the §5 scale rule before the kernel is asked. The precondition-slot pair and the unrelated-mismatch and accepted controls sit beside it. Every row asserts the complete emitted code list, with the diagnostic count asserted against it so a notification carrying no `MVK_` token cannot hide in an "empty" row. **The encoded predicate is exact equality, which is narrower than the kernel's admission rule** — a *smaller* derived scale is admitted while the operation stays constant-capable (the first law in this file). Every operand in the grid has a non-negative scale and every target scale is at most the largest of them, so no cell can derive a smaller scale than its target and the capability clause is unreachable by construction; an operand like the integer literal `100`, signed scale −2, would reach it.)*


## §11 supplement — a PROJECTED read of the computed field is its own rejection class, and it pre-empts the plain one (kernel `checkConsistency`, 2026-08-11) <a id="kf182"></a>

> Fold: folded → §11.

Canonical in §11 "Computations". The reconciliation request is the lean project's `SPEC-2026-08-10-03`; the category half is theirs, the semantic-index half was measured here.

A computation that reads its own computed field **through a projection** — an enum category `[Selected -> ServiceTier]` or a semantic index `[Legs/Distance For "L-1"]` — is rejected as `MVK_ERROR_SEMANTIC_INDEX_OR_CATEGORY_FOR_ERRORFIELD`, not as the plain `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`. Every list below is a complete singleton, on one model whose three enum fields carry the same `ServiceTier` classification over one value domain:

| shape, computing `SelectedTier` (or `Legs/Distance`) | reported code list |
|---|---|
| `[SelectedTier -> ServiceTier]`, either target position in `FirstFilledValue(… -> ServiceTier, … -> ServiceTier)` | `MVK_ERROR_SEMANTIC_INDEX_OR_CATEGORY_FOR_ERRORFIELD` |
| `[Legs/Distance For "L-1"]`, and the same read nested in `… * 2` | `MVK_ERROR_SEMANTIC_INDEX_OR_CATEGORY_FOR_ERRORFIELD` |
| the computed field as the semantic-index **KEY** rather than the indexed field — `[Legs/LegRef For /Routing/PlannedLeg]` computing `PlannedLeg`, and its `FirstFilledValue` form | `MVK_ERROR_SEMANTIC_INDEX_OR_CATEGORY_FOR_ERRORFIELD` |
| a projected self-read **beside a plain one** (`FirstFilledValue(SelectedTier, SelectedTier -> ServiceTier)`, both orders) | `MVK_ERROR_SEMANTIC_INDEX_OR_CATEGORY_FOR_ERRORFIELD` |
| a projected self-read beside a plain read of a **different** field (`FirstFilledValue(RequestedTier, SelectedTier -> ServiceTier)`, both orders — added 2026-08-11, see below) | `MVK_ERROR_SEMANTIC_INDEX_OR_CATEGORY_FOR_ERRORFIELD` |
| a **plain** self-read in the operation beside a **projected** one in either precondition slot | `MVK_ERROR_SEMANTIC_INDEX_OR_CATEGORY_FOR_ERRORFIELD` |
| the same two shapes **without** the projection (`[SelectedTier]`, `FirstFilledValue(SelectedTier, RequestedTier)`) | `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD` |
| a projection of any **other** field of the same category, alone or as a two-entry list | **accepted** |
| the identical indexed read into a **different** target (`PlannedDistance`), or keyed by a **different** field (`PreferredLeg`) | **accepted** |

Both precondition slots — common and per-alternative — split the same way: a projected self-read reports the projected class, a plain one the plain class. So the discriminator is the *reading mode*, and the projected class **pre-empts** the plain one wherever both readings occur: in one operation, in either operand order, **and across slots**. That last row is the one with client consequences, because it means the classes cannot be decided slot by slot.

**The "different field" row was added a day later, and how it arrived is the point.** The lean project's consumer generalized the two-entry pairing from "a projected self-read beside another self-read" (what the original rows measured) to "beside any plain entry", reasoning that an other-field read is inert because the class only inspects reads of the computed field — and it flagged that step as reasoning rather than observation. The composition was right, and the run that settled it took seconds: an other-field *projection* pair is accepted outright (the control), so the entry's inertness is now visible in the rows instead of argued from them. Treat this as the standing pattern for a consumer's "we widened it on this reasoning" note: measure the widened shape, then the docstring can cite the row.

Two boundaries this does **not** establish, stated so neither is read as measured. The **key** row above extends the code past "the read of the computed field" to "the computed field named anywhere in a semantic-index expression", but only for the key position measured. And a projection living **only inside a `Having` filter** is unmeasured: both authoring forms attempted are refused earlier for wildcard iteration (`MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD`, then `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` — the `$` correlation has no enclosing iteration in a computation over a non-repeatable target), including the control with no self-read at all, so nothing about the filter case was observed either way.

**The MVK ledger row for this code was wrong, and the reason it was wrong generalizes.** It read *erased — the computed-field slot is a plain field handle/path, so semantic-index or category decoration on it is unrepresentable*. That is true of the slot and irrelevant to the code: the code fires on the **read** of the computed field inside an operation or precondition, which is ordinary authorable DSL. An unrepresentability argument has to name the shape the kernel actually checks; this one named a neighbour.

Client-side consequence, now fixed: `Computation.build()` refused all of these shapes — correctly, the kernel rejects every one — but named `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD` in the message, which `PrecheckException` lifts into `Diagnostic.code()`. An author was therefore told to drop the reference when the kernel's own repair is to drop the projection. `ConditionReferences.readMode(...)` now answers *how* a path is read over one traversal, and the builder **folds that mode across every slot** before it reports one — which is what the cross-slot row above requires, and what a per-slot check got wrong by reporting whichever slot it visited first.
