*(Locked: `validate.laws.ComputedTargetProjectedReadLawsTest` — every row above, each asserting the complete emitted code list; `dsl.ComputationAuthoringTest` for the client-side class and its plain control.)*

## §12 supplement — an UNSTARRED group operand expands recursively into a message's `referenced` set, with concrete coordinates (kernel runtime, 2026-08-11) <a id="kf183"></a>

> Fold: pending — newly measured message-channel fact; folds into §12 on the next canonical KERNEL-SEMANTICS pass.

Canonical in §12 "The validation model". The experiment request is the lean project's `EXP-2026-08-11-01`, which asked it because `adapter.laws.GroupStarValueAggregateDiffTest` settles the question only for a **starred** group (the operand-expansion mechanism itself is [KF12](#kf12)'s `ExpandService`) — and the star is what turns an operand into a field set in the first place, so a fixed group could have been an entity reference contributing no field pointer at all. Three refusals in that project's structural reference projection shared this one witness.

Measured on one nonrepeatable model (`/Order/Billing` holding `Street` and `City` plus a deeper nonrepeatable `/Order/Billing/Contact` holding `Email`, a disjoint `/Order/Shipping` holding `Carrier`), reading each fired message's complete `referenced` set:

| condition operand position | `referenced` |
|---|---|
| `GroupFilled(/Order/Billing)` — single-operand presence | `Billing[1]/City`, `Billing[1]/Contact[1]/Email`, `Billing[1]/Street` |
| `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(/Order/Billing, /Order/Shipping)` — entity list | the same three **plus** `Shipping[1]/Carrier` |
| `NumberOfFilledGroups(/Order/Billing, /Order/Shipping) > 0` — numeric count atom | identical to the entity-list row |

So the answer is **recursive expansion**, and it is the *same* relation in all three positions. Of the four accounts on the table, three are refuted: **direct-child expansion** would have dropped the deeper `Contact[1]/Email`; **no contribution** is refuted because `City` and `Contact/Email` are neither the error field nor named anywhere in the condition, so they can only come from the operand; and a **group pointer** never appears, matching the starred result.

**The coordinates differ from the starred case, which is the second half of the finding.** A starred group's expanded field carries the wildcard repetition — `/Order[1]/Items[0]/Quantity` — while an unstarred group's expanded fields are **concrete at the firing row**, `Billing[1]` and `Contact[1]` included. Both readings come from the same run through the same code path.

Controls, because a set that merely looks plausible proves nothing: the starred expansion is re-read on this same model and still wildcards (so a null result here could not have been an instrument artifact), and a plain-field condition contributes exactly its two operands. One authoring constraint shaped both controls: the kernel refuses a rule whose error field the condition never references (`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`), so the request's "references only a plain field" control is not authorable — each control names the error field as one of its operands. The same constraint is why the three measured rules can carry an error field *inside* the tested group at all: the group operand satisfies the reference gate indirectly, for `AtLeastOneGroupFilled` and `NumberOfFilledGroups` exactly as for `GroupFilled`.

Nothing here generalizes to `fillToFix`, to filtered stars, or to `RepetitionNotUnique`'s duplicate-cluster projection; those channels were not read.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.UnstarredGroupReferenceDiffTest` — the three operand positions and both controls, tri-checked dynamic Groovy == generated Java == interpreter on complete sorted sets, plus the starred-versus-unstarred coordinate pair. The interpreter already agreed on every row; no production change was needed.)*

## §12 supplement — a fired OMISSION's `refOmissionErrorResponsible` set is ALL of the rule's operands, the already-filled ones included (kernel runtime, 2026-08-11) <a id="kf184"></a>

> Fold: pending — newly measured message-channel fact; folds into §12 on the next canonical KERNEL-SEMANTICS pass.

Canonical in §12 "The validation model", and the channel [KF183](#kf183) explicitly did not read. `IMessage.getRefOmissionErrorResponsiblePointers` — the `fillToFix` channel dmtool publishes — reads naturally as "the fields you still have to fill", which would make it the *empty* subset of the operands. It is not.

Measured on `Quantity > 5 And BackorderQuantity >= 0`, error field `../Quantity`, over a document with `Quantity` filled at 50 and `BackorderQuantity` **present but empty** (so the value branch holds on a filled operand and the omission branch holds on an empty one, which is the shape that types the firing OMISSION):

| channel | value |
|---|---|
| `type` | `OMISSION_ERROR` |
| `fillToFix` | `Order/BackorderQuantity`, `Order/Quantity` — **both** operands |

So the set names every field whose *change* could clear the message, not the subset that is currently unfilled: `Quantity` is filled and still appears. The rival reading — "only the empty operands" — is refuted by that single row, since dropping the filled `Quantity` is exactly what it predicts. The VALUE control on the same document (`Quantity < 100`, firing on the same filled cell) reports an **empty** `fillToFix` with a non-empty `referenced`, so the channel is coupled to polarity rather than being a copy of the operand set that happens to coincide here.

Both kernel strategies agree (dynamic Groovy == generated Java). Nothing here settles the `Or` shape, a filtered star, or `RepetitionNotUnique`'s duplicate cluster; those were not read.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.KernelRuntimeProbeTest.anOmissionCarriesFillToFixWhereAValueErrorCarriesNone` — the OMISSION content, the VALUE control, and the two-strategy agreement, observed through the kernel-runtime probe. `adapter.laws.RichMessageDiffTest` separately locks that the interpreter reproduces this set; it compares the two engines and never pinned the content, which is why the fact was unrecorded until now.)*

## §1 supplement — the group-list ROOT classes are real, and the error field's LOCUS selects the class for an unstarred repeatable operand (kernel `checkConsistency`, 2026-08-11) <a id="kf185"></a>

> Fold: pending — newly measured admission-diagnostic identities; folds into §1 on the next canonical KERNEL-SEMANTICS pass.

Canonical in §1 "Group-list quantifiers", which states the admission *rules* but assigns them no diagnostic identity. Reconciling a12-kernel-lean `SPEC-2026-08-11-03` measured the identities, and two of that entry's generalizations do not survive a fixture that can separate their variables. The whole table is `validate.laws.GroupListDiagnosticIdentityLawsTest`, asserting the **complete** emitted code list per row.

**A one-root model cannot see the root classes, because every co-operand of the root is also its descendant and the overlap gate runs first.** With a *second, disjoint* root the root classes appear:

| shape (rule group `/Shipment`, error field outside every repeatable group) | `AllGroupsFilled` / `NotAllGroupsFilled` | `AtLeastOneGroupFilled` / `NumberOfFilledGroups` |
|---|---|---|
| root beside a **disjoint** operand | `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED` | `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_WITH_OTHER_PARAMETERS` |
| root beside its **own descendant** | `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` | `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` |
| **sole** root | `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED` | admitted |

The sole-root row is the second, independent refutation of "the root rule is a consequence of the ancestor relation plus arity": there is no co-operand at all, so no ancestor relation exists, and the two families still split.

**The error field's locus selects the class for an unstarred repeatable operand** — same operand shape in every row below, only the error field moves:

| shape | error field OUTSIDE the repeatable group | error field INSIDE it (or that group is the rule group) |
|---|---|---|
| `NumberOfFilledGroups(Parcels)` | `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` | `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDGN` |
| `NumberOfFilledGroups(Parcels, Destination)` | `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` | `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` |
| `AllGroupsFilled(Parcels, Destination)` | `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` | **admitted** |
| `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(Parcels)` | `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` | **admitted** |
| `GroupFilled(Parcels)` | `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` | **admitted** |

So neither code is "the" class for that shape, and the count carrier's arity class is reachable only from the inside anchoring. A claim that names the shape without the locus is half-measured whichever code it names — including the mirror-image claim that the count draws `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDGN` for *any* single unstarred operand: true for a non-repeatable operand in both the relative and the absolute spelling, false for a repeatable one seen from outside.

Unchanged and confirmed across all four carriers, so these three stay separable from everything above: an exact duplicate is `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1` and an ancestor/descendant pair `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` (also at list length three); the three wildcard classes are `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED` for a starred operand where stars are barred, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` for scalar `GroupFilled(G*)`, and `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` for the unstarred repeatable operand above; an unknown path is `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`; and the iteration guard is its own gate — an unguarded sole `NoGroupFilled` on the iterated group draws `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` where the `GroupFilled`-guarded form is admitted.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.GroupListDiagnosticIdentityLawsTest` — six tables, 45 rows. The pre-existing single-gate owners are unchanged: `GroupQuantifierLawsTest` for the root bars, `GroupListEntityOperandLawsTest` for duplicate/arity/wildcard on the entity list, `RootGroupCountLawsTest` for the count carrier's computation-side admission.)*

## §9 supplement — `RepetitionNotUnique` key-path admission mostly REUSES the group-list classes, and one class collapses THREE structurally different shapes (kernel `checkConsistency`, 2026-08-11) <a id="kf186"></a>

> Fold: pending — newly measured admission-diagnostic identities; folds into §9 on the next canonical KERNEL-SEMANTICS pass.

Canonical in §9 "RepetitionNotUnique". Reconciling a12-kernel-lean `SPEC-2026-08-11-04`. The operator adds almost no vocabulary of its own — a key-path defect is reported with the class the group-list families already use, so an authoring surface must not invent a key-specific unknown-entity or duplicate class:

| key-path shape (rule group `/Basket` above the repeatable `Lines`) | class |
|---|---|
| a repeated key | `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1` |
| an unresolvable key path | `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` |
| a starred key path | `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` |
| an error field that leaves a repetition level unpinned | `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` |
| keys naming a sibling rather than the error field | `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED` |

**`MVK_REPEATABLE_GROUP_MISSING` is the one class of its own, and THREE structurally different shapes land in it** — a second key in a non-repeatable group, a sole key whose group is not repeatable at all, and the rule group sitting **at** the repeated group rather than above it. The third is measured in both the absolute (`RepetitionNotUnique(/Basket/Lines/Sku)`) and the relative (`RepetitionNotUnique(Sku)`) key spelling, and agrees with the pre-existing `IterationLawsTest` lock; a claim that this shape draws `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` is refuted by both spellings. Splitting the class in an authoring surface would report a distinction the engine does not make.

A key in a **parallel repeatable** group is *not* part of that collapse and is not one class either: on an index-free fixture it draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` both from the parent and from inside one of the two groups, while `MVK_NOT_IN_SAME_PATH` is what fires on the index-keyed logistics fixture (`IterationLawsTest.repetitionNotUniqueKeysMustLieOnOnePath`). What makes the same-path gate reachable there is **not** established — the obvious candidate, the semantic index, was not isolated, and an attempt to isolate it drew `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_INVALID_INDEX` on the error field instead.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueKeyPathLawsTest` — one table, 11 rows, complete code list per row. The kind axis stays in `RepetitionNotUniqueKeyKindLawsTest`, which gains the **Number** key it had never covered: the "no Number/String-only restriction" claim had no Number row in its own denominator.)*

## §9 supplement — a GROUP-scope operand's admission is per-CARRIER; two families split internally, and ACCEPT means the kernel EXPANDED (kernel `rule check`, 2026-08-14) <a id="kf187"></a>

> Fold: pending — §9 carries no per-carrier operand-admission table yet, and this one is not stable enough to fold: two families split INTERNALLY (`Min`/`Max` versus `MinValue`/`MaxValue`, and the singular versus plural date-range forms), so the canonical prose would have to state the split rather than a family rule, and DG35 will change which of these shapes the typed surface can even express.

A group operand is not a shape the kernel admits or refuses globally. Swept across every carrier a group-expansion audit had named, the verdicts partition into three sets, and **two carriers disagree with their own family member** — which is why "the family admits it" was never a safe inference from one measured sibling.

| carrier | non-repeatable group (unstarred) | repeatable group (starred) |
|---|---|---|
| the 7 field-fill quantifiers (`AllFieldsFilled`, `NoFieldFilled`, `AtLeastOneFieldFilled`, `NotAllFieldsFilled`, `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled`, `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled`, `MoreThanOneFieldFilled`) | ACCEPT | ACCEPT |
| the 3 plural value-list quantifiers (`AtLeastOneFieldValueIncludedInValueList`, `NoFieldValueIncludedInValueList`, `NotAllFieldValuesIncludedInValueList`) | ACCEPT | ACCEPT |
| `FirstFilledValue` | ACCEPT ([KF173](#kf173) records the routing) | ACCEPT |
| `NumberOfFilledFields` | ACCEPT | ACCEPT |
| `MinValue` over a **DATE** group | ACCEPT | ACCEPT |
| `MaxValue` over a **DATE** group | ACCEPT | ACCEPT |
| **`Min`** (the operand-LIST form) | `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` | `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` |
| **`Max`** (the operand-LIST form) | `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` | `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` |
| **`DateRangesOverlap`** | `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` | `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` |
| **`AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps`**, group in the `In` slot | — | ACCEPT |

**The two internal splits are the finding.** `Min`/`Max` refuse a group operand while their `MinValue`/`MaxValue` siblings accept one, so the min/max *name* does not predict the gate — the operand-list form and the entity-list aggregate are different carriers that happen to share a concept. And `DateRangesOverlap` refuses a group outright while the plural `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` accepts one in its `In` slot, so within one predicate family the singular and plural forms disagree. An implementation that generalizes either verdict across its family gets the other half wrong.

**ACCEPT means the kernel EXPANDED the group and type-checked the result — measured, because the alternative reading would have been just as consistent with every row above.** A homogeneous group cannot distinguish "expanded and checked" from "waved past the type gates on the way through a group". A DATE group against a STRING value list can, and it is refused `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED` while the same carrier over a String group is admitted. So a runtime implementation may rely on the expansion, which is what the whole sweep exists to license.

**ARITY, however, does NOT count the expansion.** `MoreThanOneFieldFilled(<single-field group>)` is ACCEPTED while `MoreThanOneFieldFilled(<that same field>)` draws `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`. So a group slot satisfies the param law unconditionally, exactly as a starred slot does — the same split [KF173](#kf173) records for `FieldValuesNotUnique`, where arity reads the authored slots and the category gate reads the expansion. Neither row shows it alone.

**`Min`/`Max` refuse a group operand AND any wildcard operand, by two separable gates.** `Min([Payouts*/Gross], 5)` — a starred FIELD — draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`, a *different* code from the group rows. That matters because `CheckFeldWertOpImpl.check` runs `checkIsField` before `checkNoWildCards`, so the starred-*group* row would report the group code under either gate and cannot on its own establish which fired; the differing code on the starred field is what separates them. Recorded because the first version of this entry rested the starred-group cell on a row that had no admissible counterfactual.

**The `$` OUTER marker is a UNIVERSAL filter-only gate and says nothing about any carrier.** `[$/Claim/ExcessDue] > 0` — a bare comparison, no aggregate anywhere — draws `MVK_INVALID_OUTER_ITERATION`, exactly as `Sum($…, …)` does, while the unmarked list is admitted. `CheckEntitySpecImpl.checkOuter` is the single locus and its predicate (`ctx.outer != null && !isInFilterCondition()`) names no carrier. This entry first read the aggregate rows as a statement about aggregate operands; they are not, and the correction matters because that misreading had been used to close a defect. The reachable spelling is `$` **inside** a filter, which this table does not cover.

**Three rows measure a SPELLING, not a gate, and are excluded on that basis** — each was caught by its own field control failing alongside it. `Min(Assessment, 5)` draws `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` because `Min`'s operands are value expressions and an entity must ride in brackets; `MinValue(<dates>) > 0` draws `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` from the comparison rather than the operand; and a `Sum` inside an overlap predicate's `Having` draws `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` in its marked *and* unmarked form.

**What the refusals do and do not license.** Each refusal makes a corresponding interpreter path unreachable *through the kernel's own validator* — the missing group branch in `minMaxOperandVals`, and the deliberately unexpanded group case in the `filteredStarCells` path `DateRangesOverlap` shares. It does **not** make them unreachable: `Expr` is public API, our parser accepts a bare group path as a function argument, and `dmtool rule eval --condition` evaluates an arbitrary condition with no MVK pre-flight at all (the native binary carries no kernel). So "the kernel refuses it" bounds what a *stored, validated* model can contain, and nothing more.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.GroupOperandCarrierAdmissionLawsTest` — one asserted table whose verdict rides in each row rather than in a lookup with a `default -> ACCEPT` arm, so a mistyped label can no longer silently downgrade a refusal expectation. The fixture carries a homogeneous group per field kind in both repetition shapes, since a mixed group's expansion offends the kind gate first and its rejection then cannot distinguish "this carrier refuses a group" from "this carrier refuses these kinds". Every ACCEPT family carries an explicit-field control; the `Min`/`Max` and `DateRangesOverlap` refusals carry an admitted field control each, and the starred-group cells additionally carry the starred-field row that separates the wildcard gate from the group gate.)*
